\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{M}-|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}e^{\theta f}+C|\mathcal{T}|^{2}e^{\theta f}+C|\nabla u|^{4}+C\left(|\nabla f|^{4}+|\Delta f|^{2}+|\mathrm{R}|^{2}+1\right)e^{2\theta f}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t},

where we used the fact that
\left\lvert\square(e^{\theta f})\right\rvert\leq C\left(|\Delta f|+|\nabla f|^{2}+|\mathrm{R}|+1\right)e^{\theta f}
. Using Proposition
2.7
and (
3.6
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{-10}^{-1/10}\int_{M}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}e^{\theta f}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y)+\int_{M}|\nabla u|^{2}e^{\theta f}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-10}\leq C(n,Y),

(3.7)

where for the last inequality we used (
3.5
).

Combining (
3.7
) with Proposition
2.7
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{-10}^{-1/10}\int_{M}|\nabla^{2}u_{0}|^{2}e^{\theta f}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y),

which completes the proof.
∎

3.2
Almost self-similar points

We begin by defining the following concept of almost selfsimilar points, which is similar to
[
Bam20b
, Definition 5.1]
.

Definition 3.2

(
(\delta,r)
-selfsimilar)
.

A point
x^{*}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{I}}
is called
(\delta,r)
-selfsimilar
if
\mathfrak{t}(x^{*})-\delta^{-1}r^{2}\in\mathbb{I}^{-}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{x^{*}}(\delta r^{2})-\mathcal{W}_{x^{*}}(\delta^{-1}r^{2})\leq\delta.

Here, we implicitly assume
\delta\ll 1
.

Lemma 3.3
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, if
x^{*}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{I}}
is
(\delta,r)
-selfsimilar and
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y,\epsilon)
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{x^{*}}(\epsilon r^{2})-\mathcal{N}_{x^{*}}(\epsilon^{-1}r^{2})\leq\epsilon.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\mathfrak{t}(x^{*})=0
and
r=1
.

Suppose the statement is false. Then there exist a constant
\epsilon>0
and a sequence
\mathcal{X}^{i}\in\mathcal{M}(n,Y,T_{i})
with
(i^{-2},1)
-selfsimilar points
x_{i}^{*}\in\mathcal{X}^{i}_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{x_{i}^{*}}(\epsilon)-\mathcal{N}_{x_{i}^{*}}(\epsilon^{-1})>\epsilon.

(3.8)

Passing to a subsequence if necessary, it follows from Theorems
2.11
and
2.12
that

\displaystyle(M_{i}\times\mathbb{I},d^{*}_{i},x_{i}^{*},\mathfrak{t}_{i})\xrightarrow[i\to\infty]{\hskip 8.19447pt\hat{C}^{\infty}\hskip 8.19447pt}(Z,d_{Z},z,\mathfrak{t}),

(3.9)

where
(Z,d_{Z},z,\mathfrak{t})
is a noncollapsed Ricci flow limit space over
(-\infty,0]
, whose regular part is a Ricci flow spacetime
(\mathcal{R},\mathfrak{t},\partial_{\mathfrak{t}},g^{Z})
.

By Proposition
2.3
(iii) and the smooth convergence in Theorem
2.12
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ric}(g^{Z})+\nabla^{2}f_{z}=\frac{g^{Z}}{2|\mathfrak{t}|}

on
\iota_{z}(\mathcal{R}^{z}_{(-\infty,0)})
, where
\mathcal{R}^{z}
is the regular part of a metric flow
\mathcal{X}^{z}
associated with
z
, and
\iota_{z}
is the embedding in
[
FL25a
, Theorem 1.4]
.

It follows from the proof of
[
Bam20b
, Theorem 15.69]
that on
\iota_{z}(\mathcal{R}^{z}_{(-\infty,0)})
, the heat kernel
K_{Z}
satisfies

\displaystyle X_{x}K_{Z}(x;y)+X_{y}K_{Z}(x;y)=\frac{n}{2}K_{Z}(x,y)

for any
x,y\in\iota_{z}(\mathcal{R}^{z}_{(-\infty,0)})
, where
X:=|\mathfrak{t}|(\partial_{\mathfrak{t}}-\nabla f_{z})
.

As a result, applying the same argument as in
[
Bam20b
, Theorem 15.69]
(see also
[
FL25a
, Lemma 7.28]
), we obtain a one-parameter family of diffeomorphisms
\boldsymbol{\psi}^{s}
on
\iota_{z}(\mathcal{R}^{z})
generated by the vector field
X
, satisfying

\displaystyle d_{g^{Z}_{e^{-s}t}}(\boldsymbol{\psi}^{s}(x),\boldsymbol{\psi}^{s}(y))=e^{-\frac{s}{2}}d_{g^{Z}_{t}}(x,y)

for any
x,y\in\iota_{z}(\mathcal{R}^{z}_{t})
and any
s\in\mathbb{R}
. In other words,
\boldsymbol{\psi}^{s}
corresponds to the rescaling by
e^{-\frac{s}{2}}
. Since
f_{z}
is constant along the flow lines of
\boldsymbol{\psi}^{s}
, it is clear that the Nash entropy
\mathcal{N}_{z}(\tau)
remains constant for
\tau>0
. Thus,
(Z,d_{Z},z,\mathfrak{t})
is a Ricci shrinker space.

Since the Nash entropy is continuous under the convergence in (
3.9
) (see
[
FL25a
, Lemma 7.2]
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{N}_{x_{i}^{*}}(\epsilon)-\mathcal{N}_{x_{i}^{*}}(\epsilon^{-1})\right\rvert\to 0,

which contradicts (
3.8
). This completes the proof.
∎

Conversely, we have

Lemma 3.4
.

For any
\epsilon>0
and
\delta\in(0,\epsilon^{4}/2)
, if
x^{*}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{I}}
satisfies
\mathfrak{t}(x^{*})-\delta^{-1}r^{2}\in\mathbb{I}^{-}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{x^{*}}(\delta r^{2})-\mathcal{N}_{x^{*}}(\delta^{-1}r^{2})\leq\delta,

(3.10)

then
x^{*}
is
(\epsilon,r)
-selfsimilar.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\mathfrak{t}(x^{*})=0
and
r=1
.

Since
s\mathcal{N}_{x^{*}}^{\prime}(s)=\mathcal{W}_{x^{*}}(s)-\mathcal{N}_{x^{*}}(s)\leq 0
, it follows from (
3.10
) and the mean value theorem that there exists a time
s_{0}\in[\epsilon^{-1},\epsilon^{-1}+1]
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{x^{*}}(s_{0})-\mathcal{W}_{x^{*}}(s_{0})\leq(\epsilon^{-1}+1)\delta.

In other words, by Proposition
2.3
(ii), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s_{0}}\mathcal{W}_{x^{*}}(s)-\mathcal{W}_{x^{*}}(s_{0})\,\mathrm{d}s\leq(\epsilon^{-1}+1)^{2}\delta\leq 2\epsilon^{-2}\delta.

This inequality is equivalent to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s_{0}}\int_{-s_{0}}^{-s}\int_{M}\tau^{-1}|\mathcal{T}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x^{*};t}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}s\leq\epsilon^{-2}\delta,

where
\mathcal{T}:=\tau\mathrm{Ric}+\nabla^{2}(\tau f)-g/2
and
\tau=-t
. Switching the order of
\mathrm{d}t
and
\mathrm{d}s
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{-s_{0}}^{0}\int_{M}|\mathcal{T}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x^{*};t}\mathrm{d}t=\int_{-s_{0}}^{0}\int_{0}^{-t}\int_{M}\tau^{-1}|\mathcal{T}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x^{*};t}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}t\leq\epsilon^{-2}\delta.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{x^{*}}(\epsilon)-\mathcal{W}_{x^{*}}(\epsilon^{-1})=2\int_{-\epsilon^{-1}}^{-\epsilon}\int_{M}\tau^{-1}|\mathcal{T}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x^{*};t}\mathrm{d}t\leq 2\epsilon^{-1}\int_{-\epsilon^{-1}}^{-\epsilon}\int_{M}|\mathcal{T}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x^{*};t}\mathrm{d}t\leq 2\epsilon^{-3}\delta\leq\epsilon.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemmas
3.3
,
3.4
and
[
Bam20b
, Proposition 7.1]
indicate that the definition of
(\delta,r)
-selfsimilarity in
[
Bam20b
]
is equivalent to the one used here.

Our next result follows from Lemma
3.3
and
[
Bam20b
, Propositions 7.1, 9.1]
.

Lemma 3.5
.

Given a constant
\beta\in(0,1)
, let
x^{*}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{I}}
be
(\delta,r)
-selfsimilar with
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y,\beta)
. Suppose
d^{*}(x^{*},y^{*})\leq r
, then for any
s\in[\min\{\mathfrak{t}(x^{*}),\mathfrak{t}(y^{*})\}-\beta^{-1}r^{2},\min\{\mathfrak{t}(x^{*}),\mathfrak{t}(y^{*})\}-\beta r^{2}]
,

\displaystyle d_{W_{1}}^{s}(\nu_{x^{*};s},\nu_{y^{*};s})\leq C(n,Y,\beta)r.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
\mathfrak{t}(x^{*})\geq\mathfrak{t}(y^{*})
. By our assumption and Definition
2.10
, we have

\displaystyle d_{W_{1}}^{\mathfrak{t}(x^{*})-r^{2}}(\nu_{x^{*};\mathfrak{t}(x^{*})-r^{2}},\nu_{y^{*};\mathfrak{t}(x^{*})-r^{2}})\leq\epsilon_{0}r\leq r.

Thus, the conclusion follows from Lemma
3.3
and
[
Bam20b
, Propositions 7.1, 9.1]
.
∎

3.3
Static estimates in Ricci flow

In this subsection, we prove the following static estimate, which gives a quantitative version of
[
Bam20b
, Proposition 10.1]
and will play a crucial role later.

Theorem 3.6

(Static estimate)
.

Suppose that
x_{0}^{*}=(x_{0},t_{0}),\,x_{1}^{*}=(x_{1},t_{1})\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{I}}
satisfy
d^{*}(x_{0}^{*},x_{1}^{*})\leq r
and
\beta:=|t_{1}-t_{0}|\leq r^{2}/100
. Set

\displaystyle\Lambda:=\max\{\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(r^{2}/30)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(30r^{2}),\mathcal{W}_{x_{1}^{*}}(r^{2}/30)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{1}^{*}}(30r^{2})\}.

There exists a constant
\bar{\delta}=\bar{\delta}(n,Y)>0
such that if either
x_{0}^{*}
or
x_{1}^{*}
is
(\bar{\delta},r)
-selfsimilar, then the following estimate holds
:

\displaystyle\beta^{2}\int_{t_{0}-20r^{2}}^{t_{0}-r^{2}/20}\int_{M}|\mathrm{Ric}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{0}^{*};t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y)\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{2}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
r=1
and
t_{0}=0
. Moreover, we set
\nu_{t}=\nu_{x_{0}^{*};t}
,
f_{i}=f_{x_{i}^{*}}
,
\tau_{i}=t_{i}-t
,
\mathcal{T}_{i}:=\tau_{i}\mathrm{Ric}+\nabla^{2}(\tau_{i}f)-g/2
and
w_{i}=\tau_{i}(2\Delta f_{i}-|\nabla f_{i}|^{2}+\mathrm{R})+f_{i}-n
for
i\in\{0,1\}
. Note that
|\tau_{0}-\tau_{1}|=\beta
.

By Proposition
2.3
(iii), we have

\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}|\mathcal{T}_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq 10\Lambda.

(3.11)

By our assumption,
x_{0}^{*}
or
x_{1}^{*}
is
(\bar{\delta},1)
-selfsimilar, then it follows from Lemma
3.5
that

\displaystyle d_{W_{1}}^{s}(\nu_{x_{0}^{*};s},\nu_{x_{1}^{*};s})\leq C(n,Y)

(3.12)

for any
s\in[-20,-1/20]
, provided
\bar{\delta}\leq\bar{\delta}(n,Y)
.

By Proposition
2.7
, (
3.12
) and
[
FL25a
, Proposition A.2]
, we obtain for a small constant
\theta=\theta(n)>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}|\mathcal{T}_{1}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y)\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}|\mathcal{T}_{1}|^{2}e^{\theta f_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x^{*}_{1};t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(n,Y)\left(\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}|\mathcal{T}_{1}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x^{*}_{1};t}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}|\mathcal{T}_{1}|^{2}e^{2\theta f_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x^{*}_{1};t}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\leq C(n,Y)\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(3.13)

Here, we used the fact that
[-20,-1/20]\subset[t_{1}-30,t_{1}-1/30]
by our assumption.

Now we calculate:

\displaystyle\int_{M}\langle\mathrm{Ric},\nabla^{2}f_{1}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=\int_{M}\mathrm{Ric}(\nabla f_{0},\nabla f_{1})-\langle\nabla f_{1},\mathrm{div}(\mathrm{Ric})\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=\int_{M}\mathrm{Ric}(\nabla f_{0},\nabla f_{1})-\frac{\langle\nabla f_{1},\nabla\mathrm{R}\rangle}{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{M}\left(\mathrm{Ric}+\nabla^{2}f_{0}-\frac{g}{2\tau_{0}}\right)(\nabla f_{0},\nabla f_{1})-(\nabla^{2}f_{0})(\nabla f_{1},\nabla f_{0})+\frac{\langle\nabla f_{1},\nabla f_{0}\rangle}{2\tau_{0}}-\frac{\langle\nabla f_{1},\nabla\mathrm{R}\rangle}{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{M}\left(\mathrm{Ric}+\nabla^{2}f_{0}-\frac{g}{2\tau_{0}}\right)(\nabla f_{0},\nabla f_{1})+\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}}\langle\nabla\left(-\tau_{0}(|\nabla f_{0}|^{2}+\mathrm{R})+f_{0}\right),\nabla f_{1}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{M}\tau_{0}^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{0}(\nabla f_{0},\nabla f_{1})-\langle\nabla\left(\Delta f_{0}+\mathrm{R}-\frac{n}{2\tau_{0}}\right),\nabla f_{1}\rangle+\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}}\langle\nabla w_{0},\nabla f_{1}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}.

(3.14)

Similarly to (
3.3
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}|\nabla f_{1}|^{4}+|\nabla^{2}f_{1}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}\left(|\nabla f_{1}|^{4}+|\nabla^{2}f_{1}|^{2}\right)e^{\theta f_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x^{*}_{1};t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y).

(3.15)

Combining (
3.11
) and (
3.15
), we get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}\mathcal{T}_{0}(\nabla f_{0},\nabla f_{1})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right|\leq C(n,Y)\Lambda^{1/2}.

(3.16)

Similarly, using the integration by parts, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}\big\langle\nabla\left(\Delta f_{0}+\mathrm{R}-\frac{n}{2\tau_{0}}\right),\nabla f_{1}\big\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}\left(\Delta f_{0}+\mathrm{R}-\frac{n}{2\tau_{0}}\right)\left(\Delta f_{1}-\langle\nabla f_{1},\nabla f_{0}\rangle\right)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right|\leq C(n,Y)\Lambda^{1/2}.

(3.17)

By the weighted Bianchi identity (see Lemma
2.8
), we also obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}\langle\nabla w_{0},\nabla f_{1}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right|=

\displaystyle 2\left|\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}\langle\mathrm{div}_{f_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{0},\nabla f_{1}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left|\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}\langle\mathcal{T}_{0},\nabla^{2}f_{1}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right|\leq C(n,Y)\Lambda^{1/2}.

(3.18)

Combining (
3.16
), (
3.3
), (
3.3
) with (
3.14
), we conclude that

\left|\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}\langle\mathrm{Ric},\nabla^{2}f_{1}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right|\leq C(n,Y)\Lambda^{1/2}.

(3.19)

A similar argument also gives

\left|\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}\langle\mathrm{Ric},\nabla^{2}f_{0}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right|\leq C(n,Y)\Lambda^{1/2}.

(3.20)

Now, using

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}-\mathcal{T}_{1}-\nabla^{2}(\tau_{0}f_{0}-\tau_{1}f_{1})=(\tau_{0}-\tau_{1})\mathrm{Ric},

we obtain

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{0}-\mathcal{T}_{1}|^{2}=\beta^{2}|\mathrm{Ric}|^{2}+|\nabla^{2}(\tau_{0}f_{0}-\tau_{1}f_{1})|^{2}+2(\tau_{0}-\tau_{1})\langle\mathrm{Ric},\nabla^{2}(\tau_{0}f_{0}-\tau_{1}f_{1})\rangle.

Then, by (
3.11
), (
3.3
), (
3.19
) and (
3.20
), we finally get

\displaystyle\beta^{2}\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}|\mathrm{Ric}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{-20}^{-1/20}\int_{M}|\mathcal{T}_{0}-\mathcal{T}_{1}|^{2}-2(\tau_{0}-\tau_{1})\langle\mathrm{Ric},\nabla^{2}(\tau_{0}f_{0}-\tau_{1}f_{1})\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y)\Lambda^{1/2}.

This completes the proof.
∎

3.4
Construction of sharp splitting maps

Fix a base point
x_{0}^{*}=(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{I}}
. For simplicity, we set
\tau=t_{0}-t
and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}:=\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{0}^{*};t}=(4\pi\tau)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-f}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}.

The following definition of independent points can be viewed as a parabolic counterpart considered in prior work (see
[
CJN21
, Definition 4.4]
). Here,
\bar{\delta}=\bar{\delta}(n,Y)
is the constant from Theorem
3.6
.

Definition 3.7

(
(k,\alpha,\delta,r)
-independent points)
.

Given constants
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\delta>0
,
r>0
,
k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and a
(\bar{\delta},r)
-selfsimilar point
x_{0}^{*}
, a set of spacetime points
\{x_{i}^{*}=(x_{i},t_{i})\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
is called
(k,\alpha,\delta,r)
-independent
at
x_{0}^{*}
, if the following conditions hold.

(i)

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(r^{2}/30)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(30r^{2})\leq\delta
for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,k\}
.

(ii)

\displaystyle d^{*}(x_{i}^{*},x_{0}^{*})\leq r
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
.

(iii)

\displaystyle|t_{0}-t_{i}|\leq\frac{r^{2}}{100}
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
.

(iv)

Set
f_{i}=f_{x_{i}^{*}}
,
\tau_{i}=t_{i}-t
and
F_{i}=\tau_{i}f_{i}
. For
h_{i}=r^{-1}(F_{i}-F_{0})
and the symmetric matrix

\displaystyle A=(a_{ij}),\quad\text{where}\quad a_{ij}:=\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{t_{0}-10r^{2}}^{t_{0}-r^{2}/10}\int_{M}\langle\nabla h_{i},\nabla h_{j}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t,

the first eigenvalue
\lambda_{1}(A)
of
A
satisfies

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(A)\geq\alpha^{2}.

For the rest of the section, we always assume
\delta\ll\bar{\delta}
. Next, we prove the existence of almost splitting maps constructed from
(k,\alpha,\delta,r)
-independent sets.

Proposition 3.8
.

Assume
\{x_{i}^{*}=(x_{i},t_{i})\}_{i=1,\ldots,k}
is
(k,\alpha,\delta,r)
-independent at
x_{0}^{*}
. If
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y,\alpha)
, then we can find a
(k,\epsilon,r)
-splitting map
\vec{u}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k}):M\times[t_{0}-10r^{2},t_{0}]\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
at
x_{0}^{*}
, where

\displaystyle\epsilon=C(n,Y,\alpha)\sum_{i=0}^{k}\left(\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(r^{2}/30)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(30r^{2})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

For each
i\in\{0,\ldots,k\}
, by Lemma
3.1
, we can find
v_{i}
so that
\square v_{i}=-n/2
and

\displaystyle\int^{t_{0}-r^{2}/10}_{t_{0}-10r^{2}}\int_{M}\left|\tau_{i}\mathrm{Ric}+\nabla^{2}v_{i}-\frac{g}{2}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{i}^{*};t}\mathrm{d}t\leq 75r^{2}\left(\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(r^{2}/30)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(30r^{2})\right),

where
\tau_{i}=t_{i}-t
. By Lemma
3.5
, we can apply
[
FL25a
, Proposition A.2]
to obtain for
\theta=\theta(n)
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
,

\displaystyle\int^{t_{0}-r^{2}/10}_{t_{0}-10r^{2}}\int_{M}\left|\tau_{i}\mathrm{Ric}+\nabla^{2}v_{i}-\frac{g}{2}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq\int^{t_{0}-r^{2}/10}_{t_{0}-10r^{2}}\int_{M}\left|\tau_{i}\mathrm{Ric}+\nabla^{2}v_{i}-\frac{g}{2}\right|^{2}e^{\theta f_{i}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{i}^{*};t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int^{t_{0}-r^{2}/10}_{t_{0}-10r^{2}}\int_{M}\left|\tau_{i}\mathrm{Ric}+\nabla^{2}v_{i}-\frac{g}{2}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{i}^{*};t}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\left(\int^{t_{0}-r^{2}/10}_{t_{0}-10r^{2}}\int_{M}\left|\tau_{i}\mathrm{Ric}+\nabla^{2}v_{i}-\frac{g}{2}\right|^{2}e^{2\theta f_{i}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{i}^{*};t}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,Y)r^{2}\left(\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(r^{2}/30)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(30r^{2})\right)^{1/2},

(3.21)

where for the last inequality, we have used Proposition
2.7
and Lemma
3.1
(iii).

Therefore, it follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\int^{t_{0}-r^{2}/10}_{t_{0}-10r^{2}}\int_{M}\left|\tau_{i}\mathrm{Ric}+\nabla^{2}v_{i}-\frac{g}{2}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y)r^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(r^{2}/30)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(30r^{2})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
, set
u_{i}:=r^{-1}(v_{i}-v_{0})
. Thus, by taking difference of (
3.4
) and applying Theorem
3.6
to the integral of the
\mathrm{Ric}
-term, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\int^{t_{0}-r^{2}/10}_{t_{0}-10r^{2}}\int_{M}\left|\nabla^{2}u_{i}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y)\sum_{i=0}^{k}\left(\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(r^{2}/30)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(30r^{2})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

It follows from Lemma
3.1
(ii) that for any
t\in[t_{0}-10r^{2},t_{0}-r^{2}/10]
,

\displaystyle\int_{M}|\nabla(F_{0}-v_{0})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq 30r^{2}\left(\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(r^{2}/30)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(30r^{2})\right).

(3.22)

By Lemma
3.5
and
[
FL25a
, Proposition A.2]
again, we have for
\theta=\theta(n)>0
,
t\in[t_{0}-10r^{2},t_{0}-r^{2}/10]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{M}|\nabla(F_{i}-v_{i})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq\int_{M}|\nabla(F_{i}-v_{i})|^{2}e^{\theta f_{i}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{i}^{*};t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{M}|\nabla(F_{i}-v_{i})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{i}^{*};t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{M}|\nabla(F_{i}-v_{i})|^{2}e^{2\theta f_{i}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{i}^{*};t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,Y)r^{2}\left(\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(r^{2}/30)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(30r^{2})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(3.23)

where the last inequality holds by Lemma
3.1
(ii)(iii). Combining (
3.22
) and (
3.4
), we obtain for any
t\in[t_{0}-10r^{2},t_{0}-r^{2}/10]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{M}|\nabla(u_{i}-h_{i})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq C(n,Y)\left(\left(\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(r^{2}/30)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(30r^{2})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(r^{2}/30)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(30r^{2})\right).

(3.24)

Now we can use Definition
3.7
to modify the function
\{u_{i}\}
to make them satisfy the orthogonality condition. In fact, by Definition
3.7
, we see that for
A=(a_{ij})
satisfies
\lambda_{1}(A)\geq\alpha^{2}
. Set
A^{\prime}=(a_{ij}^{\prime})
, where

\displaystyle a_{ij}^{\prime}=\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{t_{0}-10r^{2}}^{t_{0}-r^{2}/10}\int_{M}\langle\nabla u_{i},\nabla u_{j}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t.

By (
3.24
), if
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y,\alpha)
, the first eigenvalue
\lambda_{1}(A^{\prime})\geq\alpha^{2}/2
.
Thus, we can find a matrix
B=(b_{ij})
with
\|B\|\leq C(\alpha)
so that for
u^{\prime}_{i}:=b_{ij}u_{j}
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}-10r^{2}}^{t_{0}-r^{2}/10}\int_{M}\langle\nabla u^{\prime}_{i},\nabla u^{\prime}_{j}\rangle-\delta_{ij}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t=0.

After adding a constant vector to
\vec{u}^{\prime}=(u^{\prime}_{i},\ldots,u^{\prime}_{k})
so that
\vec{u}^{\prime}(x_{0}^{*})=0
, we conclude that
\vec{u}^{\prime}
satisfies all the requirements of Definition
2.22
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Definition 3.9

(Entropy pinching)
.

For
k\in\{1,2\ldots,n\}
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\delta>0
and
r>0
, the
(k,\alpha,\delta,r)
-entropy pinching at
x_{0}^{*}
is defined as

{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\mathcal{E}_{r}^{k,\alpha,\delta}$}}}\mathcal{E}_{r}^{k,\alpha,\delta}(x_{0}^{*}):=\inf_{\{x_{i}^{*}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}\,\mathrm{are}\,(k,\alpha,\delta,r)-\mathrm{independent}\ \mathrm{at}\ x_{0}^{*}}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\left(\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(r^{2}/30)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{i}^{*}}(30r^{2})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Here, we implicitly assume that
x_{0}^{*}
is
(\bar{\delta},r)
-selfsimilar and there is at least one
(k,\alpha,\delta,r)
-independent set at
x_{0}^{*}
.

Note that our definition of entropy pinching is different from
[
CJN21
, Definition 4.23]
. As a corollary of Proposition
3.8
, we have:

Corollary 3.10
.

Suppose
x_{0}^{*}
is
(\bar{\delta},r)
-selfsimilar and there exists a
(k,\alpha,\delta,r)
-independent set at
x_{0}^{*}
. If
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y,\alpha)
, then there exists a
(k,\epsilon,r)
-splitting map
\vec{u}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k}):M\times[t_{0}-10r^{2},t_{0}]\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
at
x_{0}^{*}
, where

\displaystyle\epsilon=C(n,Y,\alpha)\mathcal{E}_{r}^{k,\alpha,\delta}(x_{0}^{*}).

3.5
Hessian decay of heat flows

We establish the following proposition, demonstrating that the almost splitting property at an almost self-similar point can be propagated to all scales.

Proposition 3.11
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, we can find
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y,\epsilon)
such that the following hold:

(i)

If
z\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{I}^{-}}
is
(\delta,r)
-selfsimilar, and there exists a
(k,\delta,r)
-splitting map at
z
, then
z
is
(k,\epsilon,s)
-splitting (cf. Definition
2.21
) for any
s\in[\epsilon r,\epsilon^{-1}r]
.

(ii)

If
z\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{I}^{-}}
is
(\delta,r)
-selfsimilar and
(k,\delta,r)
-splitting, then
z
is
(k,\epsilon,s)
-splitting for any
s\in[\epsilon r,\epsilon^{-1}r]
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
r=1
. We will only prove (i), since the proof of (ii) is similar.

Suppose the conclusion is false. Then we can find
\mathcal{X}^{l}\in\mathcal{M}(n,Y,T_{l})
with
\mathbb{I}_{l}=[-0.98T_{l},0]
and
z_{l}^{*}\in\mathcal{X}^{l}
such that
z_{l}^{*}
is
(l^{-2},1)
-selfsimilar. In addition, one can find
(k,l^{-2},1)
-splitting map
\vec{u}^{l}=(u^{l}_{1},\ldots,u^{l}_{k})
at
z_{l}^{*}
. However,
z_{l}^{*}
is not
(k,\epsilon,s_{l})
-splitting for some
s_{l}\in[\epsilon,\epsilon^{-1}]
.

By taking a subsequence, we assume

\displaystyle(\mathcal{X}^{l},d^{*}_{l},z_{l}^{*},\mathfrak{t}_{l}-\mathfrak{t}_{l}(z_{l}^{*}))\xrightarrow[i\to\infty]{\hskip 8.19447pt\hat{C}^{\infty}\hskip 8.19447pt}(Z,d_{Z},z,\mathfrak{t}).

By the smooth convergence, we assume that
u_{i}^{l}\to y_{i}
in
C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}_{(-10,0)})
such that on
\mathcal{R}_{(-10,-1/10)}
,

\displaystyle\langle\nabla y_{i},\nabla y_{j}\rangle=\delta_{ij},\quad\nabla^{2}y_{i}=0,\quad\partial_{\mathfrak{t}}y_{i}=0.

(3.25)

Arguing as in the proof of Lemma
3.3
, we conclude that
Z
is a Ricci shrinker space. Furthermore, it follows from
[
FL25a
, Proposition 8.2, Remark 8.3]
that one can extend functions
\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}\}
on
\mathcal{R}_{(-\infty,0)}
so that (
3.25
) still holds. Thus, by
[
FL25a
, Proposition 8.4]
,
\mathcal{R}_{(-\infty,0]}
admits an
\mathbb{R}^{k}
-splitting. However, this implies that for sufficiently large
l
,
z_{l}^{*}
is
(k,\epsilon,s_{l})
-splitting. Consequently, we obtain a contradiction.
∎

Next, we prove a Hessian decay of heat flows. At the first reading, it may be helpful for the readers to assume that the Ricci flow limit space
Z
in the proof is
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{k}
or
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{m}_{k}(\Gamma)
(see Subsection
5.4
); this specialization already suffices for the main results of the paper.

Theorem 3.12

(Hessian decay)
.

Let
\eta>0
be a fixed constant. Suppose that
x_{0}^{*}=(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{I}}
is
(\delta,r)
-selfsimilar and is not
(k+1,\eta,r)
-splitting (cf. Definition
2.21
).

Let
\vec{u}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k}):M\times[t_{0}-10r^{2},t_{0}]\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
be a
(k,\delta,r)
-splitting map, and
h:M\times[t_{0}-10r^{2},t_{0}]\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function satisfying
\square h=0
. Define

\displaystyle v:=h-\sum_{i=1}^{k}b_{i}u_{i},\quad where\quad b_{i}:=\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{t_{0}-8r^{2}}^{t_{0}-8r^{2}/10}\int_{M}\langle\nabla h,\nabla u_{i}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t.

Then there exists a constant
\theta=\theta(n,Y,\eta)\in(0,1)
such that the following holds
:
if
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y,\eta)
, then

\int_{t_{0}-2r^{2}}^{t_{0}-2r^{2}/10}\int_{M}|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq\theta\int_{t_{0}-8r^{2}}^{t_{0}-8r^{2}/10}\int_{M}|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t.

(3.26)

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
r=1
and
t_{0}=0
.

In addition, we assume the following normalization holds:

\displaystyle\int_{M}|\nabla v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-8}=1,\quad\int_{M}v\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-8}=0.

(3.27)

By Theorem
2.6
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{M}v^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-8}\leq 16.

(3.28)

Since
\mathchoice{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}{\displaystyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}\int_{M}v^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=-2\int_{M}|\nabla v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq 0
, we obtain for any
t\in[-8,0]
,

\displaystyle\int_{M}v^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq 16.

Similarly, by
\mathchoice{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}{\displaystyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}\int_{M}|\nabla v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=-2\int_{M}|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle 2\int_{-8}^{0}\int_{M}|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t+\sup_{t\in[-8,0)}\int_{M}|\nabla v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq 2.

If
\displaystyle\int_{M}|\nabla v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-2}\leq 1/10
, then we have

\displaystyle 2\int_{-2}^{-2/10}\int_{M}|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq\int_{M}|\nabla v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-2}\leq\frac{1}{10}.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle 2\int_{-8}^{-8/10}\int_{M}\left|\nabla^{2}v\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\left|\nabla v\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-8}-\int_{M}\left|\nabla v\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-8/10}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{M}\left|\nabla v\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-8}-\int_{M}\left|\nabla v\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-2}\geq 1-\frac{1}{10}=\frac{9}{10}.

Thus, the conclusion (
3.26
) holds for
\theta=1/9
. Thus, in the following proof, we may also assume

\displaystyle\frac{1}{10}\leq\int_{M}\left|\nabla v\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-2}\leq 1.

(3.29)

We prove the theorem by contradiction. Suppose the conclusion fails. Then there exists a sequence of smooth, closed Ricci flows
\mathcal{X}^{l}=\{M^{n}_{l},(g_{l}(t))_{t\in\mathbb{I}_{l}^{++}}\}\in\mathcal{M}(n,Y,T_{l})
, where
\mathbb{I}_{l}^{++}=[-T_{l},0]
, and
x_{0,l}^{*}\in\mathcal{X}^{l}_{0}
is not
(k+1,\eta,1)
-splitting.

Set
\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{t}=\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{0,l}^{*};t}=(4\pi\tau)^{-n/2}e^{-f_{l}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g_{l}(t)}
, where
\tau=-t
. Assume the corresponding functions
\vec{u}^{l}=(u^{l}_{1},\ldots,u^{l}_{k}),h^{l},v^{l}
, as well as the constants
b_{i}^{l}
, are defined as in the statement of Theorem
3.12
, and satisfy the normalization conditions (
3.27
) and (
3.29
). Furthermore, suppose
\vec{u}^{l}
is a
(k,l^{-2},1)
-splitting map.

However, inequality (
3.26
) is violated; that is,

\int_{-2}^{-\frac{2}{10}}\int_{M_{l}}|\nabla^{2}v^{l}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}^{l}\mathrm{d}t>(1-l^{-2})\int_{-8}^{-\frac{8}{10}}\int_{M_{l}}|\nabla^{2}v^{l}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}^{l}\mathrm{d}t.

(3.30)

Passing to a subsequence if necessary, it follows from Theorems
2.11
and
2.12
that

\displaystyle(M_{l}\times\mathbb{I}_{l},d^{*}_{l},x_{0,l}^{*},\mathfrak{t}_{l})\xrightarrow[l\to\infty]{\hskip 8.19447pt\hat{C}^{\infty}\hskip 8.19447pt}(Z,d_{Z},z,\mathfrak{t}),

where
(Z,d_{Z},z,\mathfrak{t})
, by our assumption on
x_{0,l}^{*}
, is a Ricci shrinker space, whose regular part is a Ricci flow spacetime
(\mathcal{R},\mathfrak{t},\partial_{\mathfrak{t}},g^{Z})
. In particular, we have on
\mathcal{R}_{(-\infty,0)}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ric}(g^{Z})+\nabla^{2}f_{z}=\frac{g^{Z}}{2\tau},

where
\tau=-\mathfrak{t}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{R}_{t}
is connected for any
t<0
. Let
\phi_{l}
denote the diffeomorphisms given in Theorem
2.12
.

By the smooth convergence in Theorem
2.12
, we assume
v^{l}\to v^{\infty}
and
u_{i}^{l}\to y_{i}
smoothly on
\mathcal{R}_{(-8,0)}
. On the other hand, since
\vec{u}^{l}
is a
(k,l^{-2},1)
-splitting map, we conclude from Proposition
3.11
that
\mathcal{R}_{(-\infty,0)}
splits off an
\mathbb{R}^{k}
isometrically, on which the coordinates are given by
\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}\}
.

Next, we consider the family of cutoff functions
\{\eta_{r,A}\}
from
[
FL25a
, Proposition 8.23]
. Then, it is clear from
[
FL25a
, Proposition 8.23 (3)]
that on
\mathcal{R}_{[-100,-1/100]}
,

\displaystyle r^{2}\left(|\partial_{\mathfrak{t}}\eta_{r,A}|+|\Delta\eta_{r,A}|\right)+r|\nabla\eta_{r,A}|\leq C(n).

(3.31)

Also, it follows from
[
FL25a
, Proposition 8.23 (4)]
that for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{R}_{[-100,-1/100]}\bigcap\{0<\eta_{r,A}<1\}}1\,\mathrm{d}V_{g^{Z}_{t}}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y,A,\epsilon)r^{4-\epsilon}.

(3.32)

Claim 3.13
.

For any
s_{1}\in[1/100,2]
, we have

\lim_{l\to\infty}\int_{M_{l}}|\nabla v^{l}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{1}}=\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-s_{1}}}|\nabla v^{\infty}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-s_{1}}.

(3.33)

It follows from the hypercontractivity (see
[
Bam20a
, Theorem 12.1]
) and (
3.27
) that for any
s\in(0,2]
,

\displaystyle\int_{M_{l}}|\nabla v^{l}|^{5}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s}\leq 1.

(3.34)

Taking the limit, we also have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-s}}|\nabla v^{\infty}|^{5}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-s}\leq 1.

For fixed
s_{1}\in[1/100,2]
, set
B^{l}_{r,A}:=\phi_{l}\left(\mathrm{supp}\big(\eta_{r,A}\big)\cap\mathcal{R}_{-s_{1}}\right)
, which is well defined for fixed
r
and
A
, provided that
l
is sufficiently large.

By the smooth convergence on
\mathcal{R}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\lim_{l\to\infty}\int_{B^{l}_{r,A}}\left|\nabla v^{l}\right|^{2}\eta_{r,A}\circ\phi_{l}^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-s_{1}}^{l}=\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-s_{1}}}\left|\nabla v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\eta_{r,A}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-s_{1}}.

(3.35)

On the one hand, it follows from Theorem
2.12
that once
r
and
A
are fixed,

\displaystyle B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};A/3)\cap\phi_{l}\left(\mathrm{supp}\big(\eta_{r,A}\big)\right)\cap M_{l}\times\{-s_{1}\}\subset B^{l}_{r,A}\subset B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};3A)\cap M_{l}\times\{-s_{1}\}

(3.36)

for sufficiently large
l
. Thus, it is clear that

\displaystyle\int_{M_{l}\setminus B_{r,A}^{l}}\left|\nabla v^{l}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{1}}\leq\int_{M_{l}\setminus B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};A/3)}\left|\nabla v^{l}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{1}}+\int_{B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};A/3)\setminus\phi_{l}\left(\mathrm{supp}(\eta_{r,A})\right)}\left|\nabla v^{l}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{1}}.

By Proposition
2.5
and (
3.34
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{M_{l}\setminus B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};A/3)}\left|\nabla v^{l}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{1}}\leq\left(\int_{M_{l}\setminus B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};A/3)}\left|\nabla v^{l}\right|^{5}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{1}}\right)^{\frac{2}{5}}\left(\int_{M_{l}\setminus B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};A/3)}1\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{1}}\right)^{\frac{3}{5}}\leq\Psi(A^{-1}).

(3.37)

By
[
FL25a
, Proposition 8.23 (2)]
, we know that any
x\in\left(B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};A/3)\setminus\phi_{l}\left(\mathrm{supp}(\eta_{r,A})\right)\right)\cap M_{l}\times\{-s_{2}\}
satisfies
r_{\mathrm{Rm}}(x)\leq 3r
for all sufficiently large
l
, where
r_{\mathrm{Rm}}
denotes the curvature radius. Thus, by using
[
FL25a
, Theorem 1.12 (b)]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};A/3)\setminus\phi_{l}\left(\mathrm{supp}(\eta_{r,A})\right)}\left|\nabla v^{l}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{1}}\leq\left(\int_{B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};A/3)\setminus\phi_{l}\left(\mathrm{supp}(\eta_{r,A})\right)}1\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{1}}\right)^{\frac{3}{5}}\leq C(n,A,Y)r.

(3.38)

Combining (
3.37
) and (
3.38
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{M_{l}\setminus B_{r,A}^{l}}\left|\nabla v^{l}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{1}}\leq\Psi(A^{-1})+C(n,A,Y)r.

(3.39)

In addition, for sufficiently large
l
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B_{r,A}^{l}}\left|\nabla v^{l}\right|^{2}\left(1-\eta_{r,A}\circ\phi_{l}^{-1}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{1}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{B_{r,A}^{l}}\left|\nabla v^{l}\right|^{5}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{1}}\right)^{\frac{2}{5}}\left(\int_{B_{r,A}^{l}}\left(1-\eta_{r,A}\circ\phi_{l}^{-1}\right)^{\frac{5}{3}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{1}}\right)^{\frac{3}{5}}\leq C(n,A,Y)r,

(3.40)

where the last inequality holds by the same reason as in (
3.38
).

Thus, we can first choose a large
A
and then choose a small
r
so that all integrals (
3.39
) and (
3.5
) are as small as we want. Combining this fact with (
3.35
), we obtain (
3.33
) and thus complete the proof of Claim
3.13
.

Claim 3.14
.

For any
1/100\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}<8
, we have on
\mathcal{R}_{[-s_{2},-s_{1}]}\cap\mathrm{supp}(\eta_{r,A})
,

\displaystyle|\nabla v^{\infty}|\leq C(n,A,Y,s_{2}).

(3.41)

We set
D^{l}_{r,A}=\phi_{l}\left(\mathrm{supp}(\eta_{r,A})\right)\cap M_{l}\times[-s_{2},-s_{1}]
. Similar to (
3.36
), we have

\displaystyle D^{l}_{r,A}\subset B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};3A)\cap M_{l}\times[-s_{2},-s_{1}].

By the reproduction formula, for any
x^{*}\in D^{l}_{r,A}
, we have for
s_{2}^{\prime}=(s_{2}+8)/2
,

\displaystyle|\nabla v^{l}|^{2}(x^{*})\leq\int_{M_{l}}|\nabla v^{l}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x^{*};-s_{2}^{\prime}}.

(3.42)

Now, we choose
t^{\prime}<\mathfrak{t}_{l}(x^{*})
such that
\beta:=\mathfrak{t}_{l}(x^{*})-t^{\prime}
is small and will be determined later. By our assumption on
x_{0,l}^{*}
and Lemma
3.5
, we conclude that

\displaystyle d_{W_{1}}^{t^{\prime}}(\nu_{x^{*};t^{\prime}},\nu^{l}_{t^{\prime}})\leq C(n,Y,A,\beta).

(3.43)

For a small constant
\gamma\in(0,1)
, it follows from (
3.43
) and
[
FL25a
, Proposition A.2]
that if
\beta=\beta(n,\gamma,s_{2})
is small, then

\displaystyle\nu_{x^{*};-s_{2}^{\prime}}\leq C(n,Y,A,\gamma,s_{2})e^{\gamma f_{l}}\nu^{l}_{-s_{2}^{\prime}}.

(3.44)

By hypercontractivity (see
[
Bam20a
, Theorem 12.1]
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{M_{l}}|\nabla v^{l}|^{1+8/s^{\prime}_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s^{\prime}_{2}}\leq 1

and hence

\displaystyle\int_{M_{l}}|\nabla v^{l}|^{2}e^{\gamma f_{l}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{2}^{\prime}}\leq\left(\int_{M_{l}}|\nabla v^{l}|^{1+8/s_{2}^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{2}^{\prime}}\right)^{\frac{2s_{2}^{\prime}}{8+s_{2}^{\prime}}}\left(\int_{M_{l}}\exp\left(\gamma\frac{8+s_{2}^{\prime}}{8-s_{2}^{\prime}}f_{l}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-s_{2}^{\prime}}\right)^{\frac{8-s_{2}^{\prime}}{8+s_{2}^{\prime}}}\leq C(n,Y,s_{2}),

(3.45)

where we used Proposition
2.7
, provided that
\gamma=\gamma(n,Y,s_{2})
is small.

Combining (
3.42
), (
3.44
), (
3.45
) and using smooth convergence, we have completed the proof of Claim
3.14
.

Claim 3.15
.

For any
1/100\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}<8

2\int_{-s_{2}}^{-s_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}|\nabla^{2}v^{\infty}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t=\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-s_{2}}}|\nabla v^{\infty}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-s_{2}}-\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-s_{1}}}|\nabla v^{\infty}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-s_{1}}.

By the evolution equation on the regular part, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\left|\nabla v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\eta_{r,A}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}=-2\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\left|\nabla^{2}v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\eta_{r,A}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}+\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\big|\nabla v^{\infty}\big|^{2}\square\eta_{r,A}-2\langle\nabla\big|\nabla v^{\infty}\big|^{2},\nabla\eta_{r,A}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}.

Consequently, for fixed
1/100\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}<8
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-s_{2}}}|\nabla v^{\infty}|^{2}\eta_{r,A}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-s_{2}}-\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-s_{1}}}|\nabla v^{\infty}|^{2}\eta_{r,A}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-s_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int_{-s_{2}}^{-s_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}|\nabla^{2}v^{\infty}|^{2}\eta_{r,A}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t-\int_{-s_{2}}^{-s_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\big|\nabla v^{\infty}\big|^{2}\square\eta_{r,A}-2\langle\nabla\big|\nabla v^{\infty}\big|^{2},\nabla\eta_{r,A}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t.

(3.46)

Now we estimate the error terms. First, we compute

\displaystyle\int_{-s_{2}}^{-s_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\left|\nabla v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\left|\square\eta_{r,A}\right|\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,Y,A,s_{2})r^{-2}\left(\int_{\mathcal{R}_{[-s_{2},-s_{1}]}\bigcap\{0<\eta_{r,A}<1\}}1\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t\right)\leq C(n,Y,A,s_{2})r

where we used (
3.31
), (
3.32
) and (
3.41
) for the last line.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\int_{-s_{2}}^{-s_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\left|\nabla\big|\nabla v^{\infty}\big|^{2}\right|\left|\nabla\eta_{r,A}\right|\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,Y,A)r^{-1}\iint_{\mathcal{R}_{[-s_{2},-s_{1}]}\bigcap\{0<\eta_{r,A}<1\}}\left|\nabla^{2}v^{\infty}\right|\left|\nabla v^{\infty}\right|\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,Y,A)r^{-1}\left(\iint_{\mathcal{R}_{[-s_{2},-s_{1}]}\bigcap\{0<\eta_{r,A}<1\}}\left|\nabla^{2}v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathcal{R}_{[-s_{2},-s_{1}]}\bigcap\{0<\eta_{r,A}<1\}}\left|\nabla v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,Y,A,s_{2})r^{1/2},

where we used (
3.31
), (
3.32
) and (
3.41
).

Therefore, the second integral on the right-hand side of (
3.46
) can be made arbitrarily small if we first choose a large
A
and then choose a small
r
. Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-s_{2}}}|\nabla v^{\infty}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-s_{2}}-\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-s_{1}}}|\nabla v^{\infty}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-s_{1}}=2\int_{-s_{2}}^{-s_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}|\nabla^{2}v^{\infty}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t.

This completes the proof of Claim
3.15
.

By
[
FL25a
, Lemma D.1]
,
\left(\iota_{z}(\mathcal{X}^{z}_{-1}),d^{Z}_{-1},\nu_{z;-1}\right)
is a
\mathrm{RCD}(1/2,\infty)
-space. Thus, the eigenvalues and eigenfunctions of
\Delta_{f_{z}(-1)}
are well defined (see
[
GMS13
]
), where
\Delta_{f_{z}(-1)}
denotes the weighted Laplacian on the
\mathrm{RCD}
-space.

Claim 3.16
.

Let
\lambda_{i}
be all nonzero eigenvalues of
\Delta_{f_{z}(-1)}
with associated eigenfunctions
\psi_{i}
, then the following holds for some constant
c_{0}=c_{0}(n,Y,\eta)>0
,

\displaystyle 0<\lambda_{1}=\cdots=\lambda_{k}=\frac{1}{2}<\frac{1}{2}+c_{0}\leq\lambda_{k+1}.

Since
(\iota_{z}(\mathcal{X}^{z}_{-1}),d^{Z}_{-1})
splits off an
\mathbb{R}^{k}
, it follows from
[
GKKO20
, Theorem 1.1]
that
\lambda_{1}=\cdots=\lambda_{k}=1/2
. If
\lambda_{k+1}\leq 1/2+c_{0}
for some small
c_{0}
, then we can, by using
\eta_{r,A}
and
\phi_{l}
, pull back the first
k+1
eigenfunctions to
M_{l}\times\{-1\}
to get
\vec{\psi}^{l}=(\psi^{l}_{1},\ldots,\psi^{l}_{k+1})
satisfying for
1\leq i,j\leq k+1
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\leq\int_{M_{l}}(\psi_{i}^{l})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-1}^{l}\leq 2,\quad\left\lvert\int_{M_{l}}\langle\nabla\psi^{l}_{i},\nabla\psi^{l}_{j}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-1}^{l}-\delta_{ij}\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{100}

and

\displaystyle\dfrac{\int_{M_{l}}|\nabla\psi_{i}^{l}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-1}^{l}}{\int_{M_{l}}(\psi_{i}^{l})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-1}^{l}}\leq\frac{1}{2}+2c_{0}.

Consequently, if
c_{0}
is sufficiently small, it follows from the same argument as in
[
FL25a
, Proposition C.3, Corollary C.4]
that there exists a
(k+1,\Psi(c_{0}),1/\sqrt{10})
-splitting map at
x_{0,l}^{*}
. By Proposition
3.11
(i), this implies that if
c_{0}\leq c_{0}(n,Y,\eta)
, then
x_{0,l}^{*}
is
(k+1,\eta,1)
-splitting, which contradicts our assumption. This completes the proof of Claim
3.16
. Without loss of generality, we may assume
\psi_{i}=y_{i}/\sqrt{2}
on
\mathcal{R}_{-1}
for
1\leq i\leq k
.

We now extend
\psi_{i}
to a spacetime function, still denoted by
\psi_{i}
, on
\mathcal{R}_{(-\infty,0)}
. Let
\boldsymbol{\psi}^{s}
be the flow generated by
X:=\tau(\partial_{\mathfrak{t}}-\nabla f_{z})
, which is well-defined for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
on
\mathcal{R}_{(-\infty,0)}
by
[
FL25a
, Theorem 1.9]
. Define a function
u
on
\mathcal{R}_{(-\infty,0)}
such that
u=\psi_{i}
on
\mathcal{R}_{-1}
and
u
is invariant under
\boldsymbol{\psi}^{s}
. Then, define the function
\psi_{i}
on
\mathcal{R}_{(-\infty,0)}
by
\psi_{i}:=\tau^{\lambda_{i}}u
. A direct computation shows that
\square\psi_{i}=0
on
\mathcal{R}_{(-\infty,0)}
and

\displaystyle\Delta_{f_{z}}\psi_{i}+\tau^{-1}\lambda_{i}\psi_{i}=0.

(3.47)

Moreover, for any
t<0
, we have the identity

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\psi_{i}(t)\psi_{j}(t)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}=\tau^{2\lambda_{i}}\delta_{ij}.

(3.48)

In other words,
\{\tau^{-\lambda_{i}}\psi_{i}(t)\}
is an orthonormal basis with respect to
\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}
.

Next, we define

\displaystyle\tilde{v}(x,s)=v^{\infty}(\boldsymbol{\psi}^{s}(x))\qquad(x,s)\in\mathcal{R}_{-1}\times[0,\infty).

A direct computation yields

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\tilde{v}=\Delta_{f_{z}(-1)}\tilde{v}

on
\mathcal{R}_{-1}\times[0,\infty)
. Since
W^{1,2}(\mathcal{R}_{-1},\nu_{z;-1})
is dense in
W^{1,2}(\iota_{z}(\mathcal{X}^{z}_{-1}),\nu_{z;-1})
(see the proof of
[
FL25a
, Lemma D.1]
), we may regard
\tilde{v}
as the heat flow (cf.
[
AGS14
, Section 4]
) on the
\mathrm{RCD}(1/2,\infty)
-space
\left(\iota_{z}(\mathcal{X}^{z}_{-1}),d^{Z}_{-1},\nu_{z;-1}\right)
with initial data
v^{\infty}|_{\mathcal{R}_{-1}}
.

Define the coefficients
a_{i}=\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-1}}v^{\infty}\psi_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-1}
. Since
\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-1}}v^{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-1}=0
, we have the
L^{2}(\nu_{z;-1})
-expansion

\displaystyle v^{\infty}|_{\mathcal{R}_{-1}}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{i}\psi_{i}(-1).

As
\psi_{i}(-1)
is an eigenfunction with eigenvalue
\lambda_{i}
, the heat flow from
\psi_{i}(-1)
is
e^{-\lambda_{i}s}\psi_{i}(-1)
. By the contraction property of the heat flow,

\displaystyle\tilde{v}(\cdot,s)=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda_{i}s}a_{i}\psi_{i}(-1)\quad\text{in}\quad L^{2}(\nu_{z;-1}).

Using the definitions of
\tilde{v}
and
\psi_{i}(t)
, this implies

\displaystyle v^{\infty}|_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{i}\psi_{i}(t)

(3.49)

in
L^{2}(\nu_{z;t})
, for any
t\in[-1,0)
.

Repeating the argument for any base time in
(-8,0)
in place of
-1
, we conclude that the
L^{2}
-expansion (
3.49
) holds for all
t\in(-8,0)
. Combining this with Claims
3.15
,
3.16
, (
3.47
), and (
3.48
), we obtain for any
s\in[-1/100,8)
,

\displaystyle\int_{-s}^{-\frac{s}{10}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\left|\nabla^{2}v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-s}}\left|\nabla v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-s}-\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-s/10}}\left|\nabla v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-s/10}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}a^{2}_{i}\left(s^{2\lambda_{i}-1}-\left(\frac{s}{10}\right)^{2\lambda_{i}-1}\right)\lambda_{i}.

(3.50)

Letting
s\nearrow 8
, the identity (
3.50
) holds for any
s\in[-1/100,8]
.

Claim 3.17
.

There exists an
i_{0}\geq k+1
such that
a_{i_{0}}\neq 0
.

By our definition of
v^{l}
, for any
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{-8}^{-8/10}\int_{M_{l}}\langle\nabla v^{l},\nabla u_{i}^{l}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{t}\mathrm{d}t=0.

By the evolution equation
\mathchoice{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}{\displaystyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}\int_{M_{l}}\langle\nabla v^{l},\nabla u_{i}^{l}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{t}=-2\int_{M_{l}}\langle\nabla^{2}v^{l},\nabla^{2}u_{i}^{l}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{t}
and the fact that
\vec{u}^{l}
is a
(k,l^{-2},1)
-splitting map, we obtain

\left|\int_{M_{l}}\langle\nabla v^{l},\nabla u_{i}^{l}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{-1}\right|\leq Cl^{-1}.

Letting
l\to\infty
, we derive, by the same argument as in Claim
3.13
, that

\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-1}}\langle\nabla v^{\infty},\nabla\psi_{i}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-1}=0,

which implies

a_{i}=\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-1}}v^{\infty}\psi_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-1}=0

for any
1\leq i\leq k
. By our assumption (
3.29
) and Claim
3.13
again, we conclude that the constants
a_{i}
are not all zero for
i\geq k+1
, which completes the proof of Claim
3.17
.

By Claim
3.17
, it follows from (
3.50
) that

\displaystyle\int_{-2}^{-\frac{2}{10}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\left|\nabla^{2}v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}=\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}a^{2}_{i}\left(2^{2\lambda_{i}-1}-\left(\frac{2}{10}\right)^{2\lambda_{i}-1}\right)\lambda_{i}>0.

(3.51)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\int_{-8}^{-\frac{8}{10}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\left|\nabla^{2}v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}=\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}a^{2}_{i}\left(8^{2\lambda_{i}-1}-\left(\frac{8}{10}\right)^{2\lambda_{i}-1}\right)\lambda_{i}>0.

(3.52)

Taking the quotient of (
3.51
) and (
3.52
), we obtain

\displaystyle\dfrac{\int_{-2}^{-\frac{2}{10}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\left|\nabla^{2}v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}}{\int_{-8}^{-\frac{8}{10}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\left|\nabla^{2}v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}}=\dfrac{\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}a^{2}_{i}\left(2^{2\lambda_{i}-1}-\left(\frac{2}{10}\right)^{2\lambda_{i}-1}\right)\lambda_{i}}{\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}a^{2}_{i}\left(8^{2\lambda_{i}-1}-\left(\frac{8}{10}\right)^{2\lambda_{i}-1}\right)\lambda_{i}}\leq\left(\frac{1}{4}\right)^{2c_{0}},

(3.53)

where in the last inequality, we have used the fact from Claim
3.16
that
2\lambda_{i}-1\geq 2c_{0}
for all
i\geq k+1
.

However, by taking the limit of (
3.30
), it follows from Claims
3.13
and
3.15
that

\int_{-2}^{-\frac{2}{10}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\left|\nabla^{2}v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t\geq\int_{-8}^{-\frac{8}{10}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\left|\nabla^{2}v^{\infty}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t,

which contradicts (
3.53
), and hence completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

3.6
A covering lemma for independent points

In this subsection, we discuss how to find independent points in applications.

Lemma 3.18
.

There exists a constant
L=L(n,Y)>1
such that the following holds.

Suppose
y_{0}^{*}=(y_{0},t_{0})\in\mathcal{X}
is
(\delta,r)
-selfsimilar. Let
S
be the subset consisting of all
(\delta,r)
-selfsimilar points in
B^{*}(y_{0}^{*},r)\bigcap M\times[t_{0}-\alpha^{2}r^{2}/L^{2},t_{0}+\alpha^{2}r^{2}/L^{2}]
. Assume that there exists no
(k,\alpha,\delta,r)
-independent points in
S
at
y_{0}^{*}
. If
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y,\alpha)
, then we can find
\{y_{i}^{*}\}_{1\leq i\leq N}\subset S
with
N\leq C(n,Y)\alpha^{1-k}
and

\displaystyle S\subset\bigcup_{i=0}^{N}B^{*}(y^{*}_{i},\alpha r).

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
t_{0}=0
and
r=1
, and set
\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=\mathrm{d}\nu_{y_{0}^{*};t}=(4\pi|t|)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-f}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}
.

We choose the maximal
\alpha
-separated set
\{y_{0}^{*},y_{1}^{*},\ldots,y_{N}^{*}\}\subset S
containing
y_{0}^{*}
, i.e.,
d^{*}(y^{*}_{i},y^{*}_{j})\geq\alpha
for all
i\neq j
. By Definition
2.10
and monotonicity the following holds:

d_{W_{1}}^{-\alpha^{2}/2}\left(\nu_{y_{i}^{*};-\alpha^{2}/2},\nu_{y_{j}^{*};-\alpha^{2}/2}\right)\geq\epsilon_{0}\alpha.

We set
\beta=10\alpha/L
. Then for a constant
D=D(n,Y)>1
to be determined later, we may choose
L=L(n,Y)
large so that

\displaystyle d_{W_{1}}^{-\beta^{2}}\left(\nu_{y_{i}^{*};-\beta^{2}},\nu_{y_{j}^{*};-\beta^{2}}\right)\geq D\beta.

(3.54)

Set
\tau_{i}=\mathfrak{t}(y_{i}^{*})-t
,
\mathrm{d}\nu_{y_{i}^{*};t}=(4\pi\tau_{i})^{-n/2}e^{-f_{i}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}
,
F_{i}=\tau_{i}f_{i}
,
\mathcal{T}_{i}:=\tau_{i}\mathrm{Ric}+\nabla^{2}F_{i}-g/2
,
w_{i}=2\tau_{i}(\Delta f_{i}-|\nabla f_{i}|^{2}+\mathrm{R})+f_{i}-n
and
W_{i}=\mathcal{W}_{y_{i}^{*}}(1)
for
0\leq i\leq N
. For
1\leq i\leq N
, we define
u_{i}=F_{i}-F_{0}
.

Claim 3.19
.

For any
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, we can find a constant
q_{ij}
such that

\int_{-10}^{-\beta^{2}/2}\int_{M}\left|\langle\nabla u_{i},\nabla u_{j}\rangle-q_{ij}\right|\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y,\beta)\delta^{1/4}.

Since
y_{i}^{*}
are
(\delta,1)
-selfsimilar for
0\leq i\leq N
, we can obtain, similar to the proof of (
3.3
) by using Lemmas
3.3
and
3.5
, that

\displaystyle\int_{-10}^{-\beta^{2}/2}\int_{M}\left|\mathcal{T}_{i}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y,\beta)\delta^{1/2}.

(3.55)

Taking the difference of (
3.55
) and using Theorem
3.6
with
-1/30
replaced by
-\beta^{2}/2
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{-10}^{-\beta^{2}/2}\int_{M}\left|\nabla^{2}u_{i}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y,\beta)\delta^{1/2}.

(3.56)

Moreover, it follows from Proposition
2.7
, Lemma
3.5
and
[
FL25a
, Proposition A.2]
that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[-10,-\beta^{2}/2]}\int_{M}|\nabla u_{i}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq C(n,Y,\beta).

(3.57)

Similar to the proof of Lemma
3.1
by using
[
FL25a
, Proposition A.2]
, for each
0\leq i\leq N
, we obtain a smooth function
h_{i}
on
M\times[-10,-\beta^{2}/2]
such that

(i)

\displaystyle\square h_{i}=-\frac{n}{2}
.

(ii)

\displaystyle\int_{-10}^{-\beta^{2}/2}\int_{M}\left\lvert\nabla^{2}(F_{i}-h_{i})\right\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y,\beta)\delta^{1/2}
.

(iii)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[-10,-\beta^{2}/2]}\int_{M}|\nabla(F_{i}-h_{i})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq C(n,Y,\beta)\delta^{1/2}
.

We set
v_{i}:=h_{i}-h_{0}
for
1\leq i\leq N
. Then it follows from (
3.56
) and (ii) above that

\displaystyle\int_{-10}^{-\beta^{2}/2}\int_{M}\left\lvert\nabla^{2}v_{i}\right\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y,\beta)\delta^{1/2}.

(3.58)

A standard calculation yields

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}{\displaystyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}\int_{M}\langle\nabla v_{i},\nabla v_{j}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=\int_{M}\langle\nabla\square v_{i},\nabla v_{j}\rangle+\langle\nabla v_{i},\nabla\square v_{j}\rangle-2\langle\nabla^{2}v_{i},\nabla^{2}v_{j}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=\int_{M}-2\langle\nabla^{2}v_{i},\nabla^{2}v_{j}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}.

(3.59)

Plugging (
3.58
) into (
3.59
), we obtain for any
-10\leq s<t\leq-\beta^{2}/2
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{M}\langle\nabla v_{i},\nabla v_{j}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}-\int_{M}\langle\nabla v_{i},\nabla v_{j}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{s}\right\rvert\leq C(n,Y,\beta)\delta^{1/2}.

Therefore, it follows from (iii) above that we can find a constant
q_{ij}
such that for any
t\in[-10,-\beta^{2}/2]
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{M}\langle\nabla u_{i},\nabla u_{j}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}-q_{ij}\right\rvert\leq C(n,Y,\beta)\delta^{1/4}.

Thus, by the Poincaré inequality (see Theorem
2.6
), we have for any
t\in[-10,-\beta^{2}/2]
,

\displaystyle\int_{M}\left\lvert\langle\nabla u_{i},\nabla u_{j}\rangle-q_{ij}\right\rvert\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq\sqrt{10\pi}\int_{M}|\nabla u_{i}||\nabla^{2}u_{j}|+|\nabla u_{j}||\nabla^{2}u_{i}|\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}+C(n,Y,\beta)\delta^{1/4}.

By integration, we obtain from Proposition
2.7
and (
3.56
) that

\displaystyle\int_{-10}^{-\beta^{2}/2}\int_{M}\left\lvert\langle\nabla u_{i},\nabla u_{j}\rangle-q_{ij}\right\rvert\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 100\int_{-10}^{-\beta^{2}/2}\int_{M}|\nabla u_{i}||\nabla^{2}u_{j}|+|\nabla u_{j}||\nabla^{2}u_{i}|\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t+C(n,Y,\beta)\delta^{1/4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 100\left(\int_{-10}^{-\beta^{2}/2}\int_{M}|\nabla^{2}u_{j}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{-10}^{-\beta^{2}/2}\int_{M}|\nabla u_{i}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+100\left(\int_{-10}^{-\beta^{2}/2}\int_{M}|\nabla^{2}u_{i}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{-10}^{-\beta^{2}/2}\int_{M}|\nabla u_{j}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C(n,Y,\beta)\delta^{1/4}\leq C(n,Y,\beta)\delta^{1/4},

where we used (
3.57
). This proves Claim
3.19
.

Claim 3.20
.

For any
1\leq i\neq j\leq N
,

\int_{-10}^{-1/10}\int_{M}\left|\nabla(u_{i}-u_{j})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\geq 100\beta^{2}.

Fix
i\neq j
and without loss of generality, assume
\tau_{j}\leq\tau_{i}
. By Claim
3.19
, we can find
q\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{-10}^{-\beta^{2}/2}\int_{M}\left||\nabla(u_{i}-u_{j})|^{2}-q\right|\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y,\beta)\delta^{1/4}.

(3.60)

By (
3.60
) and
[
Bam20b
, Proposition 7.3]
, we can choose some
t^{*}\in[-\beta^{2},-\beta^{2}/2]
such that

\displaystyle\int_{M}\left||\nabla(\tau_{i}f_{i}-\tau_{j}f_{j})|^{2}-q\right|\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t^{*}}\leq

\displaystyle\Psi(\delta),

\displaystyle\int_{M}\left|-\tau_{i}(|\nabla f_{i}|^{2}+\mathrm{R})+f_{i}-W_{i}\right|\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{y_{i}^{*};t_{*}}\leq

\displaystyle\Psi(\delta),

\displaystyle\int_{M}\left|-\tau_{j}(|\nabla f_{j}|^{2}+\mathrm{R})+f_{j}-W_{j}\right|\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{y_{j}^{*};t_{*}}\leq

\displaystyle\Psi(\delta).

Then choose
x_{i}^{*}=(x_{i},t^{*})
and
x_{j}^{*}=(x_{j},t^{*})
to be
H_{n}
-centers of
y_{i}^{*}
and
y_{j}^{*}
, respectively. By (
3.54
), we have

\displaystyle d_{t^{*}}(x_{i}^{*},x_{j}^{*})\geq\frac{D}{2}\beta.

(3.61)

Note that in our setting, when evaluated at
t^{*}
,

\displaystyle\beta^{2}/3\leq\tau_{j}\leq\tau_{i}\leq 2\beta^{2}.

(3.62)

Set
B_{i}:=B_{t^{*}}(x_{i}^{*},2\sqrt{H_{n}}\beta)
and
S_{i}:=\{f_{i}\leq D\}\bigcap B_{i}
. It is clear from Proposition
2.5
and (
3.61
) that

\displaystyle\nu_{y_{i}^{*};t^{*}}(B_{i})\geq\frac{1}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\nu_{y_{j}^{*};t^{*}}(B_{i})\leq\frac{C(n)}{D^{2}}.

On the other hand, by
[
Bam20a
, Theorem 8.1]
, we have

\displaystyle\nu_{y_{i}^{*},t^{*}}(B_{i}\setminus S_{i})\leq\frac{1}{(4\pi\tau_{i}(t^{*}))^{\frac{n}{2}}}e^{-D}C(n,Y)\beta^{n}\leq C(n,Y)e^{-D}.

Thus, we choose
D
large so that

\displaystyle\nu_{y_{i}^{*},t^{*}}(B_{i}\setminus S_{i})\leq\frac{1}{8}.

By passing to a further subset of
S_{i}
, still denoted by
S_{i}
, we may assume on
S_{i}
,

\displaystyle\left||\nabla(\tau_{i}f_{i}-\tau_{j}f_{j})|^{2}-q\right|\leq

\displaystyle\Psi(\delta),

\displaystyle\left|-\tau_{i}(|\nabla f_{i}|^{2}+\mathrm{R})+f_{i}-W_{i}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\Psi(\delta),

\displaystyle\left|-\tau_{j}(|\nabla f_{j}|^{2}+\mathrm{R})+f_{j}-W_{j}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\Psi(\delta),

\displaystyle\nu_{y_{i}^{*};t^{*}}(B_{i}\setminus S_{i})+\nu_{y_{j}^{*};t^{*}}(B_{i}\setminus S_{i})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}.

Thus, we compute on
S_{i}
that if
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y,\beta)
,

\displaystyle\left|\tau_{i}f_{i}-\tau_{j}f_{j}-(\tau_{i}^{2}-\tau_{j}^{2})\mathrm{R}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|-\tau_{i}^{2}(|\nabla f_{i}|^{2}+\mathrm{R})+\tau_{i}f_{i}-\tau_{i}W_{i}\right|+\left|-\tau_{j}^{2}(|\nabla f_{j}|^{2}+\mathrm{R})+\tau_{j}f_{j}-\tau_{j}W_{j}\right|

\displaystyle+\left||\nabla(\tau_{i}f_{i})|^{2}-|\nabla(\tau_{j}f_{j})|^{2}\right|+|\tau_{i}W_{i}-\tau_{j}W_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,Y)\beta^{2}+\Psi(\delta)+\left\lvert\nabla(F_{j}-F_{i})\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla(F_{j}-F_{i})+2\nabla F_{i}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,Y)\beta^{2}+\Psi(\delta)+q+C(n,Y)q^{\frac{1}{2}}\beta\leq q+C(n,Y)\beta^{2}.

In particular, this implies that on
S_{i}

\displaystyle\tau_{j}f_{j}\leq\tau_{i}f_{i}+(\tau_{j}^{2}-\tau_{i}^{2})\mathrm{R}+q+C(Y,D)\beta^{2}\leq\tau_{i}f_{i}+q+C(n,Y)\beta^{2},

where in the last inequality, we have used (
3.62
) and
\mathrm{R}\geq-C(n)
. By (
3.62
) again, we obtain

\displaystyle f_{j}\leq 6f_{i}+3\beta^{-2}q+C(n,Y)\leq f_{i}+5D+3\beta^{-2}q+C(n,Y)\leq f_{i}+3\beta^{-2}q+C(n,Y).

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\leq\nu_{y_{i}^{*};t^{*}}(S_{i})=

\displaystyle\int_{S_{i}}(4\pi\tau_{i})^{-n/2}e^{-f_{i}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t^{*})}\leq e^{3\beta^{-2}q+C(n,Y)}\int_{S_{i}}(4\pi\tau_{j})^{-n/2}e^{-f_{j}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t^{*})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{3\beta^{-2}q+C(n,Y)}\nu_{y_{j}^{*};t^{*}}(S_{i})\leq e^{3\beta^{-2}q+C(n,Y)}\frac{C(n)}{D^{2}},

which implies

\displaystyle q\geq\frac{1}{3}\beta^{2}\left(2\log D-\log(4C(n))-C(n,Y)\right)\geq 101\beta^{2},

provided that
D
is sufficiently large. If
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y,\beta)
, it follows from (
3.60
) that

\displaystyle\int_{-10}^{-1/10}\int_{M}|\nabla(u_{i}-u_{j})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\geq 100\beta^{2},

(3.63)

which completes the proof of Claim
3.20
.

Combining Claims
3.19
and
3.20
, we finish the proof as follows. Consider the vector space
V_{0}
spanned by
\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{N}\}
, equipped with the inner product

(\cdot,\cdot):=\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{-10}^{-1/10}\int_{M}\langle\nabla\cdot,\nabla\cdot\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t.

Then by Proposition
2.7
and (
3.63
), we know that

\displaystyle|u_{i}|\leq C(n,Y),\quad|u_{i}-u_{j}|\geq 3\beta,\quad\forall i\neq j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}.

(3.64)

Since we cannot find
(k,\alpha,\delta,1)
-independent points from
\{y_{i}^{*}\}_{1\leq i\leq N}
at
y_{0}^{*}
, it follows from Definition
3.7
and an inductive argument that there exists a
(k-1)
-subspace
V_{1}\subset V_{0}
such that

\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{N}\}\subset B_{C(k)\alpha}(V_{1}),

where
B_{C(k)\alpha}
denotes the
C(k)\alpha
-neighborhood with respect to the inner product defined above. From this and (
3.64
), it is easy to see
N\leq C(n,Y)\alpha^{1-k}
.

In sum, the proof is complete.
∎

4
Nondegeneration of almost splitting maps on Ricci flow limit spaces

In this section, we extend the results in previous sections to Ricci flow limit spaces and prove a nondegeneration result for almost splitting maps.

Throughout this section, suppose that
(Z,d_{Z},\mathfrak{t})
is a noncollapsed Ricci flow limit space obtained as a pointed Gromov-Hausdorff limit of a sequence
\mathcal{X}^{l}\in\mathcal{M}(n,Y,T)
. We set
\mathcal{X}^{l}=\{M_{l}^{n},(g_{l}(t))_{t\in\mathbb{I}^{++}}\}
. Moreover, let
\phi_{l}
denote the diffeomorphisms given in Theorem
2.12
.

4.1
Modified pointed entropy

First, we generalize the pointed
\mathcal{W}
-entropy in (
2.1
) of Definition
2.2
to noncollapsed Ricci flow limit spaces.

Definition 4.1

(Pointed
\mathcal{W}
-entropy)
.

For
z\in Z_{\mathbb{I}^{-}}
, we define the pointed
\mathcal{W}
-entropy at
z
by

{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\mathcal{W}_{z}(\tau)$}}}\mathcal{W}_{z}(\tau):=\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}}\tau\left(2\Delta f_{z}-|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}+\mathrm{R}_{g^{Z}}\right)+f_{z}-n\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau},

for any
\tau>0
such that
\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau\in\mathbb{I}^{-}
.

Next, we define the following set, which consists of the time at which the integration by parts fails.

Definition 4.2
.

For
z\in Z_{\mathbb{I}^{-}}
, we define
J^{z}\subset(0,\mathfrak{t}(z)+0.98T)
to be the set consisting of
\tau
such that

\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}}\Delta f_{z}-|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}\neq 0.

Next, we investigate the continuity of
\mathcal{W}
under convergence.

Proposition 4.3
.

Given
z\in Z_{\mathbb{I}^{-}}
,
J^{z}
is a measure zero set. Moreover, for any sequence
z_{l}^{*}\in M_{l}\times\mathbb{I}
converging to
z
and any
\tau\in(0,\mathfrak{t}(z)+0.98T)\setminus J^{z}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{l\to\infty}\mathcal{W}_{z_{l}^{*}}\left(\mathfrak{t}_{l}(z_{l}^{*})-\mathfrak{t}(z)+\tau\right)=\mathcal{W}_{z}(\tau).

Proof.

For simplicity, we assume
\mathfrak{t}_{l}(z_{l}^{*})=\mathfrak{t}(z)=0
, and the general case is similar. We set
w_{z_{l}^{*}}=\tau\left(2\Delta f_{z_{l}^{*}}-|\nabla f_{z_{l}^{*}}|^{2}+\mathrm{R}_{g_{l}}\right)+f_{z_{l}^{*}}-n
and
w_{z}=\tau\left(2\Delta f_{z}-|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}+\mathrm{R}_{g^{Z}}\right)+f_{z}-n
. Note that by Perelman’s differential Harnack inequality and the smooth convergence in Theorem
2.12
, we have
w_{z_{l}^{*}}\leq 0
and
w_{z}\leq 0
.

By smooth convergence again, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{l\to\infty}\mathcal{W}_{z_{l}^{*}}(\tau)=\limsup_{l\to\infty}\int_{M_{l}}w_{z_{l}^{*}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z_{l}^{*};-\tau}\leq\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}w_{z}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}.

(4.1)

Indeed, for any compact set
D\subset\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}
, it follows from Theorem
2.12
that for the diffeomorphism
\phi_{l}
therein, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{l\to\infty}\int_{\phi_{l}(D)}w_{z_{l}^{*}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z_{l}^{*};-\tau}=\int_{D}w_{z}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}.

Thus, (
4.1
) holds after exhausting
\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}
by such a compact set
D
.

On the other hand, since both
\mathrm{R}_{g_{l}}
and
\mathrm{R}_{g^{Z}}
are uniformly bounded below, we conclude as (
4.1
) that

\displaystyle\liminf_{l\to\infty}\int_{M_{l}}\tau\left(|\nabla f_{z_{l}^{*}}|^{2}+\mathrm{R}_{g_{l}}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z_{l}^{*};-\tau}\geq\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}\tau\left(|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}+\mathrm{R}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}.

Combining this with the continuity of the Nash entropy (see
[
FL25a
, Lemma 7.2]
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\liminf_{l\to\infty}\mathcal{W}_{z_{l}^{*}}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\liminf_{l\to\infty}\int_{M_{l}}\tau\left(|\nabla f_{z_{l}^{*}}|^{2}+\mathrm{R}\right)+f_{z_{l}^{*}}-n\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z_{l}^{*};-\tau}\geq\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}\tau\left(|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}+\mathrm{R}\right)+f-n\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}.

(4.2)

By (
4.1
) and (
4.2
), it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}\tau\left(|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}+\mathrm{R}\right)+f-n\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}\leq\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}w_{z}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}.

(4.3)

Now, we consider the cutoff functions
\eta_{r,A}
based at
z
from
[
FL25a
, Proposition 8.20]
. For any
0<\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}<\mathfrak{t}(z)+0.98T
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}\left(\tau\big(|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}+\mathrm{R}\big)+f-n\right)(1-\eta_{r,A})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n)\left(\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathrm{supp}(\eta_{r,A})}\tau^{2}\left(|\nabla f_{z}|^{4}+\mathrm{R}^{2}\right)+f^{2}+1\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathrm{supp}(\eta_{r,A})}\left|1-\eta_{r,A}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}\setminus\mathrm{supp}(\eta_{r,A})}\left(\tau\big(|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}+\mathrm{R}\big)+f-n\right)(1-\eta_{r,A})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,Y,A,\tau_{2})r+C(n,Y,\tau_{2})\Psi(A^{-1}),

(4.4)

where we used Proposition
2.7
by taking the limit,
[
FL25a
, Proposition 8.20 (4)]
and
[
FL25b
, Proposition 3.1]
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}w_{z}(1-\eta_{r,A})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau\right|\leq C(n,Y,A,\tau_{2})r+C(n,Y,\tau_{2})\Psi(A^{-1}).

(4.5)

Using integration by parts, it follows that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}\left(\tau\big(|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}+\mathrm{R}\big)+f-n\right)\eta_{r,A}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau-\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}w_{z}\eta_{r,A}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\tau_{2}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}|\nabla f_{z}||\nabla\eta_{r,A}|\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,Y,A)r^{-1}\tau_{2}\left(\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\{0<\eta_{r,A}<1\}}1\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,Y,A,\tau_{2})r^{1/2}.

(4.6)

Combining (
4.1
), (
4.5
) and (
4.1
), we first let
r\to 0
, followed by
A\to+\infty
, to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}\tau\big(|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}+\mathrm{R}\big)+f-n\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau=\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}w_{z}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau.

(4.7)

Thus, it follows from Definition
4.2
, (
4.3
) and (
4.7
) that
J^{z}
has measure zero.

Finally, by (
4.1
) and (
4.2
), we conclude that for
\tau\in(0,\mathfrak{t}(z)+0.98T)\setminus J^{z}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{l\to\infty}\mathcal{W}_{z_{l}^{*}}(\tau)=\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}\tau\left(|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}+\mathrm{R}\right)+f-n\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}=\int_{\mathcal{R}_{-\tau}}w_{z}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;-\tau}.

This completes the proof.
∎

By Proposition
4.3
, we know that
\tau\mapsto\mathcal{W}_{z}(\tau)
is nonincreasing for
\tau\notin J^{z}
. For this reason, we make the following definition:

Definition 4.4

(Modified pointed
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
-entropy)
.

For
z\in Z_{\mathbb{I}^{-}}
, the modified
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
-entropy at
z
is defined as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}(\tau):=\begin{cases}\mathcal{W}_{z}(\tau),\quad&\text{if}\quad\tau\notin J^{z}\\
\lim_{\tau_{j}\notin J^{z}\nearrow\tau}\mathcal{W}_{z}(\tau_{j}),\quad&\text{if}\quad\tau\in J^{z}.\end{cases}

It is clear that
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}(\tau)
is nonincreasing for all
\tau>0
, and
\mathcal{W}_{z}(\tau)=\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}(\tau)
for
\tau\notin J^{z}
.

Lemma 4.5
.

For
z\in Z_{\mathbb{I}^{-}}
and
0<\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}<\mathfrak{t}(z)+0.98T
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau_{2}}^{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}2\tau\left\lvert\mathrm{Ric}(g^{Z})+\nabla^{2}f_{z}-\frac{g^{Z}}{2\tau}\right\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}(\tau_{1})-\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}(\tau_{2}),

where
\tau=\mathfrak{t}(z)-\mathfrak{t}
.

Proof.

Take two sequences
\tau_{1}^{j}\nearrow\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}^{j}\nearrow\tau_{2}
such that neither
\tau_{1}^{j}
nor
\tau_{2}^{j}
belongs to
J^{z}
. By the monotonicity formula (see Proposition
2.3
(iii)), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau^{j}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau^{j}_{1}}\int_{M_{l}}2\tau_{l}\left\lvert\mathrm{Ric}(g_{l})+\nabla^{2}f_{z_{l}^{*}}-\frac{g_{l}}{2\tau_{l}}\right\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z_{l}^{*};t}\mathrm{d}t=\mathcal{W}_{z_{l}^{*}}\left(\mathfrak{t}_{l}(z_{l}^{*})-\mathfrak{t}(z)+\tau_{1}^{j}\right)-\mathcal{W}_{z_{l}^{*}}\left(\mathfrak{t}_{l}(z_{l}^{*})-\mathfrak{t}(z)+\tau_{2}^{j}\right),

where
\tau_{l}=\mathfrak{t}_{l}(z_{l}^{*})-t
, and
z_{l}^{*}
is a sequence in
\mathcal{X}^{l}
converging to
z
. Letting
l\to\infty
, we have by the smooth convergence and Proposition
4.3
that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau^{j}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau^{j}_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}2\tau\left\lvert\mathrm{Ric}(g^{Z})+\nabla^{2}f_{z}-\frac{g^{Z}}{2\tau}\right\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t\leq\mathcal{W}_{z}(\tau_{1}^{j})-\mathcal{W}_{z}(\tau_{2}^{j}).

Taking
j\to\infty
, the conclusion follows from the definition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
.
∎

Next, we have the following definition that generalizes Definition
3.2
.

Definition 4.6

(
(\delta,r)
-selfsimilar)
.

A point
z\in Z_{\mathbb{I}^{-}}
is called
(\delta,r)
-selfsimilar
if
\mathfrak{t}(z)-\delta^{-1}r^{2}\in\mathbb{I}^{-}
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}(\delta r^{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}(\delta^{-1}r^{2})\leq\delta.

4.2
Sharp splitting maps and Hessian decay on Ricci flow limit spaces

Next, we give the definition of strongly
(k,\alpha,\delta,r)
-independent points on
Z
similar to Definition
3.7
:

Definition 4.7

(Strongly
(k,\alpha,\delta,r)
-independent points)
.

Given constants
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\delta>0
,
r>0
,
k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and a Ricci flow limit space
Z
with a
(\bar{\delta}/2,r)
-selfsimilar point
z_{0}
, a set of points
\{z_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
is called
strongly
(k,\alpha,\delta,r)
-independent
at
z_{0}
, if the following conditions hold.

(i)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z_{i}}(r^{2}/40)-\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z_{i}}(40r^{2})\leq\delta
for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,k\}
.

(ii)

\displaystyle d_{Z}(z_{i},z_{0})\leq r/2
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
.

(iii)

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{t}(z_{0})-\mathfrak{t}(z_{i})|\leq\frac{r^{2}}{200}
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
.

(iv)

Set
f_{i}=f_{z_{i}}
,
\tau_{i}=\mathfrak{t}(z)-\mathfrak{t}
and
F_{i}=\tau_{i}f_{i}
. For
h_{i}=r^{-1}(F_{i}-F_{0})
and the symmetric matrix

\displaystyle A=(a_{ij}),\quad\text{where}\quad a_{ij}:=\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{\mathfrak{t}(z_{0})-10r^{2}}^{\mathfrak{t}(z_{0})-r^{2}/10}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}}\langle\nabla h_{i},\nabla h_{j}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;t}\mathrm{d}t,

the first eigenvalue
\lambda_{1}(A)
of
A
satisfies

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(A)\geq\alpha^{2}.

With Definition
4.7
, we can now give the definition of the entropy pinching in Ricci flow limit spaces similar to Definition
3.9
.

Definition 4.8

(Strongly entropy pinching)
.

For
k\in\{1,2\ldots,n\}
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\delta>0
and
r>0
, the
strongly
(k,\alpha,\delta,r)
-entropy pinching at
z_{0}
is defined as

{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\mathfrak{S}_{r}^{k,\alpha,\delta}$}}}\mathfrak{S}_{r}^{k,\alpha,\delta}(z_{0}):=\inf_{\{z_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}\,\mathrm{are\,strongly}\,(k,\alpha,\delta,r)-\mathrm{independent}\ \mathrm{at}\ z_{0}}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z_{i}}(r^{2}/40)-\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z_{i}}(40r^{2})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Let
\{z_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
be strongly
(k,\alpha,\delta,r)
-independent points at
z_{0}
and assume that
x_{i,l}^{*}\in\mathcal{X}^{l}
converges to
z_{i}
in the Gromov-Hausdorff sense. Set
\nu^{l}=\nu_{x_{0,l}^{*}}
,
t^{l}=\mathfrak{t}_{l}(x_{0,l}^{*})
,
\tau_{i}^{l}=\mathfrak{t}_{l}(x_{i,l}^{*})-t
,
f^{l}_{i}=f_{x_{i,l}^{*}}
,
F^{l}_{i}=\tau_{i}^{l}f^{l}_{i}
and
h^{l}_{i}=r^{-1}(F^{l}_{i}-F^{l}_{0})
.

By Theorem
2.12
, we have

\displaystyle f^{l}_{i}\xrightarrow[l\to\infty]{\hskip 8.19447ptC_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}(\mathcal{R})\hskip 8.19447pt}f_{i}.

We define
A^{l}=(a_{ij}^{l})
as

\displaystyle a_{ij}^{l}:=\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{t^{l}-10r^{2}}^{t^{l}-r^{2}/10}\int_{M_{l}}\langle\nabla h^{l}_{i},\nabla h^{l}_{j}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{t}\mathrm{d}t.

Lemma 4.9
.

For any
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
,

\displaystyle a_{ij}^{l}\xrightarrow[l\to\infty]{}a_{ij},\quad\lambda_{1}(A^{l})\xrightarrow[l\to\infty]{}\lambda_{1}(A).

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
\mathfrak{t}(z_{0})=\mathfrak{t}_{l}(x_{0,l}^{*})=0
and
r=1
. According to Proposition
2.7
and
[
FL25a
, Proposition A.2]
, we obtain that for any
1\leq i\leq k
,

\displaystyle\int_{-11}^{-1/11}\int_{M_{l}}\left|\nabla h^{l}_{i}\right|^{4}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq C(n,Y).

(4.8)

We consider the family of cutoff functions
\{\eta_{r,L}\}
from
[
FL25a
, Proposition 8.20]
based at
z_{0}
. Set
B^{l}_{r,L}:=\phi_{l}\left(\mathrm{supp}\big(\eta_{r,L}\big)\cap\mathcal{R}_{[-10,-1/10]}\right)
, which is well defined for fixed
r
and
L
, provided that
l
is sufficiently large.

By the smooth convergence on
\mathcal{R}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\lim_{l\to\infty}\iint_{B^{l}_{r,L}}\langle\nabla h^{l}_{i},\nabla h^{l}_{j}\rangle\eta_{r,L}\circ\phi_{l}^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{t}\mathrm{d}t=\int_{\mathcal{R}_{[-10,-1/10]}}\langle\nabla h_{i},\nabla h_{j}\rangle\eta_{r,L}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z_{0};t}\mathrm{d}t.

(4.9)

On the one hand, it follows from
[
FL25a
, Theorem 1.5]
that once
r
and
L
are fixed,

\displaystyle B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};L/3)\cap\phi_{l}\left(\mathrm{supp}\big(\eta_{r,L}\big)\right)\subset B^{l}_{r,L}\subset B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};3L)

for sufficiently large
l
. Thus, it is clear that

\displaystyle\iint_{M_{l}\times[-10,-1/10]\setminus B_{r,L}^{l}}\left\lvert\langle\nabla h^{l}_{i},\nabla h^{l}_{j}\rangle\right\rvert\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq

\displaystyle\iint_{M_{l}\times[-10,-1/10]\setminus B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};L/3)}\left\lvert\langle\nabla h^{l}_{i},\nabla h^{l}_{j}\rangle\right\rvert\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\iint_{B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};L/3)\cap M_{l}\times[-10,-1/10]\setminus\phi_{l}\left(\mathrm{supp}(\eta_{r,L})\right)}\left\lvert\langle\nabla h^{l}_{i},\nabla h^{l}_{j}\rangle\right\rvert\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{t}\mathrm{d}t.

By Proposition
2.5
and (
4.8
), we have

\displaystyle\iint_{M_{l}\times[-10,-1/10]\setminus B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};L/3)}\left\lvert\langle\nabla h^{l}_{i},\nabla h^{l}_{j}\rangle\right\rvert\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\iint_{M_{l}\times[-10,-1/10]\setminus B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};L/3)}|\nabla h^{l}_{i}|^{2}|\nabla h^{l}_{j}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\iint_{M_{l}\times[-10,-1/10]\setminus B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};L/3)}1\,\mathrm{d}\nu^{l}_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,Y)\Psi(L^{-1}).

(4.10)

By
[
FL25a
, Proposition 8.20 (2)]
, we know that any
x\in B^{*}(x_{0,l}^{*};L/3)\setminus\phi_{l}\left(\mathrm{supp}(\eta_{r,L})\right)
satisfies
