q_{c^{1}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\zeta,\gamma}(x_{c^{1}})\cap B^{*}(x_{c^{1}},\gamma)
which is
(k,\epsilon,\gamma)
-quotient cylindrical regarding
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{m}_{k}(\Gamma)
with respect to
\mathcal{L}_{q_{c^{1}},\gamma}
. Notice that the model space is the same
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{m}_{k}(\Gamma)
by Proposition
5.28
and (
6.10
).

We define

\displaystyle L^{2}:=B^{*}(z,2)\bigcap\bigcup_{c^{1}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{q_{c^{1}},\gamma}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{c^{1}},2\gamma)\right)\setminus\left(\bigcup_{b^{1}}B^{*}(x_{b^{1}},2\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{1}}B^{*}(x_{d^{1}},2\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{1}}B^{*}(x_{e^{1}},2\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma)\right).

Choose a maximal
2\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma^{2}
-separated set
\{x_{f^{2}}\}\subset L^{2}
. After doing this process for each
c
-ball
B^{*}(x_{c^{1}},\gamma)
, we can re-index the above balls by
b,c,d
-balls and define

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{2}:=B^{*}(z,2)\setminus\left(\bigcup_{c^{2}}B^{*}(x_{c^{2}},\gamma^{2})\bigcup\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq 2}\left(\bigcup_{b^{j}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{j}}B^{*}(x_{d^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{j}}B^{*}(x_{e^{j}},\gamma^{j})\right)\right).

Moreover, we define
\mathscr{C}^{2}:=\{x_{b^{1}},x_{b^{2}},x_{d^{1}},x_{d^{2}},x_{e^{1}},x_{e^{2}},x_{c^{2}}\}
, with the radius function chosen to be the corresponding radii of the associated balls. Then, one can check as in Claim
6.14
and the proof of Proposition
5.11
that
\mathscr{N}^{2}
is a
(k,\delta,\mathfrak{c},1)
-quotient cylindrical neck region regarding
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{m}_{k}(\Gamma)
.

Repeating the above decomposition for
l
steps, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{l}:=B^{*}(z,2)\setminus\left(\bigcup_{c^{l}}B^{*}(x_{c^{l}},\gamma^{l})\bigcup\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq l}\left(\bigcup_{b^{j}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{j}}B^{*}(x_{d^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{j}}B^{*}(x_{e^{j}},\gamma^{j})\right)\right).

It follows as before that
\mathscr{N}^{l}
is a
(k,\delta,\mathfrak{c},1)
-quotient cylindrical neck region regarding
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{m}_{k}(\Gamma)
.

Set
\mathcal{G}^{l}:=\bigcup_{c^{l}}\{x_{c^{l}}\}
. It is clear from our construction that
\mathcal{G}^{l+1}\subset B^{*}_{2\gamma^{l}}(\mathcal{G}^{l})
. Then we denote the Hausdorff limit of
\mathcal{G}^{l}
by
\mathscr{C}_{0}
and define

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}:=B^{*}(z,2)\setminus\left(\mathscr{C}_{0}\bigcup\bigcup_{1\leq j<\infty}\left(\bigcup_{b^{j}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{j}}B^{*}(x_{d^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{j}}B^{*}(x_{e^{j}},\gamma^{j})\right)\right).

From our construction,
\mathscr{N}
is a
(k,\delta,\mathfrak{c},1)
-quotient cylindrical neck region regarding
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{m}_{k}(\Gamma)
, with centers given by
\mathscr{C}_{0}
together with the centers of the associated balls, and the radius function chosen to be the corresponding radii of those balls.

We rewrite the above decomposition as:

B^{*}(z,2)\subset\left(\mathscr{C}_{0}\bigcup\mathscr{N}\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup\bigcup_{d}B^{*}(x_{d},r_{d})\bigcup\bigcup_{e}B^{*}(x_{e},r_{e}).

The quotient cylindrical neck region
\mathscr{N}
we construct satisfies Ahlfors regularity with a constant
C(Y,\mathfrak{c})>1
, in the same sense as Theorem
5.21
and Proposition
5.31
. This follows directly from the construction of
\mathscr{N}
and the argument used in the proof of Theorem
5.21
.

Thus, the following content estimate holds:

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{k}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0}\bigcap B^{*}(z,3/2)\right)+\sum_{x_{b}\in B^{*}(z,3/2)}r_{b}^{k}+\sum_{x_{d}\in B^{*}(z,3/2)}r_{d}^{k}+\sum_{x_{e}\in B^{*}(z,3/2)}r_{e}^{k}\leq C(Y,\mathfrak{c}),

which completes the proof of the type-I
c
-ball decomposition.
∎

For type-II
c
-balls, we consider the cases
k=1
and
k=2
separately.

Proposition 6.15

(Decomposition of type-II
c
-balls:
k=2
)
.

For
z\in B^{*}(z_{0},4r_{0})
and
s\leq r_{0}-d_{Z}(z,z_{0})/4
, let
B^{*}(z,s)
be a type-II
c
-ball with
k=2
. For any constants
\delta>0
,
\eta>0
,
\mathfrak{c}\in(0,10^{-40})
and
\beta>0
, if
\mathfrak{c}\leq\mathfrak{c}(Y)
and
\zeta\leq\zeta(Y,\delta,\eta,\mathfrak{c},\beta)
, then we have the decomposition

B^{*}(z,2s)\subset\left(\mathscr{C}_{0}\bigcup\mathscr{N}^{\prime}\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup\bigcup_{d}B^{*}(x_{d},r_{d})\bigcup\bigcup_{e}B^{*}(x_{e},r_{e})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{\prime}}B^{*}(x_{e^{\prime}},r_{e^{\prime}})

satisfying

(i)

for each
b
,
B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})
is a
b
-ball with
r_{b}\leq\mathfrak{c}^{5}s;

(ii)

for each
d
,
B^{*}(x_{d},r_{d})
is a
d
-ball with
r_{d}\leq\mathfrak{c}^{5}s;

(iii)

for any
e
and
e^{\prime}
, the balls
B^{*}(x_{e},r_{e})
and
B^{*}(x_{e^{\prime}},r_{e^{\prime}})
are
e
-balls with
\max\{r_{e},r_{e^{\prime}}\}\leq\mathfrak{c}^{5}s;

(iv)

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}:=B^{*}(z,2s)\setminus\left(\mathscr{C}_{0}\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup\bigcup_{d}B^{*}(x_{d},r_{d})\bigcup\bigcup_{e}B^{*}(x_{e},r_{e})\right)
is a
(\delta,\mathfrak{c},s)
-flat neck region regarding some
\mathcal{F}^{0}(\Gamma);

(v)

any point in
\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{\prime}:=\mathscr{N}\setminus\left(\bigcup_{e^{\prime}}B^{*}(x_{e^{\prime}},r_{e^{\prime}})\right)
is
(3,\delta,\delta d_{Z}(x,\mathscr{C}))
-symmetric
;

(vi)

the following content estimate holds:

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{2}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0}\bigcap B^{*}(z,3s/2)\right)+\sum_{x_{b}\in B^{*}(z,3s/2)}r_{b}^{2}+\sum_{x_{d}\in B^{*}(z,3s/2)}r_{d}^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{x_{e}\in B^{*}(z,3s/2)}r_{e}^{2}+\sum_{x_{e^{\prime}}\in B^{*}(z,3s/2)}r_{e^{\prime}}^{2}\leq C(Y,\mathfrak{c})s^{2}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
s=1
. In the proof, we choose a small parameter
\epsilon\ll\delta
and assume that Lemma
6.12
holds for constants
\epsilon
,
\eta
,
\beta
and
\zeta
by default. Moreover, we may assume
\delta\ll\mathfrak{c}
and define
\gamma:=\mathfrak{c}^{5}
.

Since
B^{*}(z,1)
is a type-II
c
-ball, the time-function
t_{s}(z,1)
(see (
6.8
)) is well defined, and (
6.9
) holds. For simplicity, we call a type-II
c
-ball
B^{*}(x,r)

static
if
t_{s}(x,r)\geq\mathfrak{t}(x)+(2.5r)^{2}
and
quasi-static
if
t_{s}(x,r)<\mathfrak{t}(x)+(2.5r)^{2}
.

By the definition of
t_{s}
, there exists
q\in\mathcal{P}_{\zeta,1}(z)\cap B^{*}(z,1)
such that
q
is
(\epsilon,1)
-close to
\mathcal{F}^{a}(\Gamma)
with respect to
\mathcal{L}_{q,1}
, where
a=t_{s}(z,1)-\mathfrak{t}(q)
. We define
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{z,1}\subset\mathcal{L}_{q,1}
as follows.

•

If
t_{p}(z,1)\geq\sup\{\mathfrak{t}(y)\mid y\in B^{*}(z,2.3)\}-\xi
, we set

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{z,1}:=\mathcal{L}_{q,1}\bigcap B^{*}(z,2.2).

(6.11)

•

If
t_{p}(z,1)<\sup\{\mathfrak{t}(y)\mid y\in B^{*}(z,2.3)\}-\xi
, we set

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{z,1}:=\mathcal{L}_{q,1}\bigcap B^{*}(z,2.3).

(6.12)

Here,
\xi=\xi(Y)\in(0,10^{-4})
is a small constant to be determined.

We set
L^{1}:=\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{z,1}
and choose a maximal
2\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma
-separated set
\{x_{f^{1}}\}\subset L^{1}
. In particular,
\{B^{*}(x_{f^{1}},\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma)\}
are pairwise disjoint and

\displaystyle L^{1}\subset\bigcup_{f^{1}}B^{*}(x_{f^{1}},2\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma).

According to the type of
B^{*}(x_{f^{1}},\gamma)
, we can write

\displaystyle L^{1}\subset\bigcup_{b^{1}}B^{*}(x_{b^{1}},\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{c^{1}}B^{*}(x_{c^{1}},\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{1}}B^{*}(x_{d^{1}},\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{1}}B^{*}(x_{e^{1}},\gamma).

Arguing as in the proof of Proposition
6.13
, we conclude that each
c
-ball
B^{*}(x_{c^{1}},\gamma)
is of type-II with the same group
\Gamma
. We can reindex the
c
-balls
\bigcup_{c^{1}}B^{*}(x_{c^{1}},\gamma)
by

\displaystyle\bigcup_{c^{1,s}}B^{*}(x_{c^{1,s}},\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{c^{1,q}}B^{*}(x_{c^{1,q}},\gamma)

based on whether they are static or quasi-static.

By a limiting argument, we conclude that if
\mathfrak{t}(x_{c^{1}})\leq t_{p}(z,1)-9\gamma^{2}
, then
B^{*}(x_{c^{1}},\gamma)
is a static
c
-ball, provided that
\zeta\leq\zeta(Y,\epsilon,\eta,\mathfrak{c},\beta)
. Moreover, by the definition of
t_{p}
, any ball
B^{*}(x_{f^{1}},\gamma)
with
\mathfrak{t}(x_{f^{1}})\geq t_{p}(z,1)+9\gamma^{2}
must be an
e
-ball. Consequently, any quasi-static
c
-ball
B^{*}(x_{c^{1,q}},\gamma)
must satisfy

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{t}(x_{c^{1,q}})-t_{p}(z,1)|\leq 9\gamma^{2}.

Thus, the number of
\{c^{1,q}\}
is at most
C(Y)\mathfrak{c}^{-4}
.

Furthermore, by a limiting argument and using pseudolocality theorem, if
\epsilon\leq\epsilon(Y,\delta,\mathfrak{c})
and
\zeta\leq\zeta(Y,\epsilon,\eta,\mathfrak{c},\beta)
, then we can choose a small constant
\xi=\xi(Y)>0
such that

\displaystyle y\in Z_{(-\infty,t_{s}(z,1)+2\xi^{2}\gamma^{2})}\bigcap B^{*}(z,2)

with
d_{Z}(y,\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{z,1})=s\geq\gamma
is
(3,\delta,\delta s)
-symmetric for some constant
c(Y)>0
.

Next, we choose a cover
\{B^{*}(x_{e^{(1)}},\xi\gamma)\}
of

\displaystyle\left(Z_{(t_{s}(z,1)+2\xi^{2}\gamma^{2},\infty)}\setminus\bigcup_{f^{1}}B^{*}(x_{f^{1}},\gamma)\right)\bigcap B^{*}(z,2)

such that
\{B^{*}(x_{e^{(1)}},\xi\gamma/2)\}
are pairwise disjoint. Note that if
B^{*}(z,1)
itself is static, then the above set is empty. By (
6.9
), all these balls are
e
-balls, and the total number of
\{B^{*}(x_{e^{(1)}},\xi\gamma)\}
is, by Proposition
2.25
, bounded by
C(Y)\gamma^{-6}
.

Now we set

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{1}:=B^{*}(z,2)\setminus\left(\bigcup_{b^{1}}B^{*}(x_{b^{1}},\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{c^{1}}B^{*}(x_{c^{1}},\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{1}}B^{*}(x_{d^{1}},\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{1}}B^{*}(x_{e^{1}},\gamma)\right),

and

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{1,\prime}:=\mathscr{N}^{1}\setminus\left(\bigcup_{e^{(1)}}B^{*}(x_{e^{(1)}},\xi\gamma)\right).

Moreover, we define
\mathscr{C}^{1}:=\{x_{b^{1}},x_{c^{1}},x_{d^{1}},x_{e^{1}}\}
with radius
r_{x}:=\gamma
.

Claim 6.16
.

If
\epsilon\leq\epsilon(Y,\delta,\mathfrak{c})
and
\zeta\leq\zeta(Y,\epsilon,\eta,\mathfrak{c},\beta)
, then
\mathscr{N}^{1}
is a
(\delta,\mathfrak{c},1)
-flat neck region regarding
\mathcal{F}^{0}(\Gamma)
. Furthermore, any point in
\mathscr{N}^{1,\prime}
is
(3,\delta,\delta d_{Z}(x,\mathscr{C}^{1}))
-symmetric.

Indeed, property
(n1)
follows from the construction. For
(n2)
, since
q\in\mathcal{P}_{\zeta,1}(z)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{q}(\tau)-\bar{W}\right|\leq\delta^{4},\quad\forall\tau\in\left[(\delta\gamma)^{2},\delta^{-2}\right],

if
\zeta
is sufficiently small. Moreover, it follows from Proposition
6.8
(i) that for any
x\in\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{z,1}
,

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{x}(\tau)-\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{q}(\tau)\right|\leq\delta^{4},\quad\forall\tau\in\left[(\delta\gamma)^{2},\delta^{-2}\right].

Combining the above two inequalities, we have for any
x\in\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{z,1}
,

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{x}(\tau)-\bar{W}\right|\leq\delta^{3},\quad\forall\tau\in\left[(\delta\gamma)^{2},\delta^{-2}\right],

which establish
(n2)
.
(n3)
and
(n4)
follow from our construction and Proposition
6.8
(iii). Finally, the last conclusion follows from our construction. This completes the proof of Claim
6.16
.

For each
c
-ball
B^{*}(x_{c^{1}},\gamma)
, we can define the set
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{x_{c^{1}},\gamma}
as in (
6.11
) and (
6.12
). Then, we define

\displaystyle L^{2}:=\bigcup_{c^{1}}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{x_{c^{1}},\gamma}\setminus\left(\bigcup_{b^{1}}B^{*}(x_{b^{1}},2\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{1}}B^{*}(x_{d^{1}},2\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{1}}B^{*}(x_{e^{1}},2\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma)\right)

and choose a maximal
2\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma^{2}
-separated set
\{x_{f^{2}}\}\subset L^{2}
. In particular,
\{B^{*}(x_{f^{2}},\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma^{2})\}
are pairwise disjoint and

\displaystyle L^{2}\subset\bigcup_{f^{2}}B^{*}(x_{f^{2}},2\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma^{2}).

Next, we choose a cover
\{B^{*}(x_{e^{(2)}},\xi\gamma^{2})\}
of

\displaystyle\bigcup_{c^{1,q}}\left(Z_{\big(t_{s}(x_{c^{1,q}},\gamma)+2\xi^{2}\gamma^{4},\infty\big)}\setminus\bigcup_{f^{2}}B^{*}(x_{f^{2}},\gamma^{2})\right)\bigcap B^{*}(x_{c^{1,q}},2\gamma),

such that
\{B^{*}(x_{e^{(2)}},\xi\gamma^{2}/2)\}
are pairwise disjoint. Note that each
B^{*}(x_{e^{(2)}},\xi\gamma^{2})
is an
e
-ball by definition and (
6.9
).

We reindex all
f^{2}
-balls according to their types:

\displaystyle B^{*}(x_{b^{2}},\gamma^{2}),\,B^{*}(x_{c^{2}},\gamma^{2}),\,B^{*}(x_{d^{2}},\gamma^{2}),\,B^{*}(x_{e^{2}},\gamma^{2}).

Moreover, we can reindex the
c
-balls
\{B^{*}(x_{c^{2}},\gamma^{2})\}
by

\displaystyle\bigcup_{c^{2,s}}B^{*}(x_{c^{2,s}},\gamma^{2})\bigcup\bigcup_{c^{2,q}}B^{*}(x_{c^{2,q}},\gamma^{2})

based on whether they are static or quasi-static.

Now, we set

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{2}:=B^{*}(z,2)\setminus\left(\bigcup_{c^{2}}B^{*}(x_{c^{2}},\gamma^{2})\bigcup\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq 2}\left(\bigcup_{b^{j}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{j}}B^{*}(x_{d^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{j}}B^{*}(x_{e^{j}},\gamma^{j})\right)\right),

with
\mathscr{C}^{2}:=\{x_{b^{1}},x_{b^{2}},x_{d^{1}},x_{d^{2}},x_{e^{1}},x_{e^{2}},x_{c^{2}}\}
and the radius function chosen to be the corresponding radii of the associated balls. Moreover, we set

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{2,\prime}:=\mathscr{N}^{2}\setminus\left(\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq 2}\bigcup_{e^{(j)}}B^{*}(x_{e^{(j)}},\xi\gamma^{j})\right).

Arguing as in Claim
6.16
, we know that
\mathscr{N}^{2}
is a
(\delta,\mathfrak{c},1)
-flat neck region regarding
\mathcal{F}^{0}(\Gamma)
. Furthermore, any point in
\mathscr{N}^{2,\prime}
is
(3,\delta,\delta d_{Z}(x,\mathscr{C}^{2}))
-symmetric.

For all
c
-balls
B^{*}(x_{c^{2}},\gamma^{2})
, we can repeat the above decomposition process. After
l
steps, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{l}:=B^{*}(z,2)\setminus\left(\bigcup_{c^{l}}B^{*}(x_{c^{l}},\gamma^{l})\bigcup\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq l}\left(\bigcup_{b^{j}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{j}}B^{*}(x_{d^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{j}}B^{*}(x_{e^{j}},\gamma^{j})\right)\right),

and corresponding
\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq l}\bigcup_{e^{(j)}}B^{*}(x_{e^{(j)}},\xi\gamma^{j})
. It follows as before that
\mathscr{N}^{l}
is a
(\delta,\mathfrak{c},1)
-flat neck region regarding
\mathcal{F}^{0}(\Gamma)
with center
\mathscr{C}^{l}
consisting of the centers of the balls and the radius function chosen to be the corresponding radii of the associated balls. Moreover, we define

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{l,\prime}:=\mathscr{N}^{l}\setminus\left(\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq l}\bigcup_{e^{(j)}}B^{*}(x_{e^{(j)}},\xi\gamma^{j})\right).

Then each point in
\mathscr{N}^{l,\prime}
is
(3,\delta,\delta d_{Z}(x,\mathscr{C}^{l}))
-symmetric. As before, we can reindex the
c
-balls
\bigcup_{c^{l}}B^{*}(x_{c^{l}},\gamma^{l})
by

\bigcup_{c^{l,s}}B^{*}(x_{c^{l,s}},\gamma^{l})\bigcup\bigcup_{c^{l,q}}B^{*}(x_{c^{l,q}},\gamma^{l})

based on whether they are static or quasi-static.

Next, we carry out the content estimates for all these balls up to the
l
-th step. For simplicity, we use
r_{b^{j}},\,r_{c^{j}},\,r_{d^{j}},\,r_{e^{j}},\,r_{e^{(j)}}
to denote the corresponding radii of these balls. Moreover, we call a
c
-ball of the form
B^{*}(x_{c^{j}},\gamma^{j})
a
c^{j}
-ball
. Similarly,
B^{*}(x_{c^{j,s}},\gamma^{j})
and
B^{*}(x_{c^{j,q}},\gamma^{j})
are referred to as a
c^{j,s}
-ball
and a
c^{j,q}
-ball
, respectively. The notations
b^{j}
-ball,
d^{j}
-ball,
e^{j}
-ball and
e^{(j)}
-ball are defined in the same way. Moreover, we say that a
c^{j}
-ball
produces
a
b^{j+1}
-ball,
c^{j+1}
-ball,
d^{j+1}
-ball,
e^{j+1}
-ball, or
e^{(j+1)}
-ball if such a ball arises in the decomposition of the
c^{j}
-ball.

From our construction, we obtain several facts that will be used below:

•

Only a
c^{j,q}
-ball can produce
e^{(j+1)}
-balls. In this case, the total number of
e^{(j+1)}
-balls produced by a
c^{j,q}
-ball is at most
C(Y)\gamma^{-6}
.

•

Each
c^{j}
-ball can produce at most
C(Y)\mathfrak{c}^{-4}\gamma^{-2}

c^{j+1,s}
-balls.

•

Each
c^{j}
-ball can produce at most
C(Y)\mathfrak{c}^{-4}

c^{j+1,q}
-balls.

•

If a
c^{j,s}
-ball can produce a
c^{j+1,q}
-ball, then
t_{p}(x_{c^{j,s}},\gamma^{j})-\sup\{\mathfrak{t}(y)\mid y\in B^{*}(x_{c^{j,s}},2.3\gamma^{j})\}<-\xi\gamma^{2j}
.

For each
e^{(l)}
-ball, we can assign an
(l-1)
-tuple
(a_{0},\ldots,a_{l-2})
with
a_{i}\in\{0,1\}
as follows. We know that the
e^{(l)}
-ball is produced by some
c^{l-1,q}
-ball. If this
c^{l-1,q}
-ball is produced by a
c^{l-2,s}
-ball, we set
a_{l-2}=1
. Otherwise, if it is produced by a
c^{l-2,q}
-ball, we set
a_{l-2}=0
.

In general, for any
1\leq k\leq l-2
, if the
c^{k}
-ball in the chain is produced by a
c^{k-1,s}
-ball, we set
a_{k-1}=1
; if it is produced by a
c^{k-1,q}
-ball, we set
a_{k-1}=0
.

For any
e^{(l)}
-ball, let
S_{e^{(l)}}
denote the largest number
k\in[0,l-2]
such that
a_{k}=1
. If no such
k
exists, we set
S_{e^{(l)}}=-1
. Then it is clear that the number of
e^{(l)}
-balls such that
S_{e^{(l)}}=k
is at most

\displaystyle N_{q}^{k,s}\cdot(C(Y)\mathfrak{c}^{-4})^{l-1-k}\cdot C(Y)\gamma^{-6}\leq N_{q}^{k,s}(C(Y))^{l-k}\mathfrak{c}^{-4(l-1-k)}\gamma^{-6},

where
N_{q}^{k,s}
is the total number of
c^{k,s}
-balls that can produce
c^{k+1,q}
-balls. Here, we set
N^{-1,s}_{q}=1
.

Therefore, we have the following content estimate:

\displaystyle\sum_{e^{(l)}}r_{e^{(l)}}^{2}=\sum_{-1\leq k\leq l-2}\sum_{S_{e^{(l)}}=k}r_{e^{(l)}}^{2}\leq\sum_{-1\leq k\leq l-2}N_{q}^{k,s}(C(Y))^{l-k}\gamma^{2l-6-4(l-k-1)/5},

(6.13)

where we used the fact that
\mathfrak{c}=\gamma^{1/5}
.

On the other hand, we claim that for each
c^{k,s}
-ball
B^{*}(x_{c^{k,s}},\gamma^{k})
that can produce a
c^{k+1,q}
-ball, there must exist a ball of the form

\displaystyle B^{*}(x_{b^{k}},\gamma^{k}),\,B^{*}(x_{d^{k}},\gamma^{k}),\,B^{*}(x_{e^{k}},\gamma^{k}),\,\text{ or }B^{*}(x_{e^{k+1}},\gamma^{k+1})

contained in
B^{*}(x_{c^{k,s}},10\gamma^{k})
. Indeed, since
B^{*}(x_{c^{k,s}},\gamma^{k})
can produce a
c^{k+1,q}
-ball, we know
t_{p}(x_{c^{k,s}},\gamma^{k})-\sup\{\mathfrak{t}(y)\mid y\in B^{*}(x_{c^{k,s}},2.3\gamma^{k})\}<-\xi\gamma^{2k}
. If no ball of the form

\displaystyle B^{*}(x_{b^{k}},\gamma^{k}),\,B^{*}(x_{d^{k}},\gamma^{k}),\,B^{*}(x_{e^{k}},\gamma^{k})

lies inside
B^{*}(x_{c^{k,s}},10\gamma^{k})
, then by (
6.12
), we can find an
e^{k+1}
-ball with its center on
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{x_{c^{k,s}},\gamma^{k}}
, provided
\mathfrak{c}\leq\mathfrak{c}(Y)
. This implies such an
e^{k+1}
-ball is contained in
B^{*}(x_{c^{k,s}},10\gamma^{k})
. This establishes the claim.

For each
c^{k,s}
-ball that can produce a
c^{k+1,q}
-ball, we assign to it a
b^{k}
-,
d^{k}
-,
e^{k}
- or
e^{k+1}
-ball as described in the claim above. This assignment defines a map:

\displaystyle\iota_{k}:\left\{1,\ldots,N^{k,s}_{q}\right\}\longrightarrow\left\{b^{k},d^{k},e^{k},e^{k+1}\right\}.

By Proposition
2.25
and condition
(n1)
of Definition
6.5
, each element in the image of
\iota_{k}
has at most
C(Y,\gamma)
preimages.

Consequently, we obtain that for
k\in\{0,1,\ldots,l-2\}
,

\displaystyle N_{q}^{k,s}\leq C(Y,\gamma)\left(\gamma^{-2k}\left(\sum_{b^{k}}r_{b^{k}}^{2}+\sum_{d^{k}}r_{d^{k}}^{2}+\sum_{e^{k}}r_{e^{k}}^{2}\right)+\gamma^{-2k-2}\sum_{e^{k+1}}r_{e^{k+1}}^{2}\right),

(6.14)

where
\{x_{b^{0}},x_{d^{0}},x_{e^{0}}\}
is empty.

Note that

\displaystyle\sum_{S_{e^{(l)}}=-1}r_{e^{(l)}}^{2}\leq(C(Y)\mathfrak{c}^{-4})^{l}\cdot C(Y)\gamma^{-6}\cdot\gamma^{2l}\leq C(Y,\gamma)(C(Y)\gamma^{6/5})^{l}.

Combining this with (
6.13
) and (
6.14
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{e^{(l)}}r_{e^{(l)}}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle C(Y,\gamma)(C(Y)\gamma^{6/5})^{l}

\displaystyle+C(Y,\gamma)\sum_{0\leq k\leq l-2}(C(Y)\gamma^{2})^{l-k}\gamma^{-4(l-k-1)/5}\left(\gamma^{-6}\left(\sum_{b^{k}}r_{b^{k}}^{2}+\sum_{d^{k}}r_{d^{k}}^{2}+\sum_{e^{k}}r_{e^{k}}^{2}\right)+\gamma^{-8}\sum_{e^{k+1}}r_{e^{k+1}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(Y,\gamma)\sum_{0\leq k\leq l-1}(C(Y)\gamma^{6/5})^{l-k}\left(\sum_{b^{k}}r_{b^{k}}^{2}+\sum_{d^{k}}r_{d^{k}}^{2}+\sum_{e^{k}}r_{e^{k}}^{2}\right)+C(Y,\gamma)(C(Y)\gamma^{6/5})^{l}.

(6.15)

Set
\mathcal{G}^{l}:=\bigcup_{c^{l}}\{x_{c^{l}}\}
. It is clear from our construction that
\mathcal{G}^{l+1}\subset B^{*}_{2\gamma^{l}}(\mathcal{G}^{l})
. Then we denote the Hausdorff limit of
\mathcal{G}^{l}
by
\mathscr{C}_{0}
. We define

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}:=B^{*}(z,2)\setminus\left(\mathscr{C}_{0}\bigcup\bigcup_{1\leq j<\infty}\left(\bigcup_{b^{j}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{j}}B^{*}(x_{d^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{j}}B^{*}(x_{e^{j}},\gamma^{j})\right)\right).

From our construction,
\mathscr{N}
is a
(\delta,\mathfrak{c},1)
-flat neck region regarding
\mathcal{F}^{0}(\Gamma)
with centers given by
\mathscr{C}_{0}
together with the centers of the associated balls, and the radius function chosen to be the corresponding radii of those balls. Moreover, we define

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{\prime}:=\mathscr{N}\setminus\left(\bigcup_{1\leq j<\infty}\bigcup_{e^{(j)}}B^{*}(x_{e^{(j)}},\xi\gamma^{j})\right).

Note that each point in
\mathscr{N}^{\prime}
is
(3,\delta,\delta d_{Z}(x,\mathscr{C}))
-symmetric.

We rewrite the above decomposition as:

B^{*}(z,2)\subset\left(\mathscr{C}_{0}\bigcup\mathscr{N}^{\prime}\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup\bigcup_{d}B^{*}(x_{d},r_{d})\bigcup\bigcup_{e}B^{*}(x_{e},r_{e})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{\prime}}B^{*}(x_{e^{\prime}},r_{e^{\prime}}),

where
\bigcup_{e^{\prime}}B^{*}(x_{e^{\prime}},r_{e^{\prime}}):=\bigcup_{1\leq j<\infty}\bigcup_{e^{(j)}}B^{*}(x_{e^{(j)}},\xi\gamma^{j})
.

By Proposition
6.7
, the following content estimate holds:

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{2}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0}\bigcap B^{*}(z,1.6)\right)+\sum_{x_{b}\in B^{*}(z,1.6)}r_{b}^{2}+\sum_{x_{d}\in B^{*}(z,1.6)}r_{d}^{2}+\sum_{x_{e}\in B^{*}(z,1.6)}r_{e}^{2}\leq C(Y,\mathfrak{c}).

(6.16)

Moreover, by (
6.3
) and (
6.16
), the following content estimate holds:

\displaystyle\sum_{x_{e^{\prime}}\in B^{*}(z,1.5)}r_{e^{\prime}}^{2}=\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\sum_{e^{(l)}}r_{e^{(l)}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(Y,\gamma)\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\sum_{0\leq k\leq l}(C(Y)\gamma^{6/5})^{l-k}\left(\sum_{x_{b^{k}}\in B^{*}(z,1.6)}r_{b^{k}}^{2}+\sum_{x_{d^{k}}\in B^{*}(z,1.6)}r_{d^{k}}^{2}+\sum_{x_{e^{k}}\in B^{*}(z,1.6)}r_{e^{k}}^{2}\right)+C(Y,\gamma)\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(C(Y)\gamma^{6/5})^{l}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(Y,\gamma)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k\leq l<\infty}(C(Y)\gamma^{6/5})^{l-k}\left(\sum_{x_{b^{k}}\in B^{*}(z,1.6)}r_{b^{k}}^{2}+\sum_{x_{d^{k}}\in B^{*}(z,1.6)}r_{d^{k}}^{2}+\sum_{x_{e^{k}}\in B^{*}(z,1.6)}r_{e^{k}}^{2}\right)+C(Y,\gamma)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(Y,\gamma)\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{x_{b^{k}}\in B^{*}(z,1.6)}r_{b^{k}}^{2}+\sum_{x_{d^{k}}\in B^{*}(z,1.6)}r_{d^{k}}^{2}+\sum_{x_{e^{k}}\in B^{*}(z,1.6)}r_{e^{k}}^{2}\right)+C(Y,\gamma)\leq C(Y,\gamma)=C(Y,\mathfrak{c}).

Here, we have chosen
\mathfrak{c}\leq\mathfrak{c}(Y)
such that
C(Y)\gamma^{6/5}\leq 1/2
.
This completes the proof of the decomposition.
∎

Proposition 6.17

(Decomposition of type-II
c
-balls:
k=1
)
.

For
z\in B^{*}(z_{0},4r_{0})
and
s\leq r_{0}-d_{Z}(z,z_{0})/4
, let
B^{*}(z,s)
be a type-II
c
-ball with
k=1
. For any constants
\delta>0
,
\eta>0
,
\mathfrak{c}\in(0,10^{-40})
and
\beta>0
, if
\mathfrak{c}\leq\mathfrak{c}(Y,\eta)
and
\zeta\leq\zeta(Y,\delta,\eta,\mathfrak{c},\beta)
, then we have the decomposition

B^{*}(z,2s)\subset\left(\mathscr{C}_{0}\bigcup\mathscr{N}^{\prime}\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup\bigcup_{d}B^{*}(x_{d},r_{d})\bigcup\bigcup_{e}B^{*}(x_{e},r_{e})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{\prime}}B^{*}(x_{e^{\prime}},r_{e^{\prime}})

satisfying

(i)

for each
b
,
B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})
is a
b
-ball with
r_{b}\leq\mathfrak{c}^{5}s;

(ii)

for each
d
,
B^{*}(x_{d},r_{d})
is a
d
-ball with
r_{d}\leq\mathfrak{c}^{5}s;

(iii)

for any
e
and
e^{\prime}
, the balls
B^{*}(x_{e},r_{e})
and
B^{*}(x_{e^{\prime}},r_{e^{\prime}})
are
e
-balls with
\max\{r_{e},r_{e^{\prime}}\}\leq\mathfrak{c}^{5}s;

(iv)

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}:=B^{*}(z,2s)\setminus\left(\mathscr{C}_{0}\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup\bigcup_{b^{\prime}}B^{*}(x_{b^{\prime}},r_{b^{\prime}})\bigcup\bigcup_{d}B^{*}(x_{d},r_{d})\bigcup\bigcup_{e}B^{*}(x_{e},r_{e})\right)
is a
(\delta,\mathfrak{c},s)
-flat neck region regarding some
\mathcal{F}^{0}(\Gamma)
and for each
b^{\prime}
,
B^{*}(x_{b^{\prime}},r_{b^{\prime}})
is a
b
-ball with
r_{b^{\prime}}\leq\mathfrak{c}^{5}s;

(v)

any point in
\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{\prime}:=\mathscr{N}\bigcup\bigcup_{b^{\prime}}B^{*}(x_{b^{\prime}},r_{b^{\prime}})\setminus\left(\bigcup_{e^{\prime}}B^{*}(x_{e^{\prime}},r_{e^{\prime}})\right)
is
(2,\delta,\delta d_{Z}(x,\mathscr{C}^{\prime}))
-symmetric, where

\mathscr{C}^{\prime}:=\mathscr{C}_{0}\bigcup_{b,d,e}\{x_{b},x_{d},x_{e}\};

(vi)

the following content estimates hold
:

\displaystyle\sum_{x_{b}\in B^{*}(z,3s/2)}r_{b}+\sum_{x_{d}\in B^{*}(z,3s/2)}r_{d}+\sum_{x_{e}\in B^{*}(z,3s/2)}r_{e}\leq s\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{x_{e^{\prime}}\in B^{*}(z,3s/2)}r_{e^{\prime}}\leq C(Y,\mathfrak{c})s.

(vii)

for any
r\in(0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert B^{*}_{rs}\left(\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\bigcap B^{*}(z,3s/2)\right)\right\rvert\leq C(Y,\eta)r^{5.1}s^{6};

in particular,
\mathscr{H}^{1}\left(\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\bigcap B^{*}(z,3s/2)\right)=0
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
s=1
. The construction of the flat neck region
\mathscr{N}
is analogous to that in Proposition
6.15
, and we only sketch the proof. As before, we assume
\delta\ll\mathfrak{c}
and set
\gamma:=\mathfrak{c}^{5}
.

Since
B^{*}(z,1)
is a type-II
c
-ball, by the definition of
t_{s}
, there exists
q\in\mathcal{P}_{\zeta,1}(z)\cap B^{*}(z,1)
such that
q
is
(\epsilon,1)
-close to
\mathcal{F}^{a}(\Gamma)
with respect to
\mathcal{L}_{q,1}
, where
a=t_{s}(z,1)-\mathfrak{t}(q)
. We define

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{z,1}:=\mathcal{L}_{q,1}\bigcap B^{*}(z,2.2)\bigcap Z_{(-\infty,t_{p}(z,1)]}.

We set
L^{1}=\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{z,1}
and choose a maximal
2\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma
-separated set
\{x_{f^{1}}\}\subset L^{1}
. In particular,
\{B^{*}(x_{f^{1}},\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma)\}
are pairwise disjoint and

\displaystyle L^{1}\subset\bigcup_{f^{1}}B^{*}(x_{f^{1}},2\mathfrak{c}^{2}\gamma).

By a limiting argument, if
\mathfrak{c}\leq\mathfrak{c}(\eta)
,
\epsilon\leq\epsilon(Y,\eta,\mathfrak{c})
and
\zeta\leq\zeta(Y,\epsilon,\eta,\mathfrak{c},\beta)
, then any ball
B^{*}(x_{f^{1}},\gamma)
satisfying
\mathfrak{t}(x_{f^{1}})<t_{p}(z,1)-2\eta^{-1}\gamma^{2}
must be a
b
-ball.

According to the type of
B^{*}(x_{f^{1}},\gamma)
, we can write

\displaystyle L^{1}\subset\bigcup_{b^{1}}B^{*}(x_{b^{1}},\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{c^{1}}B^{*}(x_{c^{1}},\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{1}}B^{*}(x_{d^{1}},\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{1}}B^{*}(x_{e^{1}},\gamma).

Then we set

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{1}:=B^{*}(z,2)\setminus\left(\bigcup_{b^{1}}B^{*}(x_{b^{1}},\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{c^{1}}B^{*}(x_{c^{1}},\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{1}}B^{*}(x_{d^{1}},\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{1}}B^{*}(x_{e^{1}},\gamma)\right).

Moreover, we define
\mathscr{C}^{1}:=\{x_{b^{1}},x_{c^{1}},x_{d^{1}},x_{e^{1}}\}
with radius
r_{x}:=\gamma
. As in the proof of Proposition
6.15
,
\mathscr{N}^{1}
is a
(\delta,\mathfrak{c},1)
-flat neck region regarding
\mathcal{F}^{0}(\Gamma)
.

We can reindex the
b
-balls
\bigcup_{b^{1}}B^{*}(x_{b^{1}},\gamma)
as

\displaystyle\bigcup_{b^{1,s}}B^{*}(x_{b^{1,s}},\gamma)\bigcup\bigcup_{b^{1,q}}B^{*}(x_{b^{1,q}},\gamma)

in the following way: if a
b
-ball
B^{*}(x_{b^{1}},\gamma)
satisfies
\mathfrak{t}(x_{b^{1}})\leq t_{p}(z,1)-2\eta^{-1}\gamma^{2}
, then it is designated as
B^{*}(x_{b^{1,s}},\gamma)
; otherwise, it is designated as
B^{*}(x_{b^{1,q}},\gamma)
.

By another limiting argument, if
\mathfrak{c}\leq\mathfrak{c}(\eta)
,
\epsilon\leq\epsilon(Y,\delta,\eta,\mathfrak{c})
and
\zeta\leq\zeta(Y,\epsilon,\eta,\mathfrak{c},\beta)
, then there exists
\xi=\xi(Y)>0
such that any point

\displaystyle y\in Z_{(-\infty,t_{p}(z,1)+2\xi^{2}\gamma^{2})}\bigcap B^{*}(z,2)

with
d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}^{1,\prime})=s\geq\gamma
is
(2,\delta,\delta s)
-symmetric for some constant
c(Y)>0
, where

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\prime}:=\mathscr{C}^{1}\setminus\bigcup_{b^{1,s}}\{x_{b^{1,s}}\}.

Next, we choose a cover
\{B^{*}(x_{e^{(1)}},\xi\gamma)\}
of

\displaystyle\left(Z_{(t_{p}(z,1)+2\xi^{2}\gamma^{2},\infty)}\setminus\bigcup_{f^{1}}B^{*}(x_{f^{1}},\gamma)\right)\bigcap B^{*}(z,2)

such that
\{B^{*}(x_{e^{(1)}},\xi\gamma/2)\}
are pairwise disjoint. By (
6.9
), all these balls are
e
-balls, and the total number of
\{B^{*}(x_{e^{(1)}},\xi\gamma)\}
is bounded by
C(Y)\gamma^{-6}
.

We define

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{1,\prime}=\mathscr{N}^{1}\bigcup\bigcup_{b^{1,s}}B^{*}(x_{b^{1,s}},\gamma)\setminus\left(\bigcup_{e^{(1)}}B^{*}(x_{e^{(1)}},\xi\gamma)\right).

Then it is clear from our construction that any point
y
in
\mathscr{N}^{1,\prime}
is
(2,\delta,\delta d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}^{1,\prime}))
-symmetric.

It is clear that if
x_{f^{1}}\in\{x_{b^{1,q}},x_{c^{1}},x_{d^{1}},x_{e^{1}}\}
, then
\mathfrak{t}(x_{f^{1}})\geq t_{p}(z,1)-2\eta^{-1}\gamma^{2}
. Thus, we have the content estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{b^{1,q}}r_{b^{1,q}}+\sum_{c^{1}}r_{c^{1}}+\sum_{d^{1}}r_{d^{1}}+\sum_{e^{1}}r_{e^{1}}\leq C(Y)\gamma\eta^{-1}\mathfrak{c}^{-4}

(6.17)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{e^{(1)}}r_{e^{(1)}}\leq C(Y)\gamma^{-5}.

(6.18)

Similar to the proof of Proposition
6.15
, after repeating the above decomposition for
l
times, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{l}:=B^{*}(z,2)\setminus\left(\bigcup_{c^{l}}B^{*}(x_{c^{l}},\gamma^{l})\bigcup\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq l}\left(\bigcup_{b^{j}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{j}}B^{*}(x_{d^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{j}}B^{*}(x_{e^{j}},\gamma^{j})\right)\right),

together with the corresponding collection
\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq l}\bigcup_{e^{(j)}}B^{*}(x_{e^{(j)}},\xi\gamma^{j})
.

As before,
\mathscr{N}^{l}
is a
(\delta,\mathfrak{c},1)
-flat neck region regarding
\mathcal{F}^{0}(\Gamma)
, with center
\mathscr{C}^{l}
consisting of the centers of the balls and radius function given by their associated radii.

Moreover, we define

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{l,\prime}=\mathscr{N}^{l}\bigcup\left(\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq l}\bigcup_{b^{j,s}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j,s}},\gamma^{j})\right)\setminus\left(\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq l}\bigcup_{e^{(j)}}B^{*}(x_{e^{(j)}},\xi\gamma^{j})\right).

Then any point
y
in
\mathscr{N}^{l,\prime}
is
(2,\delta,\delta d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}^{l,\prime}))
-symmetric, where

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{l,\prime}=\mathscr{C}^{l}\setminus\left(\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq l}\bigcup_{b^{j,s}}\{x_{b^{j,s}}\}\right).

Similar to (
6.17
) and (
6.18
), we obtain the following facts that will be used below:

•

For each
j\in[1,l]
, the total number of
\{x_{b^{j,q}},x_{c^{j}},x_{d^{j}},x_{e^{j}}\}
is at most
\left(C(Y)\eta^{-1}\mathfrak{c}^{-4}\right)^{j}
.

•

The total number of
\{x_{e^{(j)}}\}
is at most
C(Y)\gamma^{-6}\left(C(Y)\eta^{-1}\mathfrak{c}^{-4}\right)^{j-1}
.

Consequently, we compute

\displaystyle\sum_{b^{j,q}}r_{b^{j,q}}+\sum_{d^{j}}r_{d^{j}}+\sum_{e^{j}}r_{e^{j}}\leq\left(C(Y)\eta^{-1}\mathfrak{c}^{-4}\gamma\right)^{j}=\left(C(Y)\eta^{-1}\gamma^{1/5}\right)^{j}

(6.19)

where we used the fact that
\mathfrak{c}=\gamma^{1/5}
. Also, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{e^{(j)}}r_{e^{(j)}}\leq C(Y)\gamma^{-5}\left(C(Y)\eta^{-1}\mathfrak{c}^{-4}\gamma\right)^{j-1}=C(Y)\gamma^{-5}\left(C(Y)\eta^{-1}\gamma^{1/5}\right)^{j-1}.

(6.20)

Set
\mathcal{G}^{l}:=\bigcup_{x_{c^{l}}}\{x_{c^{l}}\}
. It is clear from our construction that
\mathcal{G}^{l+1}\subset B^{*}_{2\gamma^{l}}(\mathcal{G}^{l})
. Then we denote the Hausdorff limit of
\mathcal{G}^{l}
by
\mathscr{C}_{0}
. We define

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}:=B^{*}(z,2)\setminus\left(\mathscr{C}_{0}\bigcup\bigcup_{1\leq j<\infty}\left(\bigcup_{b^{j}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{j}}B^{*}(x_{d^{j}},\gamma^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{j}}B^{*}(x_{e^{j}},\gamma^{j})\right)\right).

From our construction,
\mathscr{N}
is a
(\delta,\mathfrak{c},1)
-flat neck region regarding
\mathcal{F}^{0}(\Gamma)
with centers given by
\mathscr{C}_{0}
together with the centers of the associated balls, and the radius function chosen to be the corresponding radii of those balls. Moreover, we define

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{\prime}=\mathscr{N}\bigcup\left(\bigcup_{1\leq j<\infty}\bigcup_{b^{j,s}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j,s}},\gamma^{j})\right)\setminus\left(\bigcup_{1\leq j<\infty}\bigcup_{e^{(j)}}B^{*}(x_{e^{(j)}},\xi\gamma^{j})\right).

Note that each point in
\mathscr{N}^{\prime}
is
(2,\delta,\delta d_{Z}(x,\mathscr{C}^{\prime}))
-symmetric, where

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{\prime}=\mathscr{C}\setminus\left(\bigcup_{1\leq j<\infty}\bigcup_{b^{j,s}}\{x_{b^{j,s}}\}\right).

We rewrite the above decomposition as:

B^{*}(z,2)\subset\left(\mathscr{C}_{0}\bigcup\mathscr{N}^{\prime}\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup\bigcup_{d}B^{*}(x_{d},r_{d})\bigcup\bigcup_{e}B^{*}(x_{e},r_{e})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{\prime}}B^{*}(x_{e^{\prime}},r_{e^{\prime}}),

where

\displaystyle\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b}):=

\displaystyle\bigcup_{1\leq j<\infty}\bigcup_{b^{j,q}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j,q}},\gamma^{j}),

\displaystyle\bigcup_{b^{\prime}}B^{*}(x_{b^{\prime}},r_{b^{\prime}}):=

\displaystyle\bigcup_{1\leq j<\infty}\bigcup_{b^{j,s}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j,s}},\gamma^{j}),

\displaystyle\bigcup_{e^{\prime}}B^{*}(x_{e^{\prime}},r_{e^{\prime}}):=

\displaystyle\bigcup_{1\leq j<\infty}\bigcup_{e^{(j)}}B^{*}(x_{e^{(j)}},\xi\gamma^{j}).

From (
6.19
), we compute

\displaystyle\sum_{x_{b}\in B^{*}(z,3/2)}r_{b}+\sum_{x_{d}\in B^{*}(z,3/2)}r_{d}+\sum_{x_{e}\in B^{*}(z,3/2)}r_{e}\leq\sum_{1\leq j<\infty}\left(C(Y)\eta^{-1}\gamma^{1/5}\right)^{j}\leq 1,

where we choose
\mathfrak{c}\leq\mathfrak{c}(Y,\eta)
so that
C(Y)\eta^{-1}\gamma^{1/5}\leq 1/2
.

Similarly, by (
6.20
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{x_{e^{\prime}}\in B^{*}(z,3/2)}r_{e^{\prime}}\leq\sum_{1\leq j<\infty}C(Y)\gamma^{-5}\left(C(Y)\eta^{-1}\gamma^{1/5}\right)^{j-1}\leq C(Y,\mathfrak{c}).

To complete the proof, it remains to show (vii). Indeed, since the total number of elements in
\mathscr{C}^{l,\prime}
is at most
\left(C(Y)\eta^{-1}\mathfrak{c}^{-4}\right)^{l}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\lvert B^{*}_{\gamma^{l}}\left(\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\bigcap B^{*}(z,3/2)\right)\right\rvert\leq\left\lvert B^{*}_{4\gamma^{l}}\left(\mathscr{C}^{l,\prime}\bigcap B^{*}(z,1.6)\right)\right\rvert\leq C(Y)\left(C(Y)\eta^{-1}\mathfrak{c}^{-4}\right)^{l}\gamma^{6l}\leq C(Y)\left(C(Y)\eta^{-1}\gamma^{1/5}\right)^{l}\gamma^{5l}.

We choose
\mathfrak{c}\leq\mathfrak{c}(Y,\eta)
such that
C(Y)\eta^{-1}\gamma^{1/5}\leq\gamma^{1/10}
. From this, the conclusion easily follows.

In sum, the proof is complete.
∎

Based on Propositions
6.15
and
6.17
, it is natural to introduce the following concept.

Definition 6.18
.

The set
\mathscr{N}^{\prime}
obtained in Proposition
6.15
or Proposition
6.17
is called the
modified
\delta
-region associated with

\mathscr{N}
.

•

In the case
k=2
, we have
\mathscr{N}^{\prime}\subset\mathscr{N}
, and any point
x\in\mathscr{N}^{\prime}
is
(3,\delta,\delta d_{Z}(x,\mathscr{C}))
-symmetric.

•

In the case
k=1
, we have
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\subset\mathscr{C}
, and any point
x\in\mathscr{N}^{\prime}
is
(2,\delta,\delta d_{Z}(x,\mathscr{C}^{\prime}))
-symmetric. Here,
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
is called the
modified center
.

Proposition 6.19

(Decomposition of
d
-balls)
.

For
z\in B^{*}(z_{0},4r_{0})
and
s\leq r_{0}-d_{Z}(z,z_{0})/4
, let
B^{*}(z,s)
be a
d
-ball with
k\in\{1,2\}
. If
\beta\leq\beta(Y)
, we have the decomposition
:

B^{*}(z,s)\subset S_{d}^{k}\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup\bigcup_{c}B^{*}(x_{c},r_{c})\bigcup\bigcup_{e}B^{*}(x_{e},r_{e})

satisfying

(i)

for each
b
,
B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})
is a
b
-ball with
r_{b}\leq\beta s;

(ii)

for each
c
,
B^{*}(x_{c},r_{c})
is a
c
-ball with
r_{c}\leq\beta s;

(iii)

for each
e
,
B^{*}(x_{e},r_{e})
is an
e
-ball with
r_{e}\leq\beta s;

(iv)

S_{d}^{k}\subset\mathcal{S}
with
\mathscr{H}^{k}(S_{d}^{k})=0;

(v)

the following content estimates hold:

\displaystyle\sum_{b}r_{b}^{k}+\sum_{e}r_{e}^{k}\leq C(Y)\beta^{k-6}s^{k}\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{c}r_{c}^{k}\leq C(Y)\beta s^{k}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
s=1
.

We first choose a maximal cover
\{B^{*}(x_{f^{1}},\beta)\}
of
B^{*}(z,1)
with
x_{f^{1}}\in B^{*}(z,1)
so that
\{B^{*}(x_{f^{1}},\beta/2)\}
pairwise disjoint. According to their types, we can write

\displaystyle B^{*}(z,1)\subset\bigcup_{b^{1}}B^{*}(x_{b^{1}},\beta)\bigcup\bigcup_{c^{1}}B^{*}(x_{c^{1}},\beta)\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{1}}B^{*}(x_{d^{1}},\beta)\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{1}}B^{*}(x_{e^{1}},\beta).

(6.21)

By Proposition
2.25
, we have the following content estimate:

\displaystyle\sum_{b^{1}}\beta^{k}+\sum_{c^{1}}\beta^{k}+\sum_{d^{1}}\beta^{k}+\sum_{e^{1}}\beta^{k}\leq C(Y)\beta^{k-6}=:C_{1}.

On the other hand, since
B^{*}(z,1)
is a
d
-ball, we have
\left\lvert B^{*}_{3\beta}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\zeta,1}(z)\right)\right\rvert\leq D\beta^{7-k}
by (
6.7
). Then for
c
-ball
B^{*}(x_{c^{1}},\beta)
and
d
-ball
B^{*}(x_{d^{1}},\beta)
, we know that neither
\mathcal{P}_{\zeta,\beta}(x_{c^{1}})
nor
\mathcal{P}_{\zeta,\beta}(x_{d^{1}})
is empty. Hence, by definition, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\zeta,\beta}(x_{c^{1}})\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{\zeta,\beta}(x_{d^{1}})\subset\mathcal{P}_{\zeta,1}(z)

which implies

\displaystyle B^{*}(x_{c^{1}},\beta/2)\bigcup B^{*}(x_{d^{1}},\beta/2)\subset B^{*}_{3\beta}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\zeta,1}(z)\right).

Therefore, if we denote the number of
c
-balls and
d
-balls in (
6.21
) by
N_{c}
and
N_{d}
, respectively, it follows from Proposition
2.25
that

\displaystyle(N_{c}+N_{d})\beta^{6}\leq C(Y)\beta^{7-k},

which implies that
N_{c}+N_{d}\leq C(Y)\beta^{1-k}
. Thus, we have the following better content estimate for
c
-balls and
d
-balls:

\displaystyle\sum_{c^{1}}\beta^{k}+\sum_{d^{1}}\beta^{k}\leq C(Y)\beta.

(6.22)

For each
d
-ball
B^{*}(x_{d^{1}},\beta)
, we can repeat the above decomposition to get

\displaystyle\bigcup_{d^{1}}B^{*}(x_{d^{1}},\beta)\subset\bigcup_{b^{2}}B^{*}(x_{b^{2}},\beta^{2})\bigcup\bigcup_{c^{2}}B^{*}(x_{c^{2}},\beta^{2})\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{2}}B^{*}(x_{d^{2}},\beta^{2})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{2}}B^{*}(x_{e^{2}},\beta^{2}).

By Proposition
2.25
and (
6.22
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{d^{2}}(\beta^{2})^{k}\leq\big(C(Y)\beta\big)^{2},\quad\sum_{b^{2}}(\beta^{2})^{k}+\sum_{e^{2}}(\beta^{2})^{k}\leq C_{1}C(Y)\beta,\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{c^{2}}(\beta^{2})^{k}\leq(C(Y)\beta)^{2}.

We write the decomposition of
B^{*}(z,1)
as

\displaystyle B^{*}(z,1)\subset\bigcup_{d^{2}}B^{*}(x_{d^{2}},\beta^{2})\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq 2}\left(\bigcup_{b^{j}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j}},\beta^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{c^{j}}B^{*}(x_{c^{j}},\beta^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{j}}B^{*}(x_{e^{j}},\beta^{j})\right)

with the content estimates:

\displaystyle\sum_{d^{2}}(\beta^{2})^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\big(C(Y)\beta\big)^{2},

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j\leq 2}\left(\sum_{b^{j}}(\beta^{j})^{k}+\sum_{e^{j}}(\beta^{j})^{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}+C_{1}C(Y)\beta,

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j\leq 2}\sum_{c^{j}}(\beta^{j})^{k}

\displaystyle\leq C(Y)\beta+(C(Y)\beta)^{2}.

After repeating the above decomposition for
l
steps, we obtain

\displaystyle B^{*}(z,1)\subset\bigcup_{d^{l}}B^{*}(x_{d^{l}},\beta^{l})\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq l}\left(\bigcup_{b^{j}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j}},\beta^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{c^{j}}B^{*}(x_{c^{j}},\beta^{j})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{j}}B^{*}(x_{e^{j}},\beta^{j})\right),

with the content estimates:

\displaystyle\sum_{d^{l}}(\beta^{l})^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\big(C(Y)\beta\big)^{l},

(6.23)

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j\leq l}\left(\sum_{b^{j}}(\beta^{j})^{k}+\sum_{e^{j}}(\beta^{j})^{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sum_{0\leq j\leq l-1}(C(Y)\beta)^{j},

(6.24)

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j\leq l}\sum_{c^{j}}(\beta^{j})^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{1\leq j\leq l}(C(Y)\beta)^{j}.

(6.25)

We assume that
\beta
is small enough so that
C(Y)\beta<1/2
. From (
6.24
) and (
6.25
), we have for any
l\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j\leq l}\left(\sum_{b^{j}}(\beta^{j})^{k}+\sum_{e^{j}}(\beta^{j})^{k}\right)\leq C_{1}C(Y)\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{1\leq j\leq l}\sum_{c^{j}}(\beta^{j})^{k}\leq C(Y)\beta.

Set
S^{k,l}_{d}:=\bigcup_{d^{l}}\{x_{d^{l}}\}
. By our construction, we have
S^{k,l+1}_{d}\subset B^{*}_{\beta^{l}}\left(S^{k,l}_{d}\right)
. Then we denote the Hausdorff limit of
S^{k,l}_{d}
by
S^{k}_{d}
. From (
6.23
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert B^{*}_{\beta^{l}}\left(S^{k,l}_{d}\right)\right\rvert\leq\sum_{d^{l}}\left\lvert B^{*}(x_{d^{l}},\beta^{l})\right\rvert\leq C(Y)\sum_{d^{l}}\beta^{6l}\leq C(Y)\left(C(Y)\beta\right)^{l}\beta^{(6-k)l}.

Thus, we obtain
\mathscr{H}^{k}(S^{k}_{d})=0
. Moreover, since any
x_{d^{l}}
is not
(k+1,\eta,\beta^{l})
-symmetric, it is easy to show that
S^{k}_{d}\subset\mathcal{S}
.

In sum, the proof is complete.
∎

Proposition 6.20

(Inductive decomposition)
.

Given
k\in\{1,2\}
,
z\in B^{*}(z_{0},4r_{0})
and
s\leq r_{0}-d_{Z}(z,z_{0})/4
, for any constants
\delta>0
,
\eta>0
,
\mathfrak{c}\in(0,10^{-40})
and
\beta>0
, if
\mathfrak{c}\leq\mathfrak{c}(Y,\eta)

(
for
k=2
it suffices to assume
\mathfrak{c}\leq\mathfrak{c}(Y)
)
,
\beta\leq\beta(Y,\mathfrak{c})
and
\zeta\leq\zeta(Y,\delta,\eta,\mathfrak{c},\beta)
, we have the decomposition:

B^{*}(z,s)\subset\bigcup_{a}\left((\mathscr{C}_{0,a}\bigcup\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a})\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup\bigcup_{e}B^{*}(x_{e},r_{e})\bigcup S_{0},

satisfying

(i)

for all
a
,
\mathscr{N}_{a}\subset B^{*}(x_{a},2r_{a})
is either a
(k,\delta,\mathfrak{c},r_{a})
-quotient cylindrical neck region or a
(\delta,\mathfrak{c},r_{a})
-flat neck region with center
\mathscr{C}_{a}=\mathscr{C}_{a,0}\bigcup\mathscr{C}_{a,+}

(
in the former case, we set
\mathscr{N}_{a}^{\prime}=\mathscr{N}_{a};
in the latter,
\mathscr{N}_{a}^{\prime}
is the modified
\delta
-region associated with
\mathscr{N}_{a}
);

(ii)

for all
b
,
B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})
is a
b
-ball
;

(iii)

for all
e
,
B^{*}(x_{e},r_{e})
is an
e
-ball
;

(iv)

S_{0}\subset\mathcal{S}
with
\mathscr{H}^{k}(S_{0})=0;

(v)

the following content estimate holds:

\displaystyle\sum_{a}\left(r_{a}^{k}+\mathscr{H}^{k}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0,a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\right)\right)+\sum_{b}r_{b}^{k}+\sum_{e}r_{e}^{k}\leq C(Y,\mathfrak{c},\beta)s^{k}.

Proof.

This follows from the iterative decompositions of
c
-balls and
d
-balls. Without loss of generality, we assume
s=1
.

We consider
B^{*}(z,1)
by types. If
B^{*}(z,1)
is a
b
-ball or an
e
-ball, then we are done. So we only need to consider the remaining two cases in the following argument. Assume first that
B^{*}(z,1)
is a
d
-ball. By Proposition
6.19
, we can write

\displaystyle B^{*}(z,1)\subset\bigcup_{b^{\prime}}B^{*}(x_{b^{\prime}},r_{b^{\prime}})\bigcup\bigcup_{c^{\prime}}B^{*}(x_{c^{\prime}},r_{c^{\prime}})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{\prime}}B^{*}(x_{e^{\prime}},r_{e^{\prime}})\bigcup\tilde{S}^{1},

with
\tilde{S}^{1}\subset\mathcal{S}
satisfying
\mathscr{H}^{k}(\tilde{S}^{1})=0
and the following content estimates:

\displaystyle\sum_{b^{\prime}}r_{b^{\prime}}^{k}+\sum_{e^{\prime}}r_{e^{\prime}}^{k}\leq C(Y,\beta)\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{c^{\prime}}r_{c^{\prime}}^{k}

\displaystyle\leq C(Y)\beta.

For each
c
-ball
B^{*}(x_{c^{\prime}},r_{c^{\prime}})
, we can apply Proposition
6.13
,
6.15
, or
6.17
to obtain

\displaystyle B^{*}(x_{c^{\prime}},r_{c^{\prime}})\subset\left((\mathscr{C}_{0,c^{\prime}}\bigcup\mathscr{N}_{c^{\prime}}^{\prime})\bigcap B^{*}(x_{c^{\prime}},r_{c^{\prime}})\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b^{\prime\prime}}B^{*}(x_{b^{\prime\prime}},r_{b^{\prime\prime}})\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{\prime\prime}}B^{*}(x_{d^{\prime\prime}},r_{d^{\prime\prime}})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{\prime\prime}}B^{*}(x_{e^{\prime\prime}},r_{e^{\prime\prime}}),

together with the content estimate:

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{k}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0,c^{\prime}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{c^{\prime}},r_{c^{\prime}})\right)+\sum_{b^{\prime\prime}}r_{b^{\prime\prime}}^{k}+\sum_{d^{\prime\prime}}r_{d^{\prime\prime}}^{k}+\sum_{e^{\prime\prime}}r_{e^{\prime\prime}}^{k}\leq C(Y,\mathfrak{c})r_{c^{\prime}}^{k}.

Here,
\mathscr{N}_{c^{\prime}}^{\prime}=\mathscr{N}_{c^{\prime}}
if
\mathscr{N}_{c^{\prime}}\subset B^{*}(x_{c^{\prime}},2r_{c^{\prime}})
is a quotient cylindrical neck region, while
\mathscr{N}_{c^{\prime}}^{\prime}
denotes the modified
\delta
-region associated with
\mathscr{N}_{c^{\prime}}
if
\mathscr{N}_{c^{\prime}}
is a flat neck region.

Combining the above two decompositions and according to the type of these balls, we write

\displaystyle B^{*}(z,1)\subset

\displaystyle\bigcup_{a^{1}}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0,a^{1}}\bigcup\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a^{1}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{1}},r_{a^{1}})\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b^{1}}B^{*}(x_{b^{1}},r_{b^{1}})\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{1}}B^{*}(x_{d^{1}},r_{d^{1}})

\displaystyle\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{1}}B^{*}(x_{e^{1}},r_{e^{1}})\bigcup\tilde{S}^{1}

with
\tilde{S}^{1}\subset\mathcal{S}
satisfying
\mathscr{H}^{k}(\tilde{S}^{1})=0
and the following content estimate:

\displaystyle\sum_{b^{1}}r_{b^{1}}^{k}+\sum_{e^{1}}r_{e^{1}}^{k}\leq C(Y,\beta)+C(Y,\mathfrak{c})\beta=:C_{1},

\displaystyle\sum_{a^{1}}\left(r_{a^{1}}^{k}+\mathscr{H}^{k}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0,a^{1}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{1}},r_{a^{1}})\right)\right)+\sum_{d^{1}}r_{d^{1}}^{k}\leq C(Y,\mathfrak{c})\beta.

Now we apply the above decomposition to each
d
-ball
B^{*}(x_{d^{1}},r_{d^{1}})
to get the second decomposition

\displaystyle B^{*}(z,1)\subset

\displaystyle\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq 2}\left(\bigcup_{a^{j}}\big(\mathscr{C}_{0,a^{j}}\bigcup\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a^{j}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{j}},r_{a^{j}})\big)\bigcup\bigcup_{b^{j}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j}},r_{b^{j}})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{j}}B^{*}(x_{e^{j}},r_{e^{j}})\bigcup\tilde{S}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{2}}B^{*}(x_{d^{2}},r_{d^{2}})

with
\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq 2}\tilde{S}^{j}\subset\mathcal{S}
satisfying
\mathscr{H}^{k}(\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq 2}\tilde{S}^{j})=0
and the following content estimate:

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j\leq 2}\sum_{a^{j}}\left(r_{a^{j}}^{k}+\mathscr{H}^{k}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0,a^{j}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{j}},r_{a^{j}})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{1\leq j\leq 2}\left(C(Y,\mathfrak{c})\beta\right)^{j},

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j\leq 2}\left(\sum_{b^{j}}r_{b^{j}}^{k}+\sum_{e^{j}}r_{e^{j}}^{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sum_{0\leq j\leq 1}\left(C(Y,\mathfrak{c})\beta\right)^{j},

\displaystyle\sum_{d^{2}}r_{d^{2}}^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\left(C(Y,\mathfrak{c})\beta\right)^{2}.

Repeating this decomposition
l
times, we get

\displaystyle B^{*}(z,1)\subset

\displaystyle\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq l}\left(\bigcup_{a^{j}}\big(\mathscr{C}_{0,a^{j}}\bigcup\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a^{j}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{j}},r_{a^{j}})\big)\bigcup\bigcup_{b^{j}}B^{*}(x_{b^{j}},r_{b^{j}})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{j}}B^{*}(x_{e^{j}},r_{e^{j}})\bigcup\tilde{S}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle\bigcup\bigcup_{d^{l}}B^{*}(x_{d^{l}},r_{d^{l}})

with
\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq l}\tilde{S}^{j}\subset\mathcal{S}
satisfying
\mathscr{H}^{k}(\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq l}\tilde{S}^{j})=0
and the following content estimate:

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j\leq l}\sum_{a^{j}}\left(r_{a^{j}}^{k}+\mathscr{H}^{k}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0,a^{j}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{j}},r_{a^{j}})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{1\leq j\leq l}\left(C(Y,\mathfrak{c})\beta\right)^{j},

(6.26)

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j\leq l}\left(\sum_{b^{j}}r_{b^{j}}^{k}+\sum_{e^{j}}r_{e^{j}}^{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sum_{0\leq j\leq l-1}\left(C(Y,\mathfrak{c})\beta\right)^{j},

(6.27)

\displaystyle\sum_{d^{l}}r_{d^{l}}^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\left(C(Y,\mathfrak{c})\beta\right)^{l}.

(6.28)

Choose
\beta
small such that
C(Y,\mathfrak{c})\beta\leq 1/2
. Set
\tilde{S}_{d^{l}}=\bigcup_{d^{l}}\{x_{d^{l}}\}
and
(\tilde{S}_{d^{l}})_{2}:=\bigcup_{d^{l}}B^{*}(x_{d^{l}},2r_{d^{l}})
. By construction,
(\tilde{S}_{d^{l+1}})_{2}\subset(\tilde{S}_{d^{l}})_{2}
. Set

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{d}:=\bigcap_{l=1}^{\infty}(\tilde{S}_{d^{l}})_{2}.

Thus, by construction, we have
\tilde{S}_{d}\subset\mathcal{S}
. Define

\displaystyle S_{0}:=\tilde{S}_{d}\bigcup\bigcup_{l=1}^{\infty}\tilde{S}^{l}.

It is clear from (
6.28
) that
S_{0}\subset\mathcal{S}
with
\mathscr{H}^{k}(S_{0})=0
. Moreover, the content estimate follows from (
6.26
) and (
6.27
).

If
B^{*}(z,1)
is a
c
-ball, we first apply Proposition
6.13
,
6.15
, or
6.17
to obtain a decomposition involving only
b
-balls,
d
-balls,
e
-balls and neck regions. Then, applying the above decomposition procedure recursively to each
d
-ball completes the proof.
∎

Now, we are ready to prove Theorem
6.9
.

Proof of Theorem
6.9
.

For given constants
\delta
and
\eta
, we fix the constants as follows.

•

If
k=1
, set
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{c}(Y,\eta)
,
\beta=\beta(Y,\mathfrak{c})=\beta(Y,\eta)
and
\zeta\leq\zeta(Y,\delta,\eta,\mathfrak{c},\beta)=\zeta(Y,\delta,\eta)
so that the conclusions of Proposition
6.20
hold.

•

If
k=2
, set
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{c}(Y)
,
\beta=\beta(Y,\mathfrak{c})=\beta(Y)
and
\zeta\leq\zeta(Y,\delta,\eta,\mathfrak{c},\beta)=\zeta(Y,\delta,\eta)
so that the conclusions of Proposition
6.20
hold.

Moreover, in the following proof, the constant
C=C(Y)
if
k=2
and
C=C(Y,\eta)
if
k=1
.

For simplicity, we consider the function

\displaystyle\bar{W}_{x,r}(\zeta):=\inf_{y\in B^{*}(x,4r)}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{y}(\zeta^{-2}r^{2})

for any
x\in B^{*}(z_{0},4r_{0})
and
r\leq r_{0}-d_{Z}(x,z_{0})/4
. In particular,
\bar{W}_{z_{0},r_{0}}(\zeta)=\bar{W}
in (
6.4
).

Choose a maximal cover
\{B^{*}(x_{i},\zeta r_{0})\}
of
B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})
such that
x_{i}\in B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})
and
\{B^{*}(x_{i},\zeta/2)\}
are pairwise disjoint. We set
r_{1}:=\zeta r_{0}
. Note that

\displaystyle\bar{W}_{x_{i},r_{1}}(\zeta)=\inf_{y\in B^{*}(x_{i},4\zeta r_{0})}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{y}(r_{0}^{2})\geq\inf_{y\in B^{*}(z_{0},4)}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{y}(r_{0}^{2})=\bar{W}_{z_{0},r_{0}}(1).

(6.29)

Apply Proposition
6.20
to each
B^{*}(x_{i},r_{1})
. In this context, we consider a new quantity
\bar{W}_{1}:=\bar{W}_{x_{i},r_{1}}(\zeta)
, and define the updated pinching sets and localized pinching set as

\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\zeta,r}:=\{y\in B^{*}(x_{i},4r_{1})\mid\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{y}(\zeta^{2}r^{2})\leq\bar{W}_{1}+\zeta\}.

and

\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\zeta,r}(x):=\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\zeta,r}\bigcap B^{*}(x,2.5r).

Accordingly, the definitions of
b
-balls,
c
-balls,
d
-balls and
e
-balls are now made with respect to
\bar{W}_{1}
and the updated pinching set
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\zeta,r}(x)
.

From Proposition
6.20
, we obtain

\displaystyle B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\subset\bigcup_{a^{1}}\left((\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a^{1}}\bigcup\mathscr{C}_{0,a^{1}})\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{1}},r_{a^{1}})\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b^{1}}B^{*}(x_{b^{1}},r_{b^{1}})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{1}}B^{*}(x_{e^{1}}^{\prime},r_{e^{1}}^{\prime})\bigcup S_{0}^{1},

with
r_{a^{1}},r_{b^{1}},r_{e^{1}}^{\prime}\leq\zeta r_{0}
,
S_{0}^{1}\subset\mathcal{S}
and
\mathscr{H}^{k}(S_{0}^{1})=0
. Moreover, we have the following content estimates:

\displaystyle\sum_{a^{1}}(r_{a^{1}})^{k}+\sum_{b^{1}}(r_{b^{1}})^{k}+\sum_{e^{1}}(r_{e^{1}}^{\prime})^{k}+\mathscr{H}^{k}\left(\bigcup_{a^{1}}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0,{a^{1}}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{1}},r_{a^{1}})\right)\right)\leq Cr_{0}^{k}.

For each
e
-ball, by (
6.29
), we have

\displaystyle\bar{W}_{x^{\prime}_{e^{1}},r_{e^{1}}^{\prime}}(\zeta^{-1})=\inf_{y\in B^{*}(x_{e^{1}}^{\prime},4r_{e^{1}}^{\prime})}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{y}\left((\zeta r^{\prime}_{e^{1}})^{2}\right)\geq\inf_{i}\left\{\bar{W}_{x_{i},r_{1}}(\zeta)\right\}+\zeta\geq\bar{W}_{z_{0},r_{0}}(1)+\zeta.

(6.30)

Moreover, for each
e
-ball
B^{*}(x_{e^{1}}^{\prime},r_{e^{1}}^{\prime})
, choose a maximal cover
\{B^{*}(x_{e^{1}}^{\prime\prime},\zeta r_{e^{1}}^{\prime})\}
with
x_{e^{1}}^{\prime\prime}\in B^{*}(x_{e^{1}}^{\prime},r_{e^{1}}^{\prime})
such that
x_{e^{1}}^{\prime\prime}\in B^{*}(x_{e^{1}}^{\prime},r_{e^{1}}^{\prime})
and
\{B^{*}(x_{e^{1}}^{\prime\prime},\zeta r_{e^{1}}^{\prime}/2)\}
are pairwise disjoint. Set
r_{e^{1}}^{\prime\prime}:=\zeta r_{e^{1}}^{\prime}
. Therefore by (
6.30
), it follows that

\displaystyle\bar{W}_{x_{e^{1}}^{\prime\prime},r_{e^{1}}^{\prime\prime}}(1)=\inf_{y\in B^{*}(x_{e^{1}}^{\prime\prime},4r^{\prime\prime}_{e^{1}})}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{y}\left((\zeta r^{\prime}_{e^{1}})^{2}\right)\geq\bar{W}_{z_{0},r_{0}}(1)+\zeta.

Combining the above two decompositions, we obtain

\displaystyle B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\subset\bigcup_{a^{1}}\left((\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a^{1}}\bigcup\mathscr{C}_{0,a^{1}})\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{1}},r_{a^{1}})\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b^{1}}B^{*}(x_{b^{1}},r_{b^{1}})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{1}}B^{*}(x_{e^{1}},r_{e^{1}})\bigcup S_{0}^{1},

with
S_{0}^{1}\subset\mathcal{S}
and
\mathscr{H}^{k}(S_{0}^{1})=0
. Moreover, we have the following content estimates:

\displaystyle\sum_{a^{1}}r_{a^{1}}^{k}+\sum_{b^{1}}r_{b^{1}}^{k}+\sum_{e^{1}}r_{e^{1}}^{k}+\mathscr{H}^{k}\left(\bigcup_{a^{1}}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0,{a^{1}}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{1}},r_{a^{1}})\right)\right)\leq Cr_{0}^{k}.

And for each
e
-ball,
\bar{W}_{x_{e^{1}},r_{e^{1}}}(1)\geq\bar{W}_{z_{0},r_{0}}(1)+\zeta
.

Repeat the above process for each
e
-ball
B^{*}(x_{e^{1}},r_{e^{1}})
, we get the following second decomposition

\displaystyle B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\subset\bigcup_{a^{2}}\left((\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a^{2}}\bigcup\mathscr{C}_{0,a^{2}})\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{2}},r_{a^{2}})\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b^{2}}B^{*}(x_{b^{2}},r_{b^{2}})\bigcup\bigcup_{e^{2}}B^{*}(x_{e^{2}},r_{e^{2}})\bigcup S_{0}^{2},

with
S_{0}^{2}\subset\mathcal{S}
and
\mathscr{H}^{k}(S_{0}^{2})=0
. Moreover, we have the following content estimates:

\displaystyle\sum_{a^{2}}r_{a^{2}}^{k}+\sum_{b^{2}}r_{b^{2}}^{k}+\sum_{e^{2}}r_{e^{2}}^{k}+\mathscr{H}^{k}\left(\bigcup_{a^{2}}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0,{a^{2}}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{2}},r_{a^{2}})\right)\right)\leq Cr_{0}^{k}.

Moreover, for each
e
-ball
B^{*}(x_{e^{2}},r_{e^{2}})
, we have
\bar{W}_{x_{e^{2}},r_{e^{2}}}(1)\geq\bar{W}_{z_{0},r_{0}}(1)+2\zeta
.

We apply Proposition
6.20
to each
e
-ball
B^{*}(x_{e^{2}},r_{e^{2}})
to continue the decomposition process. This process must terminate in at most
C(Y)/\zeta
steps, since our Ricci flow limit space
Z
is obtained from a sequence of closed Ricci flows in
\mathcal{M}(n,Y,T)
. In other words, after the final step, no
e
-balls remain.

Define

\displaystyle\tilde{S}^{k,\delta,\eta}:=\bigcup_{i}S_{0}^{i}.

After re-indexing the balls, we obtain the following decomposition:

\displaystyle B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\subset\bigcup_{a}\big(\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}\bigcup\mathscr{C}_{0,a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\big)\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup\tilde{S}^{k,\delta,\eta},

with
\tilde{S}^{k,\delta,\eta}\subset\mathcal{S}
and
\mathscr{H}^{k}(\tilde{S}^{k,\delta,\eta})=0
. Moreover, the following content estimates hold:

\displaystyle\sum_{a}r_{a}^{k}+\sum_{b}r_{b}^{k}+\mathscr{H}^{k}\left(\bigcup_{a}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0,a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\right)\right)\leq Cr_{0}^{k}.

Finally, set

\displaystyle S^{k,\delta,\eta}=\tilde{S}^{k,\delta,\eta}\bigcup\bigcup_{a}\mathscr{C}_{0,a}.

This completes the proof.
∎

6.4
Rectifiability and volume estimates of singular sets

Recall that for each stratum
\mathcal{S}^{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{k}=\bigcup_{\epsilon\in(0,1)}\bigcap_{0<r<\epsilon}\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,k}_{r,\epsilon}.

where
\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,k}_{r_{1},r_{2}}
is the quantitative singular stratum in Definition
2.20
.

We now fix all related constants by the following lemma, whose proof is straightforward from our definition of neck regions and a limiting argument.

Lemma 6.21
.

Given
\epsilon>0
, there exist small positive constants
\delta=\delta(Y,\epsilon)
,
\eta=\eta(Y,\epsilon)
and
\zeta=\zeta(Y,\epsilon)
such that the following properties hold:

(i)

Theorem
6.9
holds with the constants
\delta
,
\eta
and
\zeta
.

(ii)

If
\mathscr{N}_{a}=B^{*}(x_{a},2r_{a})\setminus B^{*}_{r_{x}}(\mathscr{C}_{a})
is a
(k,\delta,\mathfrak{c}(Y),r_{a})
-quotient neck region for
k\in\{1,2\}
, then any
y\in B^{*}(x_{a},3r_{a}/2)\setminus B^{*}_{r_{x}/4}(\mathscr{C}_{a})
is
(3,\epsilon,s)
-symmetric for any
s\leq\epsilon d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}_{a})
.

(iii)

If
\mathscr{N}_{a}=B^{*}(x_{a},2r_{a})\setminus B^{*}_{r_{x}}(\mathscr{C}_{a})
is a
(\delta,\mathfrak{c}(Y),r_{a})
-flat neck region and
k=2
, then any
y\in\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}\bigcup\left(B^{*}(x_{a},3r_{a}/2)\bigcap\left(B^{*}_{r_{x}}(\mathscr{C}_{a})\setminus B^{*}_{r_{x}/4}(\mathscr{C}_{a})\right)\right)
is
(3,\epsilon,s)
-symmetric for any
s\leq\epsilon d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}_{a})
, where
\mathscr{N}_{a}^{\prime}
is the modified
\delta
-region associated with
\mathscr{N}_{a}
.

(iv)

If
\mathscr{N}_{a}=B^{*}(x_{a},2r_{a})\setminus B^{*}_{r_{x}}(\mathscr{C}_{a})
is a
(\delta,\mathfrak{c}(Y),r_{a})
-flat neck region and
k=1
, then any
y\in\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},3r_{a}/2)
is
(2,\epsilon,s)
-symmetric for any
s=\epsilon d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{a})
, where
\mathscr{N}_{a}^{\prime}
is the modified
\delta
-region associated with
\mathscr{N}_{a}
.

(v)

If
B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})
is a
b
-ball, then any
y\in B^{*}(x_{b},3r_{b}/2)
is
(k+1,\epsilon,\epsilon r_{b})
-symmetric if
k=2
, and
(k+1,\epsilon,r)
-symmetric for some
r\in(c(Y,\epsilon)r_{b},r_{b})
if
k=1
.

Based on Lemma
6.21
, we define

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}_{a}^{\prime\prime}:=\begin{cases}B^{*}(x_{a},3r_{a}/2)\setminus B^{*}_{r_{x}/4}(\mathscr{C}_{a}),\quad&\text{for case (ii)};\\
\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}\bigcup\left(B^{*}(x_{a},3r_{a}/2)\bigcap\left(B^{*}_{r_{x}}(\mathscr{C}_{a})\setminus B^{*}_{r_{x}/4}(\mathscr{C}_{a})\right)\right),\quad&\text{for case (iii)}.\end{cases}

(6.31)

Next, we prove the rectifiability for the singular strata
\mathcal{S}^{0}
,
\mathcal{S}^{1}
and
\mathcal{S}^{2}
.

Theorem 6.22
.

For any
k\in\{0,1,2\}
,
\mathcal{S}^{k}
is parabolic
k
-rectifiable.

Proof.

We prove the the case
k\in\{0,1,2\}
separately.

Case
k=0
:
This has already been proved in Proposition
6.1
.

Case
k=1
:
Since
\mathcal{S}^{1}\setminus\mathcal{S}^{0}=\mathcal{S}^{1}_{\mathrm{qc}}\setminus\mathcal{S}^{0}_{\mathrm{qc}}
and
\mathscr{H}^{1}(\mathcal{S}^{0})=0
, it follows from Theorem
5.32
that
\mathcal{S}^{1}
is horizontally parabolic
1
-rectifiable.

Case
k=2
:
Recall that we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{2}\setminus\mathcal{S}^{1}=\left(\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\mathrm{qc}}\setminus\mathcal{S}^{1}_{\mathrm{qc}}\right)\sqcup\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}.

By Theorem
5.32
, it remains only to show that
\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}
is vertically parabolic
2
-rectifiable.

Fix any
z_{0}\in\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}
. By Theorem
6.9
(with
k=2
), there exists a sufficiently small
r_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\subset\bigcup_{a}\left(\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}\bigcup\mathscr{C}_{0,a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup\tilde{S}^{2,\delta,\eta}.

(6.32)

By Lemma
6.21
, choosing
\epsilon
sufficiently small ensures that

\displaystyle\bigcup_{a}\left(\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})

consists entirely of regular points. Therefore, from (
6.32
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\subset\bigcup_{a}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0,a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\right)\bigcup\tilde{S}^{2,\delta,\eta}.

It is clear from the definition of
\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}
that if
\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}\bigcap\mathscr{C}_{0,a}\neq\emptyset
, then
\mathscr{N}_{a}
is a static
(\delta,\mathfrak{c}(Y),r_{a})
-flat neck region, which implies, by Lemma
6.6
, that
\mathscr{C}_{0,a}
is vertically parabolic
2
-rectifiable. Thus, we conclude that
\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})
is vertically parabolic
2
-rectifiable.

A standard covering argument then shows that
\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}
is vertically parabolic
2
-rectifiable, completing the proof.
∎

Corollary 6.23
.

For
\mathscr{H}^{2}
-a.e.
x\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
, the tangent flow at
x
is backward unique in the sense that the negative part of any tangent flow at
x
is isometric to
\mathbb{R}^{4}/\Gamma\times\mathbb{R}_{-}
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Theorem
6.22
, for any
z_{0}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
, there exists
r_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\subset\bigcup_{a}\left(\mathscr{C}_{0,a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\right)\bigcup\tilde{S}^{2,\delta,\eta}.

For any
x\in\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\bigcap\mathscr{C}_{0,a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})
, it follows from the definition of the flat neck region that
x
is
(\epsilon,r)
-close to
\mathcal{F}^{0}(\Gamma)
for any
0<r\leq r_{a}
. Therefore, the negative part of any tangent flow at
x
is given by
\mathbb{R}^{4}/\Gamma\times\mathbb{R}_{-}
.

A standard covering argument then completes the proof.
∎

Next, we prove the optimal volume estimates for quantitative singular strata.

Theorem 6.24
.

For any
z_{0}\in Z
with
\mathfrak{t}(z_{0})-2r_{0}^{2}\in\mathbb{I}^{-}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert B^{*}_{rr_{0}}\left(\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{rr_{0},\epsilon r_{0}}\right)\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\right\rvert\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{4}r_{0}^{6}

(6.33)

for any
r\in(0,\epsilon)
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{2}\left(\bigcap_{0<r<\epsilon}\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{rr_{0},\epsilon r_{0}}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\right)\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r_{0}^{6}.

(6.34)

Proof.

Given
\epsilon>0
, we choose
\delta
,
\eta
and
\zeta
according to Lemma
6.21
. Without loss of generality, we assume
r_{0}=1
and
r\leq\epsilon/100
. Moreover, by a standard covering argument, we may assume
\mathfrak{t}(z_{0})-2\zeta^{-2}\in\mathbb{I}^{-}
.

By Theorem
6.9
(with
k=2
), we have

\displaystyle B^{*}(z_{0},2)\subset\bigcup_{a}\left(\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup S^{2,\delta,\eta}

(6.35)

so that
S^{2,\delta,\eta}
is parabolic
2
-rectifiable and

\displaystyle\sum_{a}r^{2}_{a}+\sum_{b}r^{2}_{b}+\mathscr{H}^{2}\left(S^{2,\delta,\eta}\right)\leq C(Y).

(6.36)

Since
r\leq 1/100
, we have
B^{*}_{r}(\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon})\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},1)\subset B^{*}_{r}\left(\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\cap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\right)
.

For any
y\in\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\cap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\cap\mathscr{N}^{\prime\prime}_{a}
, where
\mathscr{N}^{\prime\prime}_{a}
is defined in (
6.31
), it follows from Lemma
6.21
(ii) (iii) that
d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}_{a})<2\epsilon^{-1}r
, since otherwise
y
would be a
(3,\epsilon,2r)
-symmetric point, contradicting the fact that
y\in\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}
.

If
r_{a}\geq 100\epsilon^{-1}r
, we set
W_{a}=\{z\in\mathscr{C}_{a}\mid d_{Z}(y,z)=d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}_{a})\text{ for some }y\in\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap\mathscr{N}^{\prime\prime}_{a}\}
. Define
s=16\epsilon^{-1}r
. By a standard covering argument, there exists a maximal set
\{x_{i}\mid x_{i}\in W_{a}\}_{1\leq i\leq K_{a}}
such that
\{B^{*}(x_{i},s)\}_{1\leq i\leq K_{a}}
covers
W_{a}
and
\{B^{*}(x_{i},s/2)\}_{1\leq i\leq K_{a}}
are pairwise disjoint. In particular, we have

\displaystyle B^{*}(x_{i},s)\subset B^{*}(x_{a},5r_{a}/3)

by our assumption. Let
\mu_{a}
denote the corresponding packing measure on
\mathscr{N}_{a}
. Then, by Proposition
5.31
or
6.7
, we have

\displaystyle 0<K_{a}c(Y)s^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{K_{a}}\mu_{a}\big(B^{*}(x_{i},s/2)\big)\leq\mu_{a}\big(B^{*}(x_{a},5r_{a}/3)\big)\leq C(Y)r^{2}_{a},

where the last inequality can be derived from Ahlfors regularity after covering
B^{*}(x_{a},5r_{a}/3)
by balls of size
r_{a}/100
. Thus, we conclude that
K_{a}\leq C(Y)r^{2}_{a}r^{-2}
. In addition, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\bigcap\mathscr{N}^{\prime\prime}_{a}\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{K_{a}}B^{*}(x_{i},2s),

(6.37)

which implies

\displaystyle\left\lvert B^{*}_{r}\left(\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\bigcap\mathscr{N}^{\prime\prime}_{a}\right)\right\rvert\leq\sum_{i=1}^{K_{a}}\left\lvert B^{*}(x_{i},3s)\right\rvert\leq C(Y,\epsilon)K_{a}r^{6}\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{2}_{a}r^{4}.

Next, we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap\bigcup_{r_{a}\geq 100\epsilon^{-1}r}\mathscr{N}^{\prime\prime}_{a}.

In sum, we obtain

\left\lvert B_{r}^{*}\left(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\right)\right\rvert\leq C(Y,\epsilon)\sum_{a}r^{2}_{a}r^{4}\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{4},

(6.38)

where we have used (
6.36
) for the final inequality.

Next, we take a maximal
r
-separated set
\{y_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq K}
of
\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\setminus\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
. We define the subset

I^{a}:=\left\{1\leq i\leq K\mid B^{*}(y_{i},r/2)\bigcap\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\neq\emptyset\text{ and }r_{a}\geq 100\epsilon^{-1}r\right\}.

It follows from our definition of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
that for
i\in I^{a}
,
y_{i}\in B^{*}(x_{a},3r_{a}/2)\bigcap B^{*}_{r_{x}/4}(\mathscr{C}_{a})
. We choose
z_{i}\in\mathscr{C}_{a}
such that
d_{i}:=d_{Z}(z_{i},y_{i})\leq r_{z_{i}}/4
. Then it is clear that
d_{i}+r/2\geq r_{z_{i}}
since otherwise
B^{*}(y_{i},r/2)\bigcap\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}=\emptyset
. From this, we obtain that

\displaystyle r\geq\frac{3}{2}r_{z_{i}}\quad\text{and}\quad d_{i}\leq\frac{r}{6}.

(6.39)

For
i,j\in I^{a}
, it follows from (
6.39
) that
d_{Z}(z_{i},z_{j})\geq d_{Z}(y_{i},y_{j})-d_{i}-d_{j}\geq 2r/3
. In other words, the balls
\{B^{*}(z_{i},r/3)\}_{i\in I^{a}}
are mutually disjoint. By Proposition
2.25
, we conclude that any point in
\bigcup_{i\in I^{a}}B^{*}(z_{i},2r/3)
belongs to at most
C(Y)
balls in
\{B^{*}(z_{i},2r/3)\}_{i\in I^{a}}
. Thus, it follows from Theorem
6.9
(a) that

\displaystyle 0<|I^{a}|c(Y)r^{2}\leq\sum_{i\in I^{a}}\mu_{a}\big(B^{*}(z_{i},2r/3)\big)\leq C(Y)\mu_{a}\big(B^{*}(x_{a},5r_{a}/3)\big)\leq C(Y)r^{2}_{a},

which implies
|I^{a}|\leq C(Y)r^{2}_{a}r^{-2}
. Taking the sum for all possible
a
and using (
6.36
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bigcup_{a}I^{a}\right\rvert\leq C(Y)r^{-2}.

(6.40)

Next, we set
J:=\{1,\ldots,K\}\setminus\bigcup_{a}I^{a}
. For
i\in J
, if
B^{*}(y_{i},r/2)\bigcap\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\neq\emptyset
, then
r_{a}\leq 100\epsilon^{-1}r
. On the other hand, if
B^{*}(y_{i},r/2)\bigcap B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\neq\emptyset
, then it follows from Lemma
6.21
(v) that
r_{b}\leq 2\epsilon^{-1}r
. Otherwise,
y_{i}\in B^{*}(x_{b},3r_{b}/2)
and hence is
(3,\epsilon,\epsilon r_{b})
-symmetric. However, this contradicts the fact that
y_{i}\in\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}
.

It follows from the decomposition (
6.35
) that

\displaystyle\bigcup_{i\in J}B^{*}(y_{i},r/2)\subset

\displaystyle\bigcup_{r_{a}\leq 100\epsilon^{-1}r}B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\bigcup\bigcup_{r_{b}\leq 2\epsilon^{-1}r}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup S^{2,\delta,\eta}.

By comparing the volumes, it follows that

\displaystyle 0<c(Y)|J|r^{6}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in J}\left\lvert B^{*}(y_{i},r/2)\right\rvert\leq\sum_{r_{a}\leq 100\epsilon^{-1}r}\left\lvert B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\right\rvert+\sum_{r_{b}\leq 2\epsilon^{-1}r}\left\lvert B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\right\rvert+\left\lvert S^{2,\delta,\eta}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(Y)\left(\sum_{r_{a}\leq 100\epsilon^{-1}r}r_{a}^{6}+\sum_{r_{b}\leq 2\epsilon^{-1}r}r_{b}^{6}\right)\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{4}\left(\sum_{a}r^{2}_{a}+\sum_{b}r^{2}_{b}\right)\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{4},

where we have used the fact that
r_{a}\leq 100\epsilon^{-1}r
,
r_{b}\leq 2\epsilon^{-1}r
and
\left\lvert S^{2,\delta,\eta}\right\rvert=0
since
S^{2,\delta,\eta}
is parabolic
2
-rectifiable.

Consequently,
|J|\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{-2}
. Combining this with (
6.40
) and using the fact that all
\{B^{*}(y_{i},2r)\}_{1\leq i\leq K}
cover
B^{*}_{r}\left(\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\setminus\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\right)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert B^{*}_{r}\left(\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\setminus\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\right)\right\rvert\leq\sum_{i=1}^{K}\left\lvert B^{*}(y_{i},2r)\right\rvert\leq C(Y)Kr^{6}\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{4}.

(6.41)

Combining (
6.38
) and (
6.41
), we conclude that for any
r\in(0,\epsilon)
,

\left\lvert B_{r}^{*}\left(\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\right)\right\rvert\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{4},

(6.42)

which completes the proof of (
6.33
).

Since
\bigcap_{0<r<\epsilon}\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\subset\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}
for any
r\in(0,\epsilon)
, it follows from (
6.42
) that

\left\lvert B^{*}_{r}\left(\bigcap_{0<r<\epsilon}\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\right)\right\rvert\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{4}

for any
r\in(0,\epsilon)
. From this, it is clear that

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{2}\left(\bigcap_{0<r<\epsilon}\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\right)\leq C(Y,\epsilon),

which yields (
6.34
) and completes the proof.
∎

Similarly, we have

Theorem 6.25
.

For any
z_{0}\in Z
with
\mathfrak{t}(z_{0})-2r_{0}^{2}\in\mathbb{I}^{-}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert B^{*}_{rr_{0}}\left(\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,1}_{rr_{0},\epsilon r_{0}}\right)\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\right\rvert\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{5}r_{0}^{6}

for any
r\in(0,\epsilon)
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{1}\left(\bigcap_{0<r<\epsilon}\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,1}_{rr_{0},\epsilon r_{0}}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\right)\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r_{0}^{6}.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Theorem
6.24
, and we only sketch it. As before, we assume
r_{0}=1
,
r\leq\epsilon/100
and fix constants
\delta
,
\eta
and
\zeta
.

By Theorem
6.9
(with
k=1
), we have

\displaystyle B^{*}(z_{0},2)\subset\bigcup_{a}\left(\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup S^{1,\delta,\eta}.

so that
S^{1,\delta,\eta}
is parabolic
1
-rectifiable and

\displaystyle\sum_{a}r_{a}+\sum_{b}r_{b}+\mathscr{H}^{1}\left(S^{1,\delta,\eta}\right)\leq C(Y,\epsilon).

(6.43)

We reindex

\displaystyle\bigcup_{a}\big(\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\big)=\bigcup_{a^{\prime}}\big(\mathscr{N}_{a^{\prime}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{\prime}},r_{a^{\prime}})\big)\bigcup\bigcup_{a^{\prime\prime}}\big(\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime\prime}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{\prime\prime}},r_{a^{\prime\prime}})\big)

so that each
\mathscr{N}_{a^{\prime}}
is a quotient cylindrical neck region and each
\mathscr{N}_{a^{\prime\prime}}
is a flat neck region. For simplicity, we set

\displaystyle S_{0}:=\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,1}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\bigcap B^{*}_{r}\left(\bigcup_{r_{a^{\prime\prime}}\geq 100\epsilon^{-1}r}\left(\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime\prime}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{\prime\prime}},r_{a^{\prime\prime}})\right)\right).

According to the definition, for any
y\in S_{0}
, there exists
a^{\prime\prime}
with
r_{a^{\prime\prime}}\geq 100\epsilon^{-1}r
such that
d_{Z}\left(y,\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime\prime}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{\prime\prime}},r_{a^{\prime\prime}})\right)\leq r
. Then it follows from Lemma
6.21
(ii) (iv) that
d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime\prime}})<2\epsilon^{-1}r
, since otherwise
y
would be a
(2,\epsilon,\epsilon d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime\prime}}))
-symmetric point, contradicting the fact that
y\in\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,1}_{r,\epsilon}
.

Thus, we conclude from Proposition
6.17
that

\displaystyle\left\lvert B_{r}^{*}\left(S_{0}\right)\right\rvert\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{r_{a^{\prime\prime}}\geq 100\epsilon^{-1}r}\left\lvert B_{3\epsilon^{-1}r}^{*}\left(\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime\prime}}\right)\right\rvert\leq\sum_{r_{a^{\prime\prime}}\geq 100\epsilon^{-1}r}C(Y,\epsilon)r^{5.1}r^{0.9}_{a^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{r_{a^{\prime\prime}}\geq 100\epsilon^{-1}r}C(Y,\epsilon)r^{5}r_{a^{\prime\prime}}\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{5},

(6.44)

where we used (
6.43
) for the last inequality.

Next, we set

\displaystyle S_{1}=\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,1}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\setminus S_{0}.

Then it follows from the definition of
S_{0}
that

\displaystyle B^{*}_{r}\left(S_{1}\right)\subset\bigcup_{a^{\prime}}\left(\mathscr{N}_{a^{\prime}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{\prime}},r_{a^{\prime}})\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{r_{a^{\prime\prime}}\leq 100\epsilon^{-1}r}\left(\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a^{\prime\prime}}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a^{\prime\prime}},r_{a^{\prime\prime}})\right)\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup S^{1,\delta,\eta}.

By the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
6.24
, we have

\left\lvert B_{r}^{*}\left(S_{1}\right)\right\rvert\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{5}.

(6.45)

Combining (
6.4
) and (
6.45
), the proof is complete.
∎

As an application of Theorem
6.24
, we have

Theorem 6.26
.

For any
z_{0}\in Z
with
\mathfrak{t}(z_{0})-2r_{0}^{2}\in\mathbb{I}^{-}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{2}\left(\mathcal{S}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\right)\leq C(Y)r_{0}^{6}.

Proof.

It follows from
[
FL25a
, Theorem 8.14]
that if
\epsilon\leq\epsilon(Y)
,
\mathcal{S}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\subset\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{rr_{0},\epsilon r_{0}}
for any
r\in(0,\epsilon)
. Thus, for some fixed
\epsilon=\epsilon(Y)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})=\bigcap_{0<r<\epsilon}\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{rr_{0},\epsilon r_{0}}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})

which yields the conclusion by (
6.34
).
∎

We also have the following estimate for each time-slice.

Theorem 6.27
.

Fix
k\in\{1,2\}
. For any
z_{0}\in Z
with
\mathfrak{t}(z_{0})-2r_{0}^{2}\in\mathbb{I}^{-}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert B^{*}_{rr_{0}}\left(\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,k}_{rr_{0},\epsilon r_{0}}\right)\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\bigcap Z_{t}\right\rvert_{t}\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{4-k}r_{0}^{4},\quad\forall r\in(0,\epsilon).

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we set
r_{0}=1
. In the proof below, we focus on the case
k=2
, using the same notation as in the proof of Theorem
6.24
, since the case
k=1
can be treated in the same way.

It follows from (
6.37
) and
[
FL25a
, Proposition 5.34]
that if
r_{a}\geq 100\epsilon^{-1}r
, then

\displaystyle\left\lvert B^{*}_{r}\left(\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\bigcap\mathscr{N}^{\prime\prime}_{a}\right)\bigcap Z_{t}\right\rvert_{t}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{K_{a}}\left\lvert B^{*}(x_{i},3s)\bigcap Z_{t}\right\rvert_{t}\leq C(Y,\epsilon)K_{a}r^{4}\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{2}_{a}r^{2},

which implies

\left\lvert B_{r}^{*}\left(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\right)\bigcap Z_{t}\right\rvert_{t}\leq C(Y,\epsilon)\sum_{a}r^{2}_{a}r^{2}\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{2}.

(6.46)

By the same argument, we obtain as (
6.41
) that

\displaystyle\left\lvert B^{*}_{r}\left(\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\setminus\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\right)\bigcap Z_{t}\right\rvert_{t}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{K}\left\lvert B^{*}(y_{i},2r)\bigcap Z_{t}\right\rvert_{t}\leq CKr^{4}\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{2}.

(6.47)

Combining (
6.46
) and (
6.47
), we conclude that for any
r\in(0,\epsilon)
,

\left\lvert B_{r}^{*}\left(\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},3/2)\right)\bigcap Z_{t}\right\rvert_{t}\leq C(Y,\epsilon)r^{2},

which completes the proof.
∎

As a corollary, we prove

Corollary 6.28
.

For any
z_{0}\in Z
with
\mathfrak{t}(z_{0})-2r_{0}^{2}\in\mathbb{I}^{-}
,
r<1
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\{r_{\mathrm{Rm}}<rr_{0}\}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\bigcap Z_{t}\right\rvert_{t}\leq C(Y)r^{2}r_{0}^{4}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
r_{0}=1
and
r\leq r(Y)\ll 1
.

By
[
FL25a
, Theorem 8.14]
, we have

\displaystyle\{r_{\mathrm{Rm}}<r\}\subset\mathcal{S}^{\epsilon,2}_{r,\epsilon},

if
\epsilon=\epsilon(Y)
is sufficiently small. By Theorem
6.27
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\{r_{\mathrm{Rm}}<r\}\bigcap B^{*}(z_{0},1)\bigcap Z_{t}\right\rvert_{t}\leq C(Y)r^{2}.

∎

Thanks to Corollary
6.28
, the cutoff functions constructed in
[
FL25a
, Proposition 8.20]
admit improved estimates in dimension four.:

Proposition 6.29
.

For any
z\in Z_{\mathbb{I}^{-}}
, we can find a family of smooth cutoff functions
\{\eta_{r,A}\}
satisfying the following properties for
r\leq r(Y)
.

(1)

r_{\mathrm{Rm}}\geq r
and
d_{Z}(z,\cdot)\leq 2A
on
\{\eta_{r,A}>0\}
.

(2)

\eta_{r,A}=1
on
\{r_{\mathrm{Rm}}\geq 2r\}\cap B^{*}(z,A)
.

(3)

\displaystyle r|\nabla\eta_{r,A}|+r^{2}|\partial_{\mathfrak{t}}\eta_{r,A}|+r^{2}|\nabla^{2}\eta_{r,A}|\leq C
, where
C
is a universal constant.

(4)

For any
L
with
\mathfrak{t}(z)-2L^{2}\in\mathbb{I}^{-}
, there exists a constant
C=C(Y,L,A)>0
such that

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{R}_{[\mathfrak{t}(z)-L^{2},\mathfrak{t}(z)+L^{2}]}\cap\{0<\eta_{r,A}<1\}}1\,\mathrm{d}V_{g^{Z}_{t}}\mathrm{d}t\leq Cr^{4}.

Moreover, for any
t\in[\mathfrak{t}(z)-L^{2},\mathfrak{t}(z)+L^{2}]
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t}\cap\{0<\eta_{r,A}<1\}}1\,\mathrm{d}V_{g^{Z}_{t}}\leq Cr^{2}.

We also remark that, by the same reason, the cutoff functions on Ricci shrinker spaces constructed in
[
FL25a
, Proposition 8.23]
also have better estimates in dimension four; we leave the precise formulation to the interested readers.

Next, we show that in dimension four, the modified pointed
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
-entropy in Definition
4.4
coincides with the pointed
\mathcal{W}
-entropy in Definition
2.2
:

Proposition 6.30
.

Let
(Z,d_{Z},\mathfrak{t})
be a noncollapsed Ricci flow limit space arising as the pointed Gromov–Hausdorff limit of a sequence in
\mathcal{M}(4,Y,T)
. Then for any
z\in Z_{\mathbb{I}^{-}}
and any
\tau>0
such that
\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau\in\mathbb{I}^{-}
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}(\tau)=\mathcal{W}_{z}(\tau).

Proof.

By Definitions
4.2
and
4.4
, it suffices to show that for any
z\in Z_{\mathbb{I}^{-}}
and any
\tau>0
such that
\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau\in\mathbb{I}^{-}
, it holds that

\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}}\Delta f_{z}-|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}=0.

(6.48)

In fact, let
\eta_{r,A}
be the cutoff functions from Proposition
6.29
. Then, using integration by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}}\eta_{r,A}\Delta f_{z}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}=\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}}\eta_{r,A}|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}-\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}}\langle\nabla\eta_{r,A},\nabla f_{z}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}.

(6.49)

By Proposition
2.7
and smooth convergence on the regular part, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}}|\Delta f_{z}|+|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}<\infty,

(6.50)

which implies

\displaystyle\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
A\to\infty\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}}\eta_{r,A}\left(\Delta f_{z}-|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}=\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}}\Delta f_{z}-|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}.

(6.51)

On the other hand, by Proposition
6.29
, it follows that there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
A
and
r
such that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}}\langle\nabla\eta_{r,A},\nabla f_{z}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}\right|\leq Cr^{-1}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}\bigcap\{0<\eta_{r,A}<1\}}|\nabla f_{z}|\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Cr^{-1}\left(\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}\bigcap\{0<\eta_{r,A}<1\}}|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}\bigcap\{0<\eta_{r,A}<1\}}1\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq
