\displaystyle C\left(\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}\bigcap\{0<\eta_{r,A}<1\}}|\nabla f_{z}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}\right)^{1/2}.

(6.52)

Combining (
6.50
) with (
6.4
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{A\to\infty}\lim_{r\to 0}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}}\langle\nabla\eta_{r,A},\nabla f_{z}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{z;\mathfrak{t}(z)-\tau}=0.

(6.53)

From (
6.49
), (
6.51
) and (
6.53
), we conclude that (
6.48
) holds, which completes the proof.
∎

6.5
L^{1}
-curvature bounds for four-dimensional closed Ricci flows

Let
\mathcal{X}=\{M^{4},(g(t))_{t\in[-T,0)}\}
be a four-dimensional closed Ricci flow with entropy bounded below by
-Y
such that
T<\infty
and
0
is the first singular time. Assume that
(Z,d_{Z},\mathfrak{t})
is the
d^{*}
-completion of
\mathcal{X}_{[-0.98T,0)}
.

Theorem 6.31
.

With the above assumptions, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
Y
,
T
and
\mathrm{diam}_{g(-0.99T)}(M)
such that for any
t\in[-T/2,0)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{M}|\mathrm{Rm}|\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}\leq\int_{M}r^{-2}_{\mathrm{Rm}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}\leq C.

Proof.

For small parameters
\delta>0
and
\eta>0
to be determined later, we choose
\zeta=\zeta(Y,\delta,\eta)
such that Theorem
6.9
holds with
k=2
. Specifically, for any
z_{0}\in Z_{[-T/2,0]}
, set
r_{0}:=\zeta\sqrt{T/8}
. Then we obtain the following decomposition:

\displaystyle B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\subset\bigcup_{a}\big(\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\big)\bigcup\bigcup_{b}B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcup S^{2,\delta,\eta},

\displaystyle S^{2,\delta,\eta}\subset\bigcup_{a}\big(\mathscr{C}_{0,a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\big)\bigcup\tilde{S}^{2,\delta,\eta},

with the following properties:

(a)

For each
a
,
\mathscr{N}_{a}=B^{*}(x_{a},2r_{a})\setminus B^{*}_{r_{x}}(\mathscr{C}_{a})
is either a
(2,\delta,\mathfrak{c},r_{a})
-quotient cylindrical neck region or a
(\delta,\mathfrak{c},r_{a})
-flat neck region, where
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{c}(Y)
. In the former case, we set
\mathscr{N}_{a}^{\prime}=\mathscr{N}_{a}
; in the latter,
\mathscr{N}_{a}^{\prime}
denotes the modified
\delta
-region associated with
\mathscr{N}_{a}
.

(b)

For each
b
, there exists a point in
B^{*}(x_{b},2r_{b})
which is
(3,\eta,r_{b})
-symmetric.

(c)

The following content estimate holds:

\displaystyle\sum_{a}r_{a}^{2}+\sum_{b}r_{b}^{2}+\mathscr{H}^{2}(S^{2,\delta,\eta})\leq C(Y)r_{0}^{2}.

(6.54)

Fix
t\in[-T/2,0)
. Since
S^{2,\delta,\eta}\subset Z_{0}
and
Z_{t}
is smooth, we have

\displaystyle B^{*}(z_{0},r_{0})\bigcap Z_{t}\subset\bigcup_{a}\big(\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\bigcap Z_{t}\big)\bigcup\bigcup_{b}\left(B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcap Z_{t}\right).

From Lemma
6.21
(v), it follows that for any
\epsilon>0
, if
\eta\leq\eta(Y,\epsilon)
, then any point
y\in B^{*}(x_{b},3r_{b}/2)
is
(3,\epsilon,\epsilon r_{b})
-symmetric. If
\epsilon=\epsilon(Y)
is small, then by
[
FL25a
, Theorem 8.13]
, we obtain that
r^{-2}_{\mathrm{Rm}}(y)\leq C(Y)r_{b}^{-2}
.
Therefore, by
[
FL25a
, Proposition 3.17 (i)]
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B^{*}(x_{b},r_{b})\bigcap Z_{t}}r^{-2}_{\mathrm{Rm}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}\leq C(Y)r_{b}^{-2}\cdot r_{b}^{4}\leq C(Y)r_{b}^{2}.

(6.55)

Next, we consider the
a
-balls in the following two cases.

Case 1:

\mathscr{N}_{a}=B^{*}(x_{a},2r_{a})\setminus B^{*}_{r_{x}}(\mathscr{C}_{a})
is a
(2,\delta,\mathfrak{c},r_{a})
-quotient cylindrical neck region.

In this case,
\mathscr{N}_{a}^{\prime}=\mathscr{N}_{a}
. For any
x\in\mathscr{N}_{a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\bigcap Z_{t}=:Z_{a,t}
, we set
d_{x}:=d_{Z}(x,\mathscr{C}_{a})=d_{Z}(x,x^{\prime})
for some
x^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}_{a}
. By definition, we have
d_{x}\geq r_{x^{\prime}}
. Applying the Vitali covering argument, we can choose
\{x_{i}\}\subset Z_{a,t}
such that for any
i\neq j
,
d_{Z}(x_{i},x_{j})\geq 2(d_{x_{i}}+d_{x_{j}})
,
and moreover,

\displaystyle Z_{a,t}\subset\bigcup_{i}B^{*}(x_{i},10d_{x_{i}}).

(6.56)

By Definition
5.29

(n3)
, for each
i
,
x_{i}^{\prime}\in Z
is
(2,\delta,d_{x_{i}})
-quotient cylindrical (regarding
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{2}
or
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{2}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{2})
). Moreover, by Definition
5.29

(n4)
and the fact
d_{Z}(x_{i},\mathscr{C}_{a})=d_{x_{i}}
, there exists a constant
c(Y)>0
such that
t=\mathfrak{t}(x_{i})\leq\mathfrak{t}(x_{i}^{\prime})-c(Y)d_{x_{i}}^{2}
. Hence, by the quotient version of
[
FL25b
, Lemma 2.27]
, if
\delta\leq\delta(Y,\epsilon)
, then every
y\in B^{*}(x_{i},10d_{x_{i}})\bigcap Z_{t}
is
(3,\epsilon,\epsilon d_{x_{i}})
-symmetric for
\epsilon\leq\epsilon(Y)
, and therefore satisfies the curvature bound
r^{-2}_{\mathrm{Rm}}(y)\leq C(Y)d_{x_{i}}^{-2}
.

Applying
[
FL25a
, Proposition 3.17 (i)]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B^{*}(x_{i},10d_{x_{i}})\bigcap Z_{a,t}}r^{-2}_{\mathrm{Rm}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}\leq C(Y)d_{x_{i}}^{2}.

(6.57)

On the other hand, by Ahlfors regularity (Proposition
5.31
), if
d_{x_{i}}\leq r_{a}/100
,

\displaystyle\mu\left(B^{*}(x_{i}^{\prime},d_{x_{i}})\right)\geq c(Y)d_{x_{i}}^{2}>0.

Combining this with (
6.57
) gives

\displaystyle\int_{B^{*}(x_{i},10d_{x_{i}})\bigcap Z_{a,t}}r^{-2}_{\mathrm{Rm}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}\leq C(Y)\mu\left(B^{*}(x_{i}^{\prime},d_{x_{i}})\right).

(6.58)

By construction, for
i\neq j
we have
B^{*}(x_{i}^{\prime},d_{x_{i}})\bigcap B^{*}(x_{j}^{\prime},d_{x_{j}})=\emptyset
and if
d_{x_{i}}\leq r_{a}/100
, then
B^{*}(x_{i}^{\prime},d_{x_{i}})\subset B^{*}(x_{a},3r_{a}/2)
. Moreover, by Proposition
2.25
, the number of indices
i
with
d_{x_{i}}\geq r_{a}/100
is finite and bounded by
C(Y)
. For these indices, the integrals as in (
6.57
) can be estimated directly by
C(Y)r_{a}^{2}
.

Finally, combining (
6.56
) with (
6.58
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{Z_{a,t}}r^{-2}_{\mathrm{Rm}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}\leq

\displaystyle C(Y)\sum_{d_{x_{i}}\leq r_{a}/100}\mu\left(B^{*}(x_{i}^{\prime},d_{x_{i}})\right)+C(Y)r_{a}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(Y)\mu(B^{*}(x_{a},3r_{a}/2))+C(Y)r_{a}^{2}\leq C(Y)r_{a}^{2},

(6.59)

where the last inequality follows from Ahlfors regularity after covering
B^{*}(x_{a},3r_{a}/2)
by balls of radius
r_{a}/100
.

Case 2:

\mathscr{N}_{a}=B^{*}(x_{a},2r_{a})\setminus B^{*}_{r_{x}}(\mathscr{C}_{a})
is a
(\delta,\mathfrak{c},r_{a})
-flat neck region.

In this case, every point
y\in\mathscr{N}_{a}^{\prime}
is
(3,\delta,\delta d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}_{a}))
-symmetric. Hence, if we choose
\delta=\delta(Y)
and compare with the model space
\mathbb{R}^{4}/\Gamma\times(-\infty,0]
at scale
d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}_{a})
, we obtain

\displaystyle r^{-2}_{\mathrm{Rm}}(y)\leq C(Y)d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}_{a})^{-2}.

(6.60)

For any
r\in(0,r_{a}]
, set

\displaystyle A_{a,r}:=\{y\in\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{a}\bigcap B^{*}(x_{a},r_{a})\bigcap Z_{t}\mid d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}_{a})\in[r/2,2r]\}.

We choose a small constant
c_{1}=c_{1}(Y)>0
to be determined later.

If
r\in[c_{1}r_{a},r_{a}]
, then by
[
FL25a
, Proposition 3.17(i)]
we have
|A_{a,r}|_{t}\leq Cr_{a}^{4}
for a universal constant
C
. Hence, by (
6.60
),

\displaystyle\int_{A_{a,r}}r^{-2}_{\mathrm{Rm}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}\leq C(Y)r^{2}.

(6.61)

If
r\in(0,c_{1}r_{a}]
, fix
y\in A_{a,r}
and choose
y^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}_{a}
realizing the distance, i.e.
d_{Z}(y,\mathscr{C}_{a})=d_{Z}(y,y^{\prime})
. For any
x\in A_{a,r}
, pick
x^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}_{a}
with
d_{Z}(x,\mathscr{C}_{a})=d_{Z}(x,x^{\prime})
. Then
\left\lvert\mathfrak{t}(x)-\mathfrak{t}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq d_{Z}^{2}(x,x^{\prime})\leq 4r^{2}
. By Lemma
6.6
, it follows that
d^{2}_{Z}(y^{\prime},x^{\prime})\leq C(Y)|\mathfrak{t}(x^{\prime})-\mathfrak{t}(y^{\prime})|\leq C(Y)r^{2}
. Note that if
c_{1}=c_{1}(Y)
is chosen to be small, the assumption of Lemma
6.6
can be satisfied. Hence,
A_{a,r}\subset B^{*}(y^{\prime},C(Y)r)
. Applying
[
FL25a
, Proposition 3.17(i)]
again yields
|A_{a,r}|_{t}\leq C(Y)r^{4}
, which, together with (
6.60
), implies (
6.61
) as well.

Choose
r=2^{-i}r_{a}
for
i\geq 0
. Then summing (
6.61
) gives

\displaystyle\int_{\mathscr{N}_{a}^{\prime}\bigcap Z_{t}}r^{-2}_{\mathrm{Rm}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}\leq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\int_{A_{a,2^{-i}r_{a}}}r^{-2}_{\mathrm{Rm}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}\leq C(Y)r_{a}^{2}.

(6.62)

Combining (
6.55
), (
6.5
), and (
6.62
), together with the content estimate (
6.54
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B^{*}(x_{0},r_{0})\bigcap Z_{t}}r^{-2}_{\mathrm{Rm}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}\leq C(Y)\left(\sum_{a}r_{a}^{2}+\sum_{b}r_{b}^{2}\right)\leq C(Y)r_{0}^{2}.

(6.63)

We now fix constants
\delta=\delta(Y),\,\eta=\eta(Y)
and
\zeta=\zeta(Y)
so that all the above estimates hold. By
[
FL25a
, Equation (9.1)]
, the diameter of
(Z,d_{Z})
is bounded by a constant depending on
Y
,
T
and
\mathrm{diam}_{g(-0.99T)}(M)
. Consequently, for any
t\in[-T/2,0)
,
Z_{t}
can be covered by at most
C(Y)
balls
B^{*}(z_{i},r_{0})
with
z_{i}\in Z_{[-T/2,0]}
. Applying (
6.63
) on each ball yields

\displaystyle\int_{Z_{t}}r^{-2}_{\mathrm{Rm}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}\leq C,

where
C
depends only on
Y
,
T
and
\mathrm{diam}_{g(-0.99T)}(M)
.
∎

By essentially the same argument as in Theorem
6.31
, one likewise obtains integral estimates for
\nabla^{k}\mathrm{Rm}
for any
k\geq 1
.

Theorem 6.32
.

With the above assumptions, for any integer
k\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C_{k}>0
depending on
k
,
Y
,
T
and
\mathrm{diam}_{g(-0.99T)}(M)
such that for any
t\in[-T/2,0)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{M}|\nabla^{k}\mathrm{Rm}|^{\frac{2}{k+2}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}\leq C_{k}.

Using Theorem
6.31
, we obtain a refinement of
[
FL25a
, Corollary 9.3 and Proposition 9.4]
. We note that the corresponding estimate is already known in the setting of compact Kähler Ricci flows, where it follows from properties of the Kähler forms.

Corollary 6.33
.

There exists a constant
C
depending on
Y
,
T
and
\mathrm{diam}_{g(-0.99T)}(M)
such that for any
s,t\in[-T/2,0)
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert|M|_{t}-|M|_{s}\right\rvert\leq C|t-s|.

Proof.

This follows directly from Theorem
6.31
together with the identity

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}{\displaystyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mu}{\mathrm{d}\mkern 0.0mut}}}|M|_{t}=-\int_{M}\mathrm{R}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}.

∎

Appendix A
Geometric transformation theorem for almost splitting maps

In this appendix, we derive a geometric transformation theorem for almost splitting maps on closed Ricci flows and noncollapsed Ricci flow limit spaces. Roughly speaking, under suitable conditions, if a map is almost splitting at a given scale, then at each smaller scale, it can be transformed by a lower triangular matrix so that it remains almost splitting at that scale. Moreover, the growth of these transformations can be quantitatively controlled. Note that a similar geometric transformation theorem was established in the setting of noncollapsed Ricci limit spaces in
[
CJN21
, Theorem 7.2]
.

We begin with the following elementary lemma of linear algebra, whose proof is straightforward and omitted.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
A
be a
k\times k
symmetric matrix and
T
a lower triangular
k\times k
matrix such that
TAT^{t}=\mathrm{Id}
. Then the following statements hold.

(i)

If
\|A-\mathrm{Id}\|\leq\epsilon
, then
\|T-\mathrm{Id}\|\leq C(k)\epsilon
.

(ii)

If all diagonal entries of
A
lie in
[-\Lambda,\Lambda]
, then each diagonal entry of
T
has absolute value bounded below by
\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.

Here, the norm
\|\cdot\|
is the operator norm of the matrix.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\mathcal{X}=\{M^{n},(g(t))_{t\in I}\}
be a closed Ricci flow and
x_{0}^{*}=(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathcal{X}
with
[t_{0}-10r^{2},t_{0}]\subset I
. Assume that
\vec{u}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k}):M\times[t_{0}-10r^{2},t_{0}]
is a
(k,\epsilon,r)
-splitting map. If there is a lower triangular
k\times k
-matrix
T
such that
\vec{u}^{\prime}:=T\vec{u}
is a
(k,\epsilon,s)
-splitting map at
x_{0}^{*}
for some
s\in[r/2,r]
, then
\rVert T-\mathrm{Id}\rVert\leq C(n)\epsilon
.

Proof.

By
[
FL25a
, Proposition 10.2]
, we see that for any
1\leq i,j\leq k
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{-2r^{2}}^{-r^{2}}\int_{M}\langle\nabla u_{i},\nabla u_{j}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{0}^{*};t}\mathrm{d}t-\delta_{ij}\right\rvert\leq 2\epsilon\quad\text{and}\quad\left\lvert\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{-2r^{2}}^{-r^{2}}\int_{M}\langle\nabla u^{\prime}_{i},\nabla u^{\prime}_{j}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{0}^{*};t}\mathrm{d}t-\delta_{ij}\right\rvert\leq 2\epsilon.

Thus, we can find lower triangular matrices
T_{1},T_{2}
such that for any
1\leq i,j\leq k
,

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{-2r^{2}}^{-r^{2}}\int_{M}\left\langle\nabla(T_{1}\vec{u})_{i},\nabla(T_{1}\vec{u})_{j}\right\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{0}^{*};t}\mathrm{d}t=\delta_{ij}\quad\text{and}\quad\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{-2r^{2}}^{-r^{2}}\int_{M}\left\langle\nabla(T_{2}\vec{u}^{\prime})_{i},\nabla(T_{2}\vec{u}^{\prime})_{j}\right\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{0}^{*};t}\mathrm{d}t=\delta_{ij}.

By Lemma
A.1
(i), we have
\rVert T_{1}-\mathrm{Id}\rVert+\rVert T_{2}-\mathrm{Id}\rVert\leq C(n)\epsilon
.

Since
T_{2}\vec{u}^{\prime}=T_{2}T\vec{u}
, it follows from the uniqueness of Cholesky decomposition that
T_{1}=T_{2}T
. Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\rVert T-\mathrm{Id}\rVert=\rVert T_{2}^{-1}T_{1}-\mathrm{Id}\rVert\leq C(n)\epsilon,

where we used the elementary estimate

\displaystyle\|A_{1}A_{2}-\mathrm{Id}\|\leq\|A_{1}-\mathrm{Id}\|+\|A_{2}-\mathrm{Id}\|+\|A_{1}-\mathrm{Id}\|\cdot\|A_{2}-\mathrm{Id}\|

(A.1)

for any
k\times k
matrices
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
. ∎

Theorem A.3

(Geometric transformation theorem)
.

Given constants
\eta>0
and
\epsilon>0
, there exist
C=C(n)
such that the following holds.

Let
\mathcal{X}=\{(M^{n},(g(t))_{t\in I}\}
be a closed Ricci flow with entropy bounded below by
-Y
and
x_{0}^{*}=(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathcal{X}
with
[t_{0}-10r^{2},t_{0}]\subset I
.
Suppose for any
s\in[r_{0},r]
,
x_{0}^{*}
is
(\delta,s)
-selfsimilar,
(k,\delta,s)
-splitting but not
(k+1,\eta,s)
-splitting. Assume that
\vec{u}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k}):M\times[t_{0}-10r^{2},t_{0}]\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
is a
(k,\delta,r)
-splitting map. If
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y,\eta,\epsilon)
, then for each
s\in[r_{0},r]
, there exists a lower triangular
k\times k
matrix
T_{s}
such that

(i)

\vec{u}^{s}=T_{s}\vec{u}
is a
(k,\epsilon,s)
-splitting map at
x_{0}^{*}
.

(ii)

For any
r_{0}\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq r
, we have

\displaystyle\max\left\{\rVert T_{s_{1}}^{-1}\circ T_{s_{2}}\rVert,\rVert T_{s_{2}}^{-1}\circ T_{s_{1}}\rVert\right\}\leq(1+C\epsilon)\left(\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}}\right)^{C\epsilon}.

(iii)

Each diagonal entry of
T_{s}
has absolute value bounded below by
1/2
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
t_{0}=0
and
\epsilon\leq\epsilon(n,Y,\eta)
is sufficiently small. We set
\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=\mathrm{d}\nu_{x_{0}^{*};t}=(4\pi\tau)^{-n/2}e^{-f}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}
, where
\tau=-t
. Throughout the proof, constants denoted by
C
depend only on
n
, and may vary from line to line.

Define
r^{\prime}
to be the smallest number so that for any
s\in[r^{\prime},r]
, there exists a lower triangular
k\times k
matrix
T_{s}
for which
\vec{u}^{s}:=T_{s}\vec{u}
is a
(k,\epsilon,s)
-splitting map at
x_{0}^{*}
.

By the definition of
r^{\prime}
and Lemma
A.2
, for any
r^{\prime}\leq s\leq s^{\prime}\leq\min\{2s,r\}
, we have

\displaystyle\rVert T^{-1}_{s}\circ T_{s^{\prime}}-\mathrm{Id}\rVert+\rVert T^{-1}_{s^{\prime}}\circ T_{s}-\mathrm{Id}\rVert\leq C\epsilon.

(A.2)

It then follows that for any
r^{\prime}\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq r
,

\displaystyle\max\left\{\rVert T^{-1}_{s_{1}}\circ T_{s_{2}}\rVert,\rVert T^{-1}_{s_{2}}\circ T_{s_{1}}\rVert\right\}\leq(1+C\epsilon)\left(\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}}\right)^{C\epsilon}.

(A.3)

Indeed, it follows from (
A.1
), (
A.2
) and an induction argument that for any
l\geq 1

\displaystyle\rVert T^{-1}_{2^{-l}s}\circ T_{s}-\mathrm{Id}\rVert\leq\left(1+C\epsilon\right)^{l}-1

as long as
[2^{-l}s,s]\subset[r^{\prime},r]
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\rVert T^{-1}_{s}\circ T_{2^{-l}s}-\mathrm{Id}\rVert\leq\left(1+C\epsilon\right)^{l}-1.

From the above two inequalities, (
A.3
) easily follows.

On the other hand, since
\square|\nabla u_{i}|^{2}\leq 0
, it follows from
[
FL25a
, Proposition 10.2]
that

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{-10s^{2}}^{-s^{2}/10}\int_{M}|\nabla u_{i}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq 1+2\delta.

(A.4)

Moreover, for any
s\in[r^{\prime},r]
and
1\leq i,j\leq k
, we have

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{-10s^{2}}^{-s^{2}/10}\int_{M}\langle\nabla u^{s}_{i},\nabla u^{s}_{j}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t=\delta_{ij}.

(A.5)

Combining (
A.4
) and (
A.5
), it follows from Lemma
A.1
(ii) that each diagonal entry of
T_{s}
has absolute value bounded below by
1/2
, provided
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y)
.

Therefore, to complete the proof, it remains to show that
r^{\prime}=r_{0}
. Suppose, for contradiction, that
r^{\prime}>r_{0}
.

Claim A.4
.

Let
0<\lambda_{1}(t)\leq\lambda_{2}(t)\leq\ldots
be the nonzero eigenvalues of
\Delta_{f}=\Delta-\langle\nabla\cdot,\nabla f\rangle
at time
t
, counted by multiplicities. Then, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert s^{2}\lambda_{i}(-s^{2})-\frac{1}{2}\right\rvert\leq\Psi(\delta)

(A.6)

for any
s\in[r_{0},r]
and
1\leq i\leq k
, and there exists
c_{0}=c_{0}(n,Y,\eta)>0
such that

\displaystyle s^{2}\lambda_{k+1}(-s^{2})\geq\frac{1+c_{0}}{2}.

(A.7)

First, by Theorem
2.6
, we have
\lambda_{i}(t)\geq 1/2
. Thus, (
A.6
) follows from
[
FL25a
, Proposition 10.7, Corollary C.5]
.

Suppose
s^{2}\lambda_{k+1}(-s^{2})\leq 1/2+\zeta
, then it follows from
[
FL25a
, Corollary C.4]
that we can find a
(k+1,C\zeta,s/\sqrt{10})
-splitting map at
x_{0}^{*}
, where
C
is a universal constant. Thus, by Proposition
3.11
(i), we conclude that
x_{0}^{*}
is
(k+1,\Psi(\delta+\zeta),s)
-splitting. This contradicts our assumption if
\zeta\leq\zeta(n,Y,\eta)
and
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y,\eta)
, which completes the proof of Claim
A.4
.

Next, we set
\vec{u}^{\prime}:=T_{r^{\prime}}\vec{u}=(u^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,u^{\prime}_{k})
.

Claim A.5
.

For each
s\in[r^{\prime},r]
and
1\leq i\leq k
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{M}(u_{i}^{\prime})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-s^{2}}\leq Cs^{2}\left(\frac{s}{r^{\prime}}\right)^{C\epsilon}.

For
\vec{u}^{s}=(u^{s}_{1},\ldots,u^{s}_{k})
, by
[
FL25a
, Proposition 10.2]
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{M}|\nabla u^{s}_{i}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq 1+2\epsilon.

(A.8)

for any
-10s^{2}\leq t\leq-s^{2}/10
. Thus, it follows from Theorem
2.6
that

\displaystyle\int_{M}(u_{i}^{s})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-s^{2}}\leq 2s^{2}\int_{M}|\nabla u^{s}_{i}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq 4s^{2}.

(A.9)

Thus, Claim
A.5
follows from (
A.3
), (
A.8
) and (
A.9
) directly.

Next, we set
\phi_{i}(t)
for
i\in\mathbb{N}
to be the orthonormal basis of eigenfunctions of
\Delta_{f}
corresponding to
\lambda_{i}(t)
. In the following, we assume
r^{\prime}=1
for simplicity and choose constants
\delta^{\prime}\ll L^{-1}\ll 1
, both of which are
\Psi(\delta)
and will be determined later.

Since
x_{0}^{*}
is
(\delta,s)
-selfsimilar and
(k,\delta,s)
-splitting for any
s\in[r_{0},r]
, it follows from Proposition
3.11
(i) and
[
Bam20b
, Proposition 12.21]
that we can find
\vec{y}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}):M\times[-L,0]\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
such that

(a)

\square y_{i}=0
;

(b)

y_{i}(x_{0}^{*})=0
;

(c)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{M}y_{i}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}-2\tau\right\rvert\leq\delta^{\prime}
for any
t\in[-L,-L^{-1}]
;

(d)

\displaystyle\int_{M}\left|\langle\nabla y_{i},\nabla y_{j}\rangle-\delta_{ij}\right|\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq\delta^{\prime}
for any
t\in[-L,-L^{-1}]
.

For simplicity, we set

\displaystyle y_{i}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\tau}}y_{i},\quad\text{and decompose}\quad y_{i}^{\prime}=\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}Y_{i,l}\phi_{l},

where the convergence is in the
L^{2}
-sense. Note that
Y_{i,l}
depends on
t
.

Claim A.6
.

For any
t\in[-L,-1]
and
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\sum_{l=1}^{k}Y_{i,l}Y_{j,l}-\delta_{ij}\right\rvert+\sum_{l=k+1}^{\infty}Y_{i,l}^{2}\leq C(n,Y,\eta)\delta^{\prime}.

(A.10)

By (c) and (d) above, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{M}y_{i}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}-2\tau\right|\leq\delta^{\prime}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\left|\int_{M}|\nabla y_{i}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}-1\right|\leq\delta^{\prime}.

(A.11)

Since
y_{i}^{\prime}=\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}Y_{i,l}\phi_{l}
, (
A.11
) implies

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}Y_{i,l}^{2}-1\right|\leq\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2\tau}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\left|\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{l}Y_{i,l}^{2}-\frac{1}{2\tau}\right|\leq\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2\tau}.

Combining these estimates with (
A.7
), it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1+\delta^{\prime}}{2\tau}\geq\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{l}Y_{i,l}^{2}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\tau}\sum_{l=1}^{k}Y_{i,l}^{2}+\frac{1+c_{0}}{2\tau}\sum_{l=k+1}^{\infty}Y_{i,l}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\tau}\left(1-\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2\tau}-\sum_{l=k+1}^{\infty}Y_{i,l}^{2}\right)+\frac{1+c_{0}}{2\tau}\sum_{l=k+1}^{\infty}Y_{i,l}^{2},

(A.12)

which implies

\displaystyle\sum_{l=k+1}^{\infty}Y_{i,l}^{2}\leq c_{0}^{-1}\delta^{\prime}(1+1/2\tau)\leq C(n,Y,\eta)\delta^{\prime}.

Moreover, it follows from (
A.12
) again that

\displaystyle 1+\delta^{\prime}\geq 1-\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2\tau}-\sum_{l=k+1}^{\infty}Y_{i,l}^{2}+2\tau\sum_{l=k+1}^{\infty}\lambda_{l}Y_{i,l}^{2},

which implies

\displaystyle 2\tau\sum_{l=k+1}^{\infty}\lambda_{l}Y_{i,l}^{2}\leq C(n,Y,\eta)\delta^{\prime}.

(A.13)

Next, it follows from (d) above that for
1\leq i\leq j\leq k
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert 2\tau\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{l}Y_{i,l}Y_{j,l}-\delta_{ij}\right\rvert\leq\delta^{\prime},

which, when combined with (
A.13
), yields

\displaystyle\left\lvert 2\tau\sum_{l=1}^{k}\lambda_{l}Y_{i,l}Y_{j,l}-\delta_{ij}\right\rvert\leq C(n,Y,\eta)\delta^{\prime}.

Since
\lambda_{l}
is close to
\frac{1}{2\tau}
by Claim
A.4
, it is easy to see that (
A.10
) holds. This completes the proof of Claim
A.6
.

For the rest of the proof, we consider
w:=u^{\prime}_{a}
for some
1\leq a\leq k
and write

\displaystyle w(t)=w_{0}(t)+\sum_{i=1}^{k}H_{i}(t)y_{i}(t)=w_{0}(t)+\sum_{i=1}^{k}H^{\prime}_{i}(t)y^{\prime}_{i}(t),

for any
t\in[-L,-1]
, where
H^{\prime}_{i}(t)=\sqrt{2\tau}H_{i}(t)
is chosen so that

\displaystyle\int_{M}w_{0}y_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=0,\quad\forall 1\leq i\leq k.

(A.14)

Notice that by Claim
A.6
, such choices are possible.

Claim A.7
.

For any
t\in[-L,-1]
and
1\leq i\leq k
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert H^{\prime}_{i}(t)-\int_{M}wy^{\prime}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\right\rvert\leq C(n,Y,\eta)\delta^{\prime}\left(\int_{M}w^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(A.15)

By our definition of
H_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{M}wy_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}H_{j}\int_{M}y_{i}y_{j}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=2\tau\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}H_{j}Y_{i,l}Y_{j,l}

for any
t\in[-L,-1]
and
1\leq i\leq k
. By Claim
A.6
, the proof of (
A.15
) is clear.

Next, we decompose

\displaystyle w(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}J_{i}(t)\phi_{i}(t),

where the sum is in the
L^{2}
-sense. Note that by Claim
A.5
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}J^{2}_{i}(t)=\int_{M}w^{2}(t)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq C\tau^{1+C\epsilon}\leq C\tau^{2}.

(A.16)

Claim A.8
.

For any
t\in[-L,-1]
,

\displaystyle\int_{M}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}J_{i}\phi_{i}-H_{i}y_{i}\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq C(n,Y,\eta)\tau^{2}\delta^{\prime}

(A.17)

and

\displaystyle\int_{M}\left\lvert\nabla\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}J_{i}\phi_{i}-H_{i}y_{i}\right)\right\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq C(n,Y,\eta)\tau\delta^{\prime}.

(A.18)

We compute

\displaystyle\int_{M}wy^{\prime}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\int_{M}wY_{i,l}\phi_{l}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}Y_{i,l}J_{l}.

By Claim
A.6
and (
A.16
),

\displaystyle\left\lvert\sum_{l=k+1}^{\infty}Y_{i,l}J_{l}\right\rvert\leq\left(\sum_{l=k+1}^{\infty}Y^{2}_{i,l}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{l=k+1}^{\infty}J^{2}_{l}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(n,Y,\eta)\tau(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{M}wy^{\prime}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}-\sum_{l=1}^{k}Y_{i,l}J_{l}\right\rvert\leq C(n,Y,\eta)\tau(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Combining this with Claim
A.7
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert H_{i}^{\prime}-\sum_{l=1}^{k}Y_{i,l}J_{l}\right\rvert\leq C(n,Y,\eta)\tau(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(A.19)

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}H^{\prime}_{i}y^{\prime}_{i}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(H^{\prime}_{i}-\sum_{l=1}^{k}Y_{i,l}J_{l}\right)y_{i}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{l=1}^{k}Y_{i,l}J_{l}y_{i}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(H^{\prime}_{i}-\sum_{l=1}^{k}Y_{i,l}J_{l}\right)y_{i}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}Y_{i,l}Y_{i,j}J_{l}\phi_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(H^{\prime}_{i}-\sum_{l=1}^{k}Y_{i,l}J_{l}\right)y_{i}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}Y_{i,l}Y_{i,j}J_{l}\phi_{j}++\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}Y_{i,l}Y_{i,j}J_{l}\phi_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(H^{\prime}_{i}-\sum_{l=1}^{k}Y_{i,l}J_{l}\right)y_{i}^{\prime}+\sum_{l=1}^{k}J_{l}\phi_{l}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}Y_{i,l}Y_{i,j}J_{l}\phi_{j}+\sum_{l=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}Y_{i,l}Y_{i,j}-\delta_{jl}\right)J_{l}\phi_{j}.

(A.20)

Combining Claim
A.6
, (
A.19
) and (
A.20
), (
A.17
) is easily derived. On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\int_{M}|\nabla\phi_{i}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=\lambda_{i}

and, by (
A.11
),

\displaystyle\left|\int_{M}|\nabla y^{\prime}_{i}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}-\frac{1}{2\tau}\right|\leq\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2\tau}.

Thus, it follows from (
A.13
), (
A.19
) and (
A.20
) that

\displaystyle\int_{M}\left\lvert\nabla\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}J_{i}\phi_{i}-H^{\prime}_{i}y^{\prime}_{i}\right)\right\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,Y,\eta)\tau\delta^{\prime}+C(n,Y,\eta)\tau^{2}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}Y_{i,j}^{2}\lambda_{j}+C(n,Y,\eta)\tau(\delta^{\prime})^{2}\leq C(n,Y,\eta)\tau\delta^{\prime}.

In sum, the proof of Claim
A.8
is complete.

Claim A.9
.

For any
t\in[-L,-1]
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\int_{M}J_{j}\phi_{j}H_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\right\rvert\leq C(n,Y,\eta)\tau^{2}(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}

(A.21)

and for any
\sigma>0
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}\int_{M}J_{j}\phi_{j}H_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\right\rvert\leq\sigma\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}J_{j}^{2}+\frac{C(n,Y,\eta)\tau\delta^{\prime}}{\sigma}.

(A.22)

From (
A.10
), we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\int_{M}J_{j}\phi_{j}H_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\right\rvert=

\displaystyle\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}J_{j}H_{i}^{\prime}Y_{i,j}\right\rvert\leq C(n,Y,\eta)\tau\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}|J_{j}||Y_{i,j}|\leq C(n,Y,\eta)\tau^{2}(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}},

where we used the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality for the last line. Similarly, we have by (
A.13
),

\displaystyle\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}\int_{M}J_{j}\phi_{j}H_{i}^{\prime}y_{i}^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\right\rvert=\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}Y_{i,j}J_{j}H_{i}^{\prime}\right\rvert\leq\sigma\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}J_{j}^{2}+\frac{C(n,Y,\eta)\tau\delta^{\prime}}{\sigma}.

In sum, the proof of Claim
A.9
is complete.

Next, we consider the frequency function of
w_{0}
:

\displaystyle F_{w_{0}}(t):=\frac{\tau\int_{M}|\nabla w_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}}{\int_{M}w_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}}.

Recall that

\displaystyle w_{0}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}(J_{i}\phi_{i}-H_{i}y_{i})+\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}J_{i}\phi_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}(J_{i}\phi_{i}-H^{\prime}_{i}y^{\prime}_{i})+\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}J_{i}\phi_{i}.

We compute

\displaystyle\int_{M}w_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}J_{i}^{2}+\int_{M}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}J_{i}\phi_{i}-H^{\prime}_{i}y^{\prime}_{i}\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}+2\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\int_{M}J_{j}\phi_{j}(J_{i}\phi_{i}-H^{\prime}_{i}y^{\prime}_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}J_{i}^{2}+\int_{M}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}J_{i}\phi_{i}-H^{\prime}_{i}y^{\prime}_{i}\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}-2\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\int_{M}J_{j}\phi_{j}H^{\prime}_{i}y^{\prime}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}J_{i}^{2}+C(n,Y,\eta)\tau^{2}(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}},

(A.23)

where we used (
A.17
) and (
A.21
) for the last inequality.

Moreover, we calculate

\displaystyle\int_{M}|\nabla w_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}\lambda_{i}J_{i}^{2}+\int_{M}\left|\nabla\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}J_{i}\phi_{i}-H^{\prime}_{i}y^{\prime}_{i}\right)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}-2\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\int_{M}J_{j}\Delta_{f}\phi_{j}(J_{i}\phi_{i}-H^{\prime}_{i}y^{\prime}_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}\lambda_{i}J_{i}^{2}+\int_{M}\left|\nabla\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}J_{i}\phi_{i}-H^{\prime}_{i}y^{\prime}_{i}\right)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}-2\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\int_{M}\lambda_{j}J_{j}\phi_{j}H^{\prime}_{i}y^{\prime}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(1-\sigma)\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}\lambda_{i}J_{i}^{2}-C(n,Y,\eta)\tau\delta^{\prime}\sigma^{-1},

(A.24)

where we used (
A.22
) for the last inequality.

Combining (
A.23
) and (
A.24
), we obtain

\displaystyle F_{w_{0}}(t)\geq

\displaystyle\dfrac{(1-\sigma)\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}\lambda_{i}\tau J_{i}^{2}-C(n,Y,\eta)\tau^{2}\delta^{\prime}\sigma^{-1}}{\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}J_{i}^{2}+C(n,Y,\eta)\tau^{2}(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\dfrac{\frac{(1-\sigma)(1+c_{0})}{2}\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}J_{i}^{2}-C(n,Y,\eta)\tau^{2}\delta^{\prime}\sigma^{-1}}{\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}J_{i}^{2}+C(n,Y,\eta)\tau^{2}(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(A.25)

where we used (
A.7
) for the last inequality. Now, we fix the constant
\sigma=\sigma(n,Y,\eta)
so that

\displaystyle\frac{(1-\sigma)(1+c_{0})}{2}=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{3}.

Moreover, we choose
L=(\delta^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{10}}
. Thus, (
A.25
) becomes

\displaystyle F_{w_{0}}(t)\geq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{3}\right)\frac{\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}J_{i}^{2}-C(n,Y,\eta)(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{4}{5}}}{\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}J_{i}^{2}+C(n,Y,\eta)\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{3}{10}}}.

(A.26)

From (
A.26
), the next claim is immediate.

Claim A.10
.

For any
t\in[-(\delta^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{10}},-1]
, if
\delta^{\prime}\leq\delta^{\prime}(n,Y,\eta)
and
\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}J_{i}^{2}\geq(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{5}}
, then

\displaystyle F_{w_{0}}(t)\geq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}.

Since
\square w=0
and
\square\vec{y}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\square w_{0}=-\square\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}H_{i}y_{i}\right)=-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}H_{i}\right)y_{i},

which implies

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{M}w_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=

\displaystyle\int_{M}2w_{0}\square w_{0}-2|\nabla w_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{M}2-w_{0}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}H_{i}\right)y_{i}\right)-2|\nabla w_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=-2\int_{M}|\nabla w_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t},

(A.27)

where in third equality, we used (
A.14
).

Next, we consider the following two cases.

Case 1
:
\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}J_{i}^{2}\geq(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{5}}
for all
t\in[-(\delta^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{10}},-21]
.

In this case, we conclude from Claim
A.10
that

\displaystyle F(t)\geq\frac{1+c_{1}}{2},

(A.28)

where
c_{1}=c_{0}/2>0
. Set
I(t)=\int_{M}w_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}
. Then by (
A.27
) and (
A.28
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\log I(t)=\frac{-2\int_{M}|\nabla w_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}}{\int_{M}w_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}}=-\frac{2F_{w_{0}(t)}}{\tau}\leq\frac{1+c_{1}}{t}.

By integration, we have for all
t\in[-(\delta^{\prime})^{-1/10},-21]
and
s\in[-21,-20]
,

\displaystyle(21)^{1+c_{1}}I(t)\geq I(s)(-t)^{1+c_{1}}.

(A.29)

On the other hand, by Claim
A.5
, we have

\displaystyle I(t)\leq\int_{M}w^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq C(-t)^{1+C\epsilon}.

(A.30)

Therefore, by choosing
t=-(\delta^{\prime})^{-1/10}
in (
A.29
) and (
A.30
), we obtain that for any
s\in[-21,-20]
,

\displaystyle I(s)\leq C(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{c_{1}-C\epsilon}{10}}.

By (
A.27
), we can find
t_{1}\in[-21,-20]
such that

\displaystyle\int_{M}|\nabla w_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{1}}\leq C(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{c_{1}-C\epsilon}{10}}\leq C(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{c_{1}}{20}},

(A.31)

since
\epsilon\leq\epsilon(n,Y,\eta)
. Thus, we compute

\displaystyle F_{w}(t_{1})=\dfrac{|t_{1}|\int_{M}|\nabla w|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{1}}}{\int_{M}w^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{1}}}\leq\dfrac{|t_{1}|\int_{M}\left\lvert\nabla\left(w_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}H_{i}y_{i}\right)\right\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{1}}}{\int_{M}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}H_{i}y_{i}\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{1}}}.

Combining (
A.17
), (
A.18
) and (
A.31
), this implies

\displaystyle F_{w}(t_{1})\leq\dfrac{|t_{1}|\int_{M}\left\lvert\nabla\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}J_{i}\phi_{i}\right)\right\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{1}}}{\int_{M}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}J_{i}\phi_{i}\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{1}}}+\Psi(\delta^{\prime})\leq\frac{1}{2}+\Psi(\delta^{\prime}),

(A.32)

where we used (
A.6
) for the last inequality since
\delta\ll\delta^{\prime}
.

Then, one can use the same argument as in
[
FL25a
, Proposition C.3]
to conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{-20}^{-1/100}\int_{M}|\nabla^{2}w|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq\Psi(\delta^{\prime}).

(A.33)

Case 2
: There exists
t_{2}\in[-(\delta^{\prime})^{-1/10},-21]
such that
\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}J_{i}^{2}<(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{5}}
at
t_{2}
.

In this case, we have

\displaystyle\int_{M}w_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{2}}=

\displaystyle\int_{M}w^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{2}}-\int_{M}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}H_{i}y_{i}\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}J_{i}^{2}(t_{2})-\int_{M}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}H_{i}y_{i}\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{2}}+\sum_{i=k+1}^{\infty}J_{i}^{2}(t_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{M}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}J_{i}\phi_{i}-H_{i}y_{i}\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{M}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}J_{i}\phi_{i}+H_{i}y_{i}\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{5}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,Y,\eta)(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{3}{10}}+(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{5}}\leq(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{6}},

where we used (
A.16
) and (
A.17
).

By (
A.27
), for any
t\in[t_{2},-20]
,

\displaystyle\int_{M}w_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{6}}.

Moreover, using (
A.27
) again, we can find
t_{3}\in[-21,-20]
such that

\displaystyle\int_{M}|\nabla w_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{3}}\leq(\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{6}}.

(A.34)

Similar to (
A.32
) and (
A.33
), we obtain from (
A.34
) that

\displaystyle F_{w}(t_{3})=\dfrac{|t_{3}|\int_{M}|\nabla w|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{3}}}{\int_{M}w^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{3}}}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\Psi(\delta^{\prime}).

and

\displaystyle\int_{-20}^{-1/100}\int_{M}|\nabla^{2}w|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq\Psi(\delta^{\prime}).

(A.35)

Since
w=u^{\prime}_{a}
, combining (
A.33
) and (
A.35
), we conclude that for any
s\in[-1,-1/2]
, there exists a lower triangular
k\times k
matrix
T_{s}
such that
T_{s}\vec{u}^{\prime}
is a
(k,\epsilon,s)
-splitting map at
x_{0}^{*}
, provided that
\delta^{\prime}\leq\delta^{\prime}(n,Y,\eta)
. This contradicts our assumption, thereby completing the proof.
∎

Since Theorem
A.3
(ii) gives the boundedness of
T_{s}
for any fixed scale
s
, we can obtain the same result for Ricci flow limit spaces by taking the limit.

Theorem A.11
.

Given constants
\eta>0
and
\epsilon>0
, there exist
C=C(n)
such that the following holds.

Let
(Z,d_{Z},\mathfrak{t})
be a noncollapsed Ricci flow limit space obtained as the limit of a sequence of closed Ricci flows in
\mathcal{M}(n,Y,T)
. Suppose
z\in Z
and for any
s\in[r_{0},r]
,
z
is
(\delta,s)
-selfsimilar,
(k,\delta,s)
-splitting but not
(k+1,\eta,s)
-splitting. Assume that
\vec{u}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{k}):Z_{(\mathfrak{t}(z)-10r^{2},\mathfrak{t}(z)]}\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
is a
(k,\delta,r)
-splitting map. If
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y,\eta,\epsilon)
, then for each
s\in[r_{0},r]
, there exists a lower triangular
k\times k
matrix
T_{s}
such that

(i)

\vec{u}^{s}=T_{s}\vec{u}
is a
(k,\epsilon,s)
-splitting map at
z
.

(ii)

For any
r_{0}\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq r
, we have

\displaystyle\max\left\{\rVert T_{s_{1}}^{-1}\circ T_{s_{2}}\rVert,\rVert T_{s_{2}}^{-1}\circ T_{s_{1}}\rVert\right\}\leq(1+C\epsilon)\left(\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}}\right)^{C\epsilon}.

(iii)

Each diagonal entry of
T_{s}
has absolute value bounded below by
1/2
.
